# Spec: Bundle isolation

- Ticket: none.
- Status: version 7 (2026-11-17), `accepted`. The owner approved version 6 on 2026-11-17, and the approval stands for the design. Version 7 brings the text in line with `main` at `ebbf8f94`. It asked three questions about code that commit added, and the owner's decision 31 answered each as proposed ([Questions](#questions)). See [Revisions](#revisions).
- Requirements: U1–U31, the owner's decisions (jao.scheuermann, 2026-11-17 and, for U29–U31, 2026-11-18). U1–U21 are recorded as OD1–OD26 in the concept [docs/features/bundle-isolation.md](../features/bundle-isolation.md#decisions). U22 and U23 are the owner's decision 27, U24–U26 the owner's decision 29 and U27 and U28 the owner's decision 30, all three recorded in the concept's [Evolution](../features/bundle-isolation.md#evolution). U29–U31 are the owner's decision 31, which answered Q6 to Q8 and is recorded in this spec ([Questions](#questions)). Decision 28 puts the Git token gap outside this spec ([Non-goals](#non-goals)). All are binding and are not reopened here; where this spec finds a problem in one, it keeps the decision and asks; the owner has answered every question ([Questions](#questions)).
- Builds on: commit `ebbf8f94` (the tip of `main` on 2026-11-17, which the branch `feat/bundle-isolation` starts from). Versions 1 to 6 were written against `a868b5d0`; the one change between the two commits is the thread archive of pull request #59 ([docs/designs/thread-archive.md](thread-archive.md)), and version 7 re-read every reference at the new commit ([Revisions](#revisions)). Every line reference names the file as it is at that commit. Every path is written from the repository root, with two abbreviations: `L/` is `bundles/desktop/layout/` and `C/` is `bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/`. A bare `:line` refers to the last full path named in the same sentence or table cell, and a path that starts with `src/`, `tests/` or `stories/` is under the bundle directory named before it in the same sentence or cell.
- Bundles touched: 24 of the 31 existing bundles change and two are created, `workspace` and `account`. Final versions are in [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions). `comments`, `history`, `host-config`, `web`, `screen-relay`, `docker` and `firecracker` do not change.
- Feature docs: [docs/features/bundle-isolation.md](../features/bundle-isolation.md), the accepted concept of this change. Phase 12 creates or rewrites the feature docs of every feature; its design is the companion [docs/designs/bundle-isolation-docs.md](bundle-isolation-docs.md) ("the documentation plan"), which this spec cites and does not repeat. The other feature docs this change touches are listed in [Docs to update](#docs-to-update).
- Identifiers: U1–U31 requirements; G1–G18 goals, numbered as in the concept; M1–M11 the rules of the concept's model; D1–D33 design decisions of this spec; AC acceptance criteria and T tests, numbered by phase (`AC4.2`, `T5.3`); R1–R20 risks; Q1–Q8 the questions of this spec, all answered ([Questions](#questions)). OD, X and A are the concept's decisions, conflicts and assumptions.

## Terms

Background reading: the concept's [How to read this document](../features/bundle-isolation.md#how-to-read-this-document), which defines the labels OD, M, G, X and A, "Spec 1" and "Spec 2", and "stage N", a step of the migration that moved the application into bundles (`docs/architecture/bundles/migration.md`); the concept's [Background](../features/bundle-isolation.md#background), which defines bundle, part, contract, quarantine, the platform and the core, scope keys, slots, the composition and staging; [docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md](../architecture/bundles/versioning.md) for the version rules; [docs/architecture/bundles/manifest.md](../architecture/bundles/manifest.md) for the manifest.

Terms of the concept this spec uses unchanged:

| Term                    | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Extension point         | A contract or slot through which other bundles add something to a bundle.                                                                                                                  |
| Shell                   | The `layout` bundle after this change: the window's frame and its slots.                                                                                                                   |
| Records mirror          | The window's live copy of the runtime's Project and Thread records.                                                                                                                        |
| Execution picker        | The control in the conversation footer that shows and sets the selected Thread's execution.                                                                                                |
| Structural copy         | A bundle's local retyping of part of another bundle's contract, bound to it by the contract id string instead of an import.                                                                |
| `A → B`                 | Bundle A knows bundle B: its code, manifest, package dependencies or styles name B's id, a contract of B, or an element tag, tool name, kind id, route, event type or CSS selector B owns. |
| Reader, operator, owner | The reader uses the desktop app. The operator runs the host and its database. The owner is the product owner who made the decisions; the concept calls this person "the user".             |

Terms this spec adds:

| Term                 | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Page form            | The form of the frame that shows a page in place of the conversation: sidebar, title bar and one body. Today the dashboard is drawn this way (`L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:207-254`).                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Conversation form    | The other form of the frame: sidebar, title bar, conversation column with its viewer tabs, and side panel (`L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:256-377`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Owner key            | The opaque string that names one set of viewer tabs. Today the layout derives it from the route: the Thread id, or `project:<id>` (`L/src/client/domain/route-selection.ts:126-133`).                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Data item            | A slot item whose owner draws the control and whose filler supplies only data in `props`: a label, a command id and the command's input. The item's `tag` names the filler's icon element.                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Point                | Short for extension point.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Many-contract        | A service contract defined with `{ cardinality: 'many' }` (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/define.ts:262-303`): several bundles provide it, and a consumer reads every provider through `whenAvailable`, whose callback also names the provider (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/context.ts:13-30`).                                                                           |
| Violation            | A finding of a boundary test that the violations file tolerates, until a named phase or for good.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Violations file      | `tests/boundary-exceptions.json`, which the concept calls the exceptions file. It lists the violations, in the format [Boundary tests and the violations file](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file) defines.                                                                                                                                                           |
| Kept decision        | Something OD15 or OD17–OD19 keep although it breaks a rule of the model, and which no boundary test finds. The concept calls these recorded exceptions. They are listed in [Non-goals](#non-goals), and [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file) says where they are written down and checked. One recorded decision is not kept: the owner reopened it (U25). |
| Versioning deviation | One of the two contracts that keep their id and take a minor version where the repository's versioning table asks for a major: `core.open-settings/v1` and `runtime.lease/v1`. They are stated once, in [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions).                                                                                                                   |
| Gate variable        | An environment variable without which a bundle test skips itself, such as `DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Phase, step          | A phase is one numbered unit of [Phases](#phases), with its own acceptance criteria. A step is a lettered part of a phase, such as 4(a), that merges on its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Merged change        | One pull request merged to `main`. A phase or a step is one merged change, or several where [Phases](#phases) says so.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Release notes        | The section "Release notes" of a merged change's pull request, repeated as that change's dated entry in the Evolution of the feature doc ([Phases](#phases)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Phase report         | The section "Phase report" of the pull request that merges the last change of a phase or step ([Acceptance criteria](#acceptance-criteria)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Described, followed  | States of a Project in the records mirror ([section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Thread archive       | The feature `main` gained with pull request #59, after version 6 of this spec: a Thread can be archived, restored and pinned, and the runtime archives Threads by rules of its own ([docs/features/thread-archive.md](../features/thread-archive.md)).                                                                                                                       |
| View of a Thread     | A window's statement to the host that it shows a Thread. The runtime's archiver leaves a Thread alone while a window holds a view of it. A bundle holds one through `view` of `runtime.records/v1` ([section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime)). It is neither a view of the side panel nor the `view` of `settings.models/v1`.                                              |

Names from the code that the design uses:

| Name                                                        | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `CallArg`, `Disposable`, `ref`, `defineHooks`, `defineHook` | Exports of `bundle/contracts`, which every contracts module imports: the optional last argument of a contract method (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/call.ts:60`); the handle that undoes a registration or a subscription (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/context.ts:6`); the typed reference to a contract id (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/define.ts:523`); and the two functions that declare a hooks contract (`:104`, `:169`). The code blocks of this spec leave these imports out. |
| `<core-outlet>`                                             | The element in which the core draws the element of the current route: `<${core}-outlet>`, with the core's id (`packages/desktop/src/client/bootstrap.ts:13-14,94`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Stack panes                                                 | The panes other bundles add below the Thread list of the sidebar through `layout.stack/v2` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:356-390`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Viewer card                                                 | The card that frames the element of the selected viewer tab (`L/src/client/components/templates/inset-card.ts:10-11`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Kind descriptor                                             | `ModelKindDescriptor`: what a provider kind declares of itself, its id, label, description, fields and lists (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/models.ts:29-45`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Tool host                                                   | The object the runtime's runner builds for each prompt and hands to the executor (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/tool-host/host.ts:5-16`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Start effort                                                | The effort a model gets when it is picked: `modelStartEffort` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/execution.ts:174-185`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `TreeScope`                                                 | `{ projectId, cwd? }`, the tree of one Project rooted at a working directory (`bundles/features/files/src/contracts/index.ts:349-354`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Caret                                                       | The transcript's keyboard position, which rests on one block at a time (`C/domain/caret-navigation.ts`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

## Problem

The concept's [Problem](../features/bundle-isolation.md#problem) states it with evidence; this section repeats only what the design answers.

- The repository checks one boundary rule, the import of another package's `src/` (`tests/lint.test.mjs:224-245`), and CI runs no bundle test (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:48-70`).
- The layout holds the frame, the Projects and Threads navigation and the account menu in one bundle of 96 source files and 10,742 lines, requires `runtime/v1` and `runtime.threads/v1` (`L/manifest.json:23-24`), and hands the runtime's records to ten bundles through `layout.workspace/v1` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:13,169-197`).
- The layout, the chat, the settings window, the runtime, the default agent, `files` and `git`, the core, the kernel and `postgres` each name something that belongs to another bundle. The list, with its 20 undeclared contract uses, is in the concept's [What breaks which rule today](../features/bundle-isolation.md#what-breaks-which-rule-today).
- The set of bundles is written by hand in the build files of the host (`app/backend/doric-host/project.json:23`, `app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:50-97`).
- The documentation describes the state before this change, and `GROUNDING.md` is the single description of the system (the documentation plan's [Problem](bundle-isolation-docs.md#problem)).

## Goals

G1–G14 are the concept's [goals](../features/bundle-isolation.md#goals-and-success-measures), unchanged; G15–G18 are the documentation goals of the [documentation plan](bundle-isolation-docs.md#goals). Each goal is measured by the acceptance criteria named beside it.

| Goal    | In one line                                                                                                                                  | Measured by                                                                                                 |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1      | The layout requires no `runtime*` contract and names no other bundle; it keeps the quarantined-bundle notice (OD18).                         | AC4.3–AC4.9                                                                                                 |
| G2      | The navigation works from `workspace` as it does today.                                                                                      | AC4.1, AC4.2, AC4.10–AC4.12                                                                                 |
| G3      | A tool drawing, a prompt block, a provider sign-in, a skill source and a file mark are added without changing the bundle that receives them. | AC5.1, AC6.1, AC8.1, AC9.1                                                                                  |
| G4      | The runtime imports no `terminal/contracts`, provides no `runtime.terminals/v1`, and its lease has none of the nine reads.                   | AC7.3–AC7.7                                                                                                 |
| G5      | The three assertions of M6 pass; what the core still names is recorded.                                                                      | AC2.1–AC2.3, AC2.5                                                                                          |
| G6      | No contract of `postgres` returns a client; its schema has no legacy model.                                                                  | AC10.1–AC10.4                                                                                               |
| G7      | No build file of the host names a bundle.                                                                                                    | AC11.1–AC11.4                                                                                               |
| G8      | CI fails on a new boundary violation and runs every bundle's tests.                                                                          | AC0.1–AC0.4, AC1.1–AC1.3                                                                                    |
| G9      | The reader sees no change beyond the changes [Non-goals](#non-goals) lists as accepted.                                                      | AC4.2, AC5.4, AC13.2                                                                                        |
| G10     | No contract method Spec 1 adds returns a live object or takes a function other than a subscription listener.                                 | AC13.3                                                                                                      |
| G11     | The contracts modules of `layout` and `workspace` import nothing from `runtime/contracts`.                                                   | AC4.8                                                                                                       |
| G12     | `default` imports no `mcp/contracts`; `files` names no `git.*` contract; the cycle test passes with no cycle entry.                          | AC8.2, AC9.2, AC13.1                                                                                        |
| G13     | The settings manifest states one function; `settings` draws no picker and maps no kind to a link.                                            | AC6.2–AC6.6                                                                                                 |
| G14     | The kernel imports no bundle; a host test proves its buffer holds the largest attachment.                                                    | AC2.4                                                                                                       |
| G15–G18 | The documentation goals.                                                                                                                     | The [acceptance of the documentation phase](bundle-isolation-docs.md#acceptance-of-the-documentation-phase) |

## Non-goals

- **Everything of Spec 2:** a process per bundle, gRPC, contracts made only of data, the database operations contract (OD6, OD7, OD14).
- **What OD15 keeps:** the comments UI in the chat; `sandbox/v1` and `executor/v1` declared in `runtime/contracts`; the papercraft special case in the Electron main process (`app/desktop/doric/src/platform/app.ts:322-325`); the per-feature REST and Socket.IO routes of `transport`.
- **The kept decisions of OD17–OD19:** the command id `core.open-settings/v1`, the window keys `'settings'` and `'workspace'`, the two sets of window sizes and the key `doric.selection` in the core; the `projectId` of `tools/v1`; the repository hint probe in the runtime; the table of REST errors in `runtime/contracts`; the quarantined-bundle notice in the shell. Where the kept decisions of this bullet and of the one before are written down and checked is in [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file).
- **The Git token in other bundles' redaction.** The runtime registers the Git token it applies to a sandbox only with its own redaction list (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/domain/service.ts:573`, `src/host/domain/redactor.ts:19-49`) and never through `secrets/v1`, so the terminal's stream does not redact it (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:949`). The gap exists today. No phase of this spec causes or widens it ([section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs)), and no phase closes it: by the owner's decision 28 it is tracked outside this spec as the Linear issue [DORIC-94](https://linear.app/doric/issue/DORIC-94).
- **The fix of a bundle test that phase 1 skips.** Phase 1 runs every bundle's tests in CI for the first time. A test that fails there because the product is wrong, or that passes and fails on one commit, is skipped, and each skip gets its own Linear issue in the team DORIC, as DORIC-94 does for the Git token ([section 2](#2-ci)). No phase of this spec delivers those fixes (U28).
- **Reshaping a contract that only moves.** `ThreadRows` keeps its functions (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:292-310`), `TabMenuItem` keeps `icon` as a React element and `run` as a function (`:40-55`), and `settings.config/v1` keeps `setDraft(update)` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/services/settings-config.ts:23-33`). They are Spec 2's.
- **A version for the REST and Socket.IO API,** and any change to a route or to a Socket.IO payload. One REST payload gains one optional field ([The REST and Socket.IO API](#13-the-rest-and-socketio-api)).
- **A second shell.** The shell stays replaceable; nothing builds or tests a replacement.
- **A deprecation window.** A removed contract id or slot name is not provided in parallel after its phase ends (concept, A1).
- **Any change the reader can see,** apart from these three, each accepted in the concept's G9:
  - a loaded MCP skill is labelled "mcp · server" where it reads "MCP · server" (OD23);
  - a skill source that takes longer than five seconds to list its skills is left out of that prompt;
  - and a database whose legacy tables hold rows is refused even when the migration marker exists.

## Requirements

One per decision of the owner that constrains the design. The full text of OD1–OD26 is in the concept's [Decisions](../features/bundle-isolation.md#decisions); decision 27 is stated in full in U22 and U23, decision 29 in U24–U26, decision 30 in U27 and U28 and decision 31 in U29–U31.

| #   | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | From                                             |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| U1  | Each bundle announces its own capabilities through extension points; the platform declares the base capabilities and the core provides them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | OD1, OD12                                        |
| U2  | Whoever is extended does not know who extends it, between any two bundles.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | OD2                                              |
| U3  | The layout is only the frame and its slots, a feature bundle that stays replaceable. The navigation becomes the bundle `workspace`, which fills the shell's sidebar slot and owns the selection, the dashboard and the rows under each Thread.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | OD3, OD17                                        |
| U4  | The runtime is a feature bundle on the core, and the core does not know Project or Thread: the three scope keys leave the core's manifest and the `/settings` route leaves its code.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | OD4, OD13                                        |
| U5  | A persistence bundle hands out no live client. `postgres.database/v1` and, where possible, the `postgres/prisma` export are removed, and a migration drops the legacy tables.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | OD5, OD14                                        |
| U6  | Spec 1 does not depend on Spec 2 and does not make it harder: a method Spec 1 adds takes and returns plain data.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | OD6, OD7                                         |
| U7  | Spec 1 covers the boundary checks with CI and hygiene, the chat's drawings, the settings provider page, the runtime's hand-backs and the derived bundle lists.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | OD11                                             |
| U8  | No other recorded decision is reopened. The owner reopened one: the reason the image's `.Dockerfile` records for its direct `tsc` builds (U25).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | OD15; the owner's decision 29                    |
| U9  | Each phase merges to `main` when it passes, one at a time. Of what the core's desktop part names, only the route leaves. Amended by decision 34: see [Revisions](#revisions).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | OD17                                             |
| U10 | Slots stay string names exported from the owner's contracts module; `regions/v1` does not change. The nine existing slots take the names of OD18, and the six added slots the names of OD23; U22 adds `shell.sidebar.footer`. The notice stays in the shell; `tools/v1` keeps `projectId`; the repository hint stays in the runtime; `resident` stays in the composition.                                                                                                                         | OD18, OD23                                       |
| U11 | `default` declares `default.skill-sources/v1` and `mcp` supplies its source. `files` declares `files.decorations/v1`, and `git` supplies its marks and declares the `files.*` contracts it uses. The picker moves to the chat, and `settings` publishes the configured models. The kind descriptor gains `site`. The REST error table stays. The kernel sizes its buffer from its own constant.                                                                                                   | OD19                                             |
| U12 | The runtime owns the records mirror in a client part, and `workspace` publishes ids only. The account status, menu and About go to the bundle `account`. The terminal's result block leaves the chat through a point keyed by input source, and `default` gains a client part for the `load_skill` drawing. The moved contracts get a transition inside phase 4. The two localStorage keys keep their names. The proof of stage 7 is kept, rewritten with SQL. `enabled` stays hand-written.      | OD20                                             |
| U13 | Spec 1 rewrites the whole architecture documentation, documents every implemented feature and retires `GROUNDING.md`, as phase 12.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | OD21, OD22, OD24                                 |
| U14 | The bundle that draws the frame keeps the id `layout`, and its contracts keep the names `layout.tabs`, `layout.panel` and `layout.stack`. `layout.stack/v2` keeps its id. `layout.tabs` and `layout.panel` take their next major by U27, which changes that part of OD23.                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | OD23; the owner's decision 30                    |

| U29 | The four calls the thread archive added to the layout are members of `runtime.records/v1`: `archiveThread`, `restoreThread` and `setThreadPinned` as writes, and `view`, which holds the stream of `viewThread` (D33).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | The owner's decision 31                          |
| U30 | The layout tells the host which Threads the window shows until step 4(c). From step 4(c) on, the chat does (D33).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | The owner's decision 31                          |
| U31 | The sidebar's Chat and Archived tabs are in `workspace`, inside `workspace-sidebar`, with the Thread tree. They get no slot and no shell contract (D33).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | The owner's decision 31                          |

## Design decisions

The decisions this spec makes where the concept left the choice to it. Each is designed in the section named in its last column.

One order fixes the version numbers and keeps two phases from editing `chat` or `terminal` at the same time. Phase 11 runs before the two new bundles exist, and step 6(c) before step 4(d) reaches `settings`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | [Phases](#phases)                                                                                                                           |
| D2  | A change of meaning takes a major, which is a new id: `layout.tabs/v2`, `layout.panel/v3`, `chat.hooks/v2` and `runtime/v2` (U27). The two contracts the owner kept under their id, `core.open-settings/v1` (U21) and `runtime.lease/v1` (U23), are the two versioning deviations. Both sets are stated once, with their release notes and the text phase 12 writes into `versioning.md`.                                                                        | U21, U23, U27.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions)                                                                                           |

**Where this spec departs from the concept.** The concept was accepted before these facts of the code were read. Where the two differ, this spec holds, and the concept's Evolution names each point:

- The history buttons go to `workspace`; the concept left them in the shell ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)).
- The grouping of models, the effort rules and the icon lookup stay in `settings`, behind `settings.models/v1`, which has `view(stored)`, `subscribe` and `reload`; the concept moved the grouping to the chat over a contract of one read and one subscription (D13).
- The nine lease reads are deleted; the concept moved six of them into `files` ([section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs)).
- The runtime uses `terminal/v1` in a fourth place, the snapshot, besides the three imports the concept counted (D18).
- 13 test files and 9 story files name a slot; the concept counted six test files ([section 8](#8-slots)).
- Phase 0 adds 22 manifest lines; the concept counted twenty uses, all written as literals. The cycle test takes a reviewed list of edges out of the graph, which has five at phase 0; the concept says only that the test fails on a cycle ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)).
- `runtime/testing` exports a fake of the mirror for tests and stories (D31), and the sheets of seven bundles gain a line for each of the two new bundles, a recorded decision the concept does not list ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)).
- The repository has a tenth slot today, `settings.sections`, which the concept's count of nine existing slots left out. It keeps its name and gains its constant ([section 8](#8-slots)).
- A filler checks a slot's name against the owner's constant through a type-only import and never imports the constant as a value; the concept says only that the owner exports it ([section 8](#8-slots)).
- The import test also allows `<id>/package.json` in a test file, for the three harnesses that load a built bundle (D22); the concept's scan allows `<id>/contracts` alone.
- 24 existing bundles change, and the platform version rises four times, to 1.11.0 (D26).
- Four contracts take a new id (U27, D30), and phase 7 has steps for the one of the runtime; the concept kept every id that stays provided. The old ids end inside their step, so the concept's "no deprecation window" (A1) stands.
- A bundle test that phase 1 skips is tracked in its own Linear issue and fixed outside Spec 1 (U28); the concept does not say what happens to a bundle test that fails on the CI runner (A5).

## Design

The sections follow the bundles, not the phases. [Phases](#phases) says which phase builds what, and [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions) lists every id and version in one table. Contract types are written as TypeScript; each has a zod schema beside it in the same contracts module, as the existing contracts have, and the type is the schema's output.

### 1. Boundary tests and the violations file

**The test files (D23).** The concept makes the import check an extension of the existing test, `tests/lint.test.mjs:224-245`, which scans the imports of every workspace package for a `src/` path. Phase 0 does that with three files and no new script:

- `tests/support/workspace.mjs`, new. It exports `root`, `workspacePackageNames`, `sourceFiles` and `importedModules`, moved from `tests/lint.test.mjs:15,156-222`, which exports nothing today, with two changes:
  - `sourceFiles(directory)` takes its files from one run of `git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard`, where it walks the disk today (`tests/lint.test.mjs:189-206`). It keeps a listed path that ends in one of the extensions of `tests/lint.test.mjs:200` and has no directory segment named `node_modules` or starting with `dist`. The file's own name is not tested. Today's walk tests every entry's name, files included (`tests/lint.test.mjs:193`), and so skips five source files named `distribution.ts` or `distribution.test.ts`: `app/desktop/doric/src/platform/catalog/distribution.ts`, `app/desktop/doric/tests/support/distribution.ts`, `packages/bundle/src/loader/distribution.ts`, `packages/bundle/src/runtime/distribution.ts` and `packages/bundle/tests/distribution.test.ts`. Phase 0 scans them. None gives a finding at this commit: they import `bundle/contracts`, `bundle/runtime` and `desktop/protocol` and relative paths, no `<package>/src/…` path and no bundle's package. One tracked file is under a `dist*` directory, `packages/sandpool/dist-e2e/tests/sandpool.e2e.js`, and stays outside the scan, as today. The run is at `repository`, a second parameter that defaults to `root`, so that T0.1 can call the function on a repository it creates in a temporary directory. A listed file that is not on disk is skipped. The files Git ignores are therefore not read, and a built tree gives the result of a fresh checkout: the generated Prisma client is ignored (`bundles/foundation/postgres/src/generated/.gitignore:2`) and exists on a developer's disk but not in CI, which runs `lint:test` before any build (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:47-48`).
  - `importedModules` gains three patterns beside `from '…'`, `require('…')` and the bare `import '…'` (`:208-222`): `import('…')`, `import.meta.resolve('…')` and `require.resolve('…')`. A dynamic import is then checked like a static one, and a test that resolves another package's `package.json` counts as using that package (`bundles/desktop/chat/tests/client/support/window.ts:38`); rule (2) of `imports` allows that one path in a test file. No file in the repository reaches a `src/` path through one of the three today.
- `tests/support/boundaries.mjs`, new. It exports the six finders of the table below, plus `declaredContracts`, `dependencyExceptions`, `isSourcePathImport` and `diffViolations`, and holds the two tables of the checks. Each finder is a function of the files and manifests it is given and returns findings `{ check, bundle, target }`; none reads the disk. `diffViolations` compares findings with the violations file, and reports an entry listed twice as stale.
- `tests/boundaries.test.mjs`, new. It reads the files through `tests/support/workspace.mjs`, runs the six finders and compares their findings with the violations file. The test of `tests/lint.test.mjs:224-245` moves into it and becomes the `imports` test, which keeps the old rule beside the new one in one walk of the files. `tests/lint.test.mjs` keeps its Biome tests (`:46-154`) and imports `root` from the support module.
- The root `package.json` changes one script: `"lint:test": "node --test --test-concurrency=1 tests/lint.test.mjs tests/boundaries.test.mjs"` (`:8`). The option runs the two files one after the other. `tests/lint.test.mjs` writes each Biome fixture into a directory the boundary tests read, such as `packages/session/src/lib` and `packages/agent/tests`, and removes it when the case ends (`tests/lint.test.mjs:18-43,78,94`). A fixture is an untracked source file that Git lists, so a boundary test running beside it would read the fixture as a file of that package, or find it gone between the listing and the read. A skip of the missing file alone would leave the first case. CI already runs the script (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:47-48`); the step's name becomes "Test Biome configuration and bundle boundaries".

| Test           | Rule   | What it reads                                                                                                                           | Violation it reports                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `imports`      | M2     | Every source file under `packages`, `app`, `tools` and `bundles`, as the existing test walks them.                                      | Two rules. (1) As today, with no entry allowed in the violations file: an import of `<package>/src/…` of any workspace package. (2) In a file under `bundles/*/*/{src,tests,stories}`: a module path of another bundle's package that is not `<id>/contracts`. A file under `tests/` or `stories/` may also name `<id>/testing`, and a file under `tests/` may name `<id>/package.json` (D22). `bundle` is the importer, and for a finding of rule (1) it is the importing file's path; `target` is the import path. |
| `declared`     | M3 (a) | Every `src` file of each bundle, the contracts modules of the bundles and of the platform, and the 31 manifests.                        | A contract id that another bundle provides, written as a string literal or reached through an imported symbol, that the bundle's manifest lists under neither `requires` nor `optional`. `bundle` is the user, `target` the contract id. The rule in full is below the table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `cycles`       | M3 (b) | The manifests.                                                                                                                          | A cycle in the graph of the manifests that the listed edges do not break, or a listed edge that breaks none. `bundle` and `target` are the two ends of an edge. The rule in full is below the table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `core`         | M6     | `bundles/foundation/core/manifest.json`, `bundles/foundation/core/src`, and the non-test sources of `packages/*/src` and `app/*/*/src`. | One per finding of the three assertions: a `desktopScope` entry of the core that lists `projectId`, `threadId` or `promptId` (`target` is `<contract>:<key>`); a production import of a bundle's package from a platform package (`bundle` is the package, `target` the import path); the string `'/settings'` in the core's sources (`target` is the file).                                                                                                                                                         |
| `lists`        | M9     | `app/backend/doric-host/project.json`, `app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile`, `app/backend/doric-host/composition.json`.                 | A build file that names a bundle: one finding per file (`bundle` is `doric-host`, `target` the file). An id the composition names that has no manifest on disk (`target` is the id). The rule in full is below the table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `dependencies` | OD11   | Each bundle's `package.json` and the files of its directory, read with `sourceFiles` and `importedModules`.                             | A package the bundle's `package.json` lists and the bundle does not use. `bundle` is the bundle, `target` the package. The rule in full is below the table.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

**`<id>/package.json` in a test file (D22; RD-2 and CB-1 of the fifth review).** Three client-test harnesses open a window on built bundles: those of `chat` and `settings` load the core's built parts, and that of `threads` loads the core's and the chat's (`bundles/desktop/chat/tests/client/support/window.ts:37-40`, `bundles/desktop/settings/tests/client/support/window.ts:35-38`, `bundles/features/threads/tests/client/support/window.ts:34-42`). Each resolves the other package's `package.json` to find its directory and reads `dist` under it, the directory the host stages; it imports no module of that package, and reaches the loaded bundle as a window does, through its contracts. Rule (2) therefore allows that one path, in files under `tests/` only: no story resolves a package's directory. The other fix was four entries with an `until`. It was not taken because no step of this spec rewrites the three harnesses, so the entries would have no end and AC13.1 could not hold; and a harness that loads a built bundle is how a test meets the real provider, which rule (2) exists to protect. A path a test builds at run time from that directory is not a module path and no check reads it; the concept names that limit of the import scan (M2, "Module names built at run time").

**The rules of four checks, in full.** Each is written so that two implementations give the same findings. This spec ran each against commit `ebbf8f94`, with the same results as at `a868b5d0`; they are in the table of what phase 0 writes.

- **`declared`.**
  - _Contract ids._ Every id a manifest lists under `provides`, without its `@version`. The providers of an id are the bundles that list it. Ids that start with `doric.` are skipped: the platform provides them, and no manifest lists them (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/ids.ts:26-28`).
  - _Comment lines_ are blanked before anything is read: a line whose first non-blank characters are `//`, `/*` or `*`. This holds for the contracts modules, where the symbols are read, as for the bundle sources, where the uses are read. A statement runs to the next line that starts with a letter, so it runs over the doc comment of the export after it, and a doc comment names contract ids between backticks. Read with their comment lines, the contracts modules give 104 symbols at this commit; with those lines blanked they give the 87 this spec counts. A comment after code on the same line is not blanked.
  - _A whole string literal_ is a contract id with the same quote character directly before it and directly after it: `'id'`, `"id"` or `` `id` ``. Each of the three kinds is matched on its own, on the text of the file, as the pattern ``(['"`])<id>\1`` matches; the test does not read JavaScript's string syntax, so it does not ask whether the quotes stand inside a string of another kind. An id between single quotes inside a template literal is therefore a use. The finding `postgres` → `runtime.migration/v1` depends on it: the id is a SQL string inside a tagged template (`bundles/foundation/postgres/src/host/database.ts:68`), and that line is the bundle's only use of the id. An id with any other character between the same pair of quotes is no use.
  - _Symbols._ The files read are those under `bundles/*/<id>/src/contracts` for each bundle and those under `packages/bundle/src/contracts` for the platform package `bundle`. A symbol is the name of a statement that starts in column 0 with `export const <Name>`; the statement runs to the next line that starts in column 0 with a letter. The symbol stands for every contract id written in that statement as a whole string literal.
  - _A use,_ in a file under a bundle's `src`: (a) a whole string literal that is a contract id; (b) a name inside the braces of `import { … } from '<package>/contracts'`, or of a subpath of it, where `<package>` is another bundle's package or `bundle` and the name is a symbol of that package. A name written `type <Name>`, and every name of an `import type`, is skipped; `<Name> as <Other>` counts as `<Name>`.
  - _A finding_ is a use, by a bundle, of an id it does not provide and lists under neither `requires` nor `optional`. The core is skipped for the symbols of `bundle/contracts`, as the runtime skips it for a basic contract (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/services.ts:142`): it reads `models.provider/v1` through `ModelsProviderV1` (`bundles/foundation/core/src/host/models/index.ts`).
  - _A second kind of finding_ is a manifest line under `requires` or `optional` whose id no manifest provides and that does not start with `doric.`; `target` is the id. It is how a bundle left on a removed id is found (R4). No manifest has such a line at this commit.
  - _The forms the symbol rule meets at this commit,_ 87 symbols in 21 bundles and 19 in the platform: `ref<T>('id')` on one line (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/terminals.ts:92`) and over several (`bundles/desktop/notifications/src/contracts/index.ts:42-44`); `defineHooks('id', {…})` (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/contracts/index.ts:212`); a string constant (`:809`); `type: 'id'` inside a record or an event definition (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/events.ts:13-14`); and a map of ids (`:35-37`). No bundle source imports another bundle's contracts through `import * as`, a re-export or `import()`; R9 covers a later one.
- **`cycles`.**
  - _The graph._ One edge runs from each bundle to every provider of each contract in its `requires` and `optional`. For a contract that one bundle declares and others provide, the edge runs from the provider to the declarer: `sandbox/v1` and `executor/v1` (declared by `runtime`), and, once they exist, `default.skill-sources/v1` (`default`) and `files.decorations/v1` (`files`). The table of those contracts is a constant of the test.
  - _The entries_ of this check are edges that the test takes out of the graph before it looks for a cycle. They are chosen in review, as the concept's M3 asks for a wrong-way edge.
  - _The test fails_ in three cases: the graph without the listed edges has a strongly connected component of more than one bundle, and the failure names its bundles; a listed edge is not an edge of the graph; a listed edge is not needed, which is when the graph without the other listed edges, and with this one, has no cycle through it. So the list always breaks every cycle and holds no edge that breaks none. This check does not use the rule of the other five, by which each finding has one entry: an edge inside a cycle is not by itself a violation, and listing every such edge would list 41 at phase 0 and one more for each manifest line a later phase adds.
- **`lists`.**
  - _The two build files,_ read line by line, comments included. A line names a bundle when it matches one of two patterns: a path, `(?<![\w./-])bundles/[a-z][a-z0-9-]*/[a-z][a-z0-9-]*`, which is `bundles/`, a group and an id written out; or a project name between double quotes, `"bundle-[a-z][a-z0-9-]*"`, which is how every bundle's `project.json` is named. A file with one or more such lines is one finding.
  - _What the patterns leave alone,_ checked against the two files as they are and as [section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build) leaves them: `COPY bundles ./bundles` (`app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:17`); `dist/bundles/<id>` in a comment (`:37`) and `app/backend/doric-host/dist/bundles` in a command, both preceded by `/`; the glob `bundles/*/*/manifest.json` (`:86`); `stage … --all bundles`; `"tag:type:bundle"`; and the target name `"build-bundles"`. At this commit the path pattern matches 31 paths on line 23 of `project.json` and the name pattern its 31 project names (`:31-61`); the path pattern matches 64 times in the `.Dockerfile` (`:52-97`).
  - _The composition._ Every id under `core`, `enabled`, `resident`, `required.bundles`, the values of `providers`, and `default` and `options` of each entry of `alternatives` has a `bundles/*/<id>/manifest.json`. At this commit the composition names 31 ids and each has one.
- **`dependencies`.**
  - _What is checked:_ every name under `dependencies` and under `devDependencies` of a bundle's `package.json`. No bundle has `peerDependencies` or `optionalDependencies`.
  - _A package is used_ when one of five holds. (1) A file of the bundle's directory has a module specifier that is the package's name or starts with the name and `/`; the files are those `sourceFiles` returns, so tests, stories and files such as `prisma.config.ts` count, and the specifiers are those `importedModules` returns. (2) The package is `tslib`: the compiler adds its import under `importHelpers` (`tsconfig.base.json:6`), and no source file names it; 30 bundles list it. (3) The package is `react` and the bundle has a `.tsx` or `.jsx` file: under `react-jsx` the compiler adds the import of `react/jsx-runtime`. (4) The package is `@types/<name>` and `<name>` is `node` or is itself used by (1), where `a__b` stands for `@a/b`. (5) The pair is in a constant of the test, with its reason: `postgres` uses `@prisma/client`, which only the generated client imports, and that client is not tracked.
  - _A finding_ is a listed package that is not used. `devDependencies` are checked like `dependencies`, because a bundle lists its client-only and test-only packages there (`bundles/features/terminal/package.json:28-36`), and a line there also orders the Nx builds.
  - The check does not decide under which of the two keys a package belongs; Biome's rule below covers a package that is used and listed under neither.

The other half of the dependency check, used but not declared, is Biome's rule `noUndeclaredDependencies` (Biome 2.5.15, `package.json:32`), which `biome.json` does not enable today and phase 0 enables for `bundles/**`, with the rule's default options, by a second entry of `overrides` after the one the file has (`biome.json:85-96`): `{ "includes": ["bundles/**"], "linter": { "rules": { "correctness": { "noUndeclaredDependencies": "error" } } } }`. It is part of `npm run lint`, and its 13 findings at this commit are fixed in phase 0 by eight lines in `package.json` files, with no change to a source or test file: `usage` declares `host-config`, for two findings; the core declares `claude` under `devDependencies`, beside the three providers it lists there (`bundles/foundation/core/package.json:22,26,27`), for the import of `bundles/foundation/core/tests/models.test.ts:7`; `files`, `git`, `layout`, `resources` and `terminal`, whose test support imports `happy-dom`, declare it under `devDependencies`, for nine findings; and `openui` declares `typescript` under `devDependencies`, with the range of the root (`"typescript": "~5.9.2"`, `package.json:61`), for the import of `bundles/features/openui/tests/client-split.test.ts:16`. Version 7 ran the rule itself, Biome 2.5.15 with the override, on a copy of the tree at this commit; versions 5 and 6 counted the thirteen findings and listed seven of the eight lines. The core's test keeps its four imports of a provider's `host` until step 6(b); they are the `imports` entries below, and step 6(b) removes the four `devDependencies` lines with them.

**The violations file.** `tests/boundary-exceptions.json`:

```json
{
  "violations": [
    {
      "check": "cycles",
      "bundle": "files",
      "target": "git",
      "decision": "docs/features/bundle-isolation.md",
      "until": "phase 9"
    }
  ]
}
```

- `violations` is what the six tests find and tolerate, and the file holds nothing else. `check` is one of `imports`, `declared`, `cycles`, `core`, `lists`, `dependencies`. `until` names the phase or step that removes the entry, written `phase 9` or `step 4(d)`. Every entry phase 0 writes has one; the field is absent only on an entry that stays, and this spec leaves none. `decision` is the path of the document that records why the violation is tolerated. Every entry phase 0 writes has `"decision": "docs/features/bundle-isolation.md"`, the feature doc, which names each of them. Its Problem, model and Phases name the five cycle edges, the entries of `core`, the two build files and `postgres/prisma`. Its Evolution entry for version 7 of this spec names the five test imports the concept did not name before: `runtime` → `terminal/host`, and `core` → `claude/host`, `codex/host`, `openai/host` and `openrouter/host`, each with the step that removes it. An entry a later change adds names the file that records its own decision. No test reads `until`; a phase or step is accepted only when no entry names it there ([Acceptance criteria](#acceptance-criteria)), so an entry does not outlive its phase unnoticed.
- Each test but `cycles` fails on a finding that has no entry with its `check`, `bundle` and `target`; on an entry of its `check` that matches no finding; and on an entry whose `decision` is not a file in the repository. The list therefore always equals the violations at that commit. `cycles` fails as its rule above says, and on the same missing `decision`. Rule (1) of `imports` takes no entry.

**The kept decisions.** OD15 and OD17–OD19 keep things that no test finds: the second and third bullets of [Non-goals](#non-goals). They are not in the violations file, because a test of such entries could only check that they are well formed:

- They are stated once, in the concept's [Decisions](../features/bundle-isolation.md#decisions), which is the feature doc.
- A phase that confirms one of them says so in its Evolution entry of the feature doc: phase 2 for what the core still names, phase 7 for the repository hint and the REST error table, phase 4 for the notice. A drawing that stays built into the chat by R1 is recorded the same way.
- Phase 13 writes the list "Kept decisions" into the feature doc's "Current behavior and contracts": one line per kept decision, with the decision that keeps it, the path that holds it and a `git grep` command that finds it there. AC13.5 is the check: the phase report shows each command with its output.

**What phase 0 writes into `violations`.** The counts come from the rules above, which this spec ran at this commit with a script of its own; the test's own run is the authority, and a difference from this table is a finding of phase 0's review.

| Check          | Entries at phase 0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Removed by                                                                                                                   |
| -------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `imports`      | `core` → `claude/host`, `codex/host`, `openai/host`, `openrouter/host` (`bundles/foundation/core/tests/models.test.ts`). `runtime` → `postgres/prisma` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/store/support.ts:16`) and `terminal/host` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/support/harness.ts`). `runtime` → `postgres/testing` is allowed by D22 and is no entry, and neither are the four `package.json` paths of the three window harnesses, `core/package.json` in `chat`, `settings` and `threads` and `chat/package.json` in `threads` (`bundles/desktop/chat/tests/client/support/window.ts:38`, `bundles/desktop/settings/tests/client/support/window.ts:36`, `bundles/features/threads/tests/client/support/window.ts:36,40`).                                                                                                                                         | Step 6(b) (the core's test uses its own fake providers); phase 10 (`postgres/prisma`); step 7(c) (`terminal/host`).          |
| `declared`     | None. Phase 0 adds the 22 manifest lines below in the same change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | —                                                                                                                            |
| `cycles`       | Five edges, each the wrong-way edge of a cycle that exists once the 22 lines do: `files` → `git`; `runtime` → `terminal`; `runtime` → `resources`; `postgres` → `runtime`; `chat` → `terminal`. The last closes `chat` → `terminal` → `files` → `chat`: the chat lists `terminal/v1` (`bundles/desktop/chat/manifest.json:19`), and phase 0 adds `files.refresh/v1` to `terminal` and `chat.hooks/v1` to `files`. With the five taken out the graph has no cycle, and each of the five is needed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Phase 9; step 7(c); step 7(c); phase 10; step 5(b), the change of `terminal`, in which the chat stops reading `terminal/v1`. |

| `dependencies` | None. The first run finds 14 packages that no file of their bundle uses, and phase 0 removes each line and runs `npm install` and `nx sync` ([section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup)). `nx sync` removes the reference in each `tsconfig.json`; one reference that no tool writes is removed by hand, the line for `tool` in `bundles/features/papercraft/tsconfig.spec.json:23`. The 14 lines: in `layout`, `@dnd-kit/core`, `@dnd-kit/modifiers`, `@dnd-kit/sortable`, `@dnd-kit/utilities`, `@xterm/addon-fit`, `@xterm/xterm`, `monaco-editor`, `react-resizable-panels` and `resources` (`L/package.json:16-19,21-22,26,29-30`); in `papercraft`, `tool`; in `threads`, `react-dom`; and under `devDependencies`, `chat` in `openui` and `react-dom` in `resources` and in `usage`. The three bundles whose tests resolve `core/package.json` use `core` by rule (1). | —                                                                                                                            |

**The 22 manifest lines.** Nineteen are uses of an id written as a literal; three are reached through an imported symbol: `runtime.terminals/v1` in `git` (`RuntimeTerminalsV1`), `runtime.prompt-finished/v1` in `chat` (`legacyEventTypes`) and `terminal/v1` in `runtime` (`TerminalServiceV1`). Phase 0 adds each under `optional`, never under `requires`: the resolver orders activation and quarantines by `requires` alone (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/catalog.ts:590-655`). An `optional` line does one thing at run time: it adds the id to the set of contracts the bundle may use (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/activation.ts:116-119`, which `packages/bundle/src/runtime/services.ts:133-152` reads; the build's recorder builds the same set, `packages/bundle/src/build/record.ts:54-57`). The set only grows, so a `services.use` that is refused with `undeclared_requirement` today is admitted afterwards, and no call that succeeds today fails.
