Later phases remove eight of the twenty-two again, each with the use it declares: `layout.workspace/v1` in `chat` and in `threads` (step 4(d)), `core.open-settings/v1` in `layout` (step 4(c)), `git.changes/v1` (phase 9), `runtime.migration/v1` (phase 10), `resources/v1` and `terminal/v1` in `runtime` (step 7(c)), and `runtime.terminals/v1` in `git` (step 7(c), where `terminal/v1` takes its place). The three lines `chat.hooks/v1` become `chat.hooks/v2` in step 5(c), each in the change that moves its bundle.

**What stays review.** M1, M4, M5, M7, M8 and M10 have no test, as the concept's [What a machine can check](../features/bundle-isolation.md#what-a-machine-can-check) explains. M4 is covered by one acceptance test per extension point (T5.1, T5.2, T6.1, T8.1, T9.1).

### 2. CI

One step is added to the job `main` of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, after "Run Doric tests" (`:61-64`), where the database is migrated:

```yaml
- name: Run bundle tests
  env:
    DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL: postgresql://doric:doric@127.0.0.1:5432/doric
  run: npx nx run-many -t test --projects=tag:type:bundle
```

- Every one of the 31 bundle projects carries the tag `type:bundle` and has a `test` target (read from each `bundles/*/*/project.json`). A new bundle is covered by creating its `project.json` with the tag.
- **Gate variables.** Four exist in the bundle tests. The step sets one and leaves three unset, and a comment above the step names each of the three with its reason:

| Variable                     | Tests it gates                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | In CI                                                        |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL`    | The `postgres` tests (`bundles/foundation/postgres/tests/support/database.ts`), and in the runtime the store tests (`bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/store/support.ts`), `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/handover.test.ts`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/workspace-restart.test.ts` and `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/adapter-capture.test.ts`. | Set.                                                         |
| `DORIC_TEST_SANDBOX`         | The sandbox tests of `docker` (`bundles/sandboxes/docker/tests/docker.test.ts`) and of `terminal` (`bundles/features/terminal/tests/docker.test.ts`).                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Unset: they need the sandbox image, which CI does not build. |
| `DORIC_TEST_COALESCE_SIZE`   | `bundles/foundation/postgres/tests/coalesce-deltas-size.test.ts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Unset: a size measurement, not a correctness test.           |
| `DORIC_TEST_ADAPTER_CAPTURE` | `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/adapter-capture.test.ts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Unset: it records a live provider and needs a key.           |

- **Time.** Phase 1 records the duration of the job on `main` before the step exists and on the phase's own pull request. If the job takes more than twice as long, the step moves to a second job, `bundles`, with the same service and the same migration step. The two numbers go into the phase report.
- **Required.** Which checks a pull request needs is a setting of the repository on GitHub, the required status checks of the branch `main`, and no file of the repository holds it. As a step of the job `main` the bundle tests gate a merge wherever that job does, with no change to the setting. If the step moves to the job `bundles`, the repository's owner adds the check `bundles` to that setting before phase 1 merges, and the phase report says that it is there.
- **A failing bundle test at phase 1.** The bundle tests have never run on the CI runner (concept, A5). A test that fails there for a reason of the runner (a missing binary, a time zone, a port) is fixed in phase 1. A test that fails because the product is wrong is not fixed in Spec 1 (U28). The author of phase 1 creates one Linear issue for it in the team DORIC, with the test's file and name, the failure's output and the commit, and skips the test with `{ skip: 'DORIC-<n>: <reason>' }`, so the skip names its issue. The phase report lists each skip with its issue. Whoever closes an issue removes its skip in the same change.
- **A test that passes and fails on one commit.** The pull request of phase 1 runs the job five times. A bundle test that fails in one of the five runs and passes in another is skipped the same way, with its own issue, unless the cause is the runner's, which phase 1 fixes.

### 3. Core and kernel

**`core.open-settings/v1` (D19, U17).** The input gains one field, in `bundles/foundation/core/src/contracts/commands.ts:5-7`:

```ts
export const openSettingsInput = z
  .object({
    path: z.string().optional(),
    /** 1.1.0: where a new settings window opens when `path` is absent. */
    defaultPath: z.string().optional(),
  })
  .strict();
```

| `path` | `defaultPath` | A settings window is open | Result                                                                                                                         |
| ------ | ------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| given  | any           | yes or no                 | As today: the window is focused, or opened, and navigated to `path` (`bundles/foundation/core/src/desktop/commands.ts:25-43`). |
| absent | any           | yes                       | As today: the window is focused and keeps its section.                                                                         |
| absent | given         | no                        | A window opens at `defaultPath`.                                                                                               |
| absent | absent        | no                        | The command rejects with `ContractError('invalid_input')`. Today it opens at `/settings`.                                      |

- The literal `'/settings'` leaves `commands.ts`. The two callers pass `{ defaultPath: '/settings' }`: the account menu's "Settings" and the dashboard's "All settings", which both call `openSettings` in `L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:169-176` until phase 4 moves them to `settings`.
- The contract goes from 1.0.0 to 1.1.0 (`bundles/foundation/core/manifest.json:26`) and keeps its id (U21).
- **The version.** The fourth row of the table above changes the meaning of a call that is valid today, for which the repository's table asks for a major. The contract keeps its id as a versioning deviation (U21); the decision, its reasons and its release note are in [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions). One caller outside tests makes that call today, `L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:169-176`, and this phase changes it.
- **A caller this spec does not change** that executes the command with no input and no settings window open gets `invalid_input` from the first build with phase 2. A menu or a keybinding executes a command with an empty input (`bundles/foundation/core/tests/desktop/commands.test.ts:32`; `app/desktop/doric/src/platform/app.ts:1250-1255`), so an entry bound to this command must give `{ defaultPath }` as its input, as the two items of [section 8](#8-slots) do. No menu and no keybinding in the repository is bound to it.
- **Where the change is written,** in phase 2: the doc comment of `openSettingsInput` in `bundles/foundation/core/src/contracts/commands.ts` and of `openSettings` in `bundles/foundation/core/src/desktop/commands.ts:18-24`, which says "`/settings` by default" today; the core's README; and the release notes of the change, with the text [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions) gives.
- **Three existing tests make that call** and are edited in phase 2: `bundles/foundation/core/tests/desktop/commands.test.ts:26-40`, `app/desktop/doric/tests/generation.test.ts:222-228` and `app/desktop/doric-e2e/tests/platform.test.ts:180-181`. Each passes `{ defaultPath: '/settings' }` on the call that opens the window and keeps its assertions. The second call of the end-to-end test (`:185`) and the call of `commands.test.ts:175` stay without input: a window is open, and they prove the focus case. Three other tests execute the command with `{}` against a fake or a probe and are not affected: `bundles/foundation/core/tests/client/commands.test.ts:82`, `L/tests/client/fake-host.test.ts:69-72` and `L/tests/client/account-menu.test.ts:124`.

**The scope keys (U4; concept, X3).** `projectId`, `threadId` and `promptId` leave the six `desktopScope` entries of `bundles/foundation/core/manifest.json:33-40`, one contract per merged change, in this order: `windows/v1`, `menus/v1`, `tools/v1`, `models/v1`, `secrets/v1`, `core.commands/v1`. After the last, the key `desktopScope` is removed from the manifest.

- The window-side call sites of the six contracts, as found by search: `menus/v1` in `bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/index.ts:100`, `bundles/features/papercraft/src/client/index.ts:117` and `bundles/foundation/core/src/client/index.ts:50`; `models/v1` in `bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/index.ts:114` and `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:1293,1301,1319`; `tools/v1` in `settings-api.ts:1289`; `secrets/v1` in `settings-api.ts:1334-1366`, `bundles/features/git/src/client/settings/versioning.tsx:25` and `bundles/features/mcp/src/client/api.ts:164,168`; `core.commands/v1` in `bundles/foundation/core/src/client/commands.ts:60`. No client part calls `windows/v1`. None of these passes a scope. The one that forwards a `call` it received is the last, which is why `core.commands/v1` goes last.
- Each of the six changes runs the desktop end-to-end suite (`app/desktop/doric-e2e`). A call that still carries a key is rejected with `invalid_input` at the bridge (`app/desktop/doric/src/platform/bridge/dispatcher.ts:652-662`); the fix is to stop sending the key, not to restore the manifest line.
- `app/backend/doric-host/tests/bridge-exposure.snapshot.json` changes with each of the six and is updated in the same change.

**The kernel's buffer (D20, U11).** `app/backend/doric-host/src/index.ts` stops importing `runtime/contracts` (`:9`) and declares its own constant:

```ts
/** The largest Socket.IO message the host accepts, in bytes. */
export const SOCKET_MAX_HTTP_BUFFER_BYTES = 8_039_084;
```

- The value is what `Math.ceil(ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES / 3) * 4 + 1024 * 1024` gives today (`:90`, with `ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/attachments.ts:25`): 6,990,508 bytes of base64 plus 1,048,576 for the envelope. The limit of the public Socket.IO API therefore does not change.
- A new host test imports `ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` from `runtime/contracts` and fails when the constant is smaller than `Math.ceil(ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES / 3) * 4 + 1_048_576`. The allowance is written in the test as its own number.

### 4. The records mirror (`runtime`)

The runtime gains a client part, `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/client/`, which provides one client contract, `runtime.records/v1` (D3, U12). It is built from the seven files [section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file) sends to it (the host reference, the adapter, the Project watch, the Thread views, the two connection files and the Project events hook) and from the record half of four files it splits (`domain/project-tree.ts`, `stores/workspace.ts`, `domain/workspace.ts` and `hooks/use-workspace.ts`).

```ts
// bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/records.ts
/** One read of the mirror. A failed refresh keeps the last `value`. */
export type RecordsRead<T> =
  | { readonly status: 'loading'; readonly value?: T }
  | { readonly status: 'ready'; readonly value: T }
  | {
      readonly status: 'failed';
      readonly value?: T;
      readonly error: { readonly code: string; readonly message: string };
    };

export interface RuntimeRecords {
  // Reads: local, synchronous, answered from memory.
  /** The first call starts the load of the Project list and answers `loading`. */
  projects(call?: CallArg): RecordsRead<readonly Project[]>;
  project(projectId: string, call?: CallArg): Project | undefined;
  /** The first call for a Project starts the load of its Threads and answers `loading`. */
  threads(projectId: string, call?: CallArg): RecordsRead<readonly Thread[]>;
  /** A Thread of a described Project; `undefined` otherwise. Starts no load. */
  thread(threadId: string, call?: CallArg): Thread | undefined;
  connection(call?: CallArg): 'connected' | 'disconnected';
  /** Called after every change of a read's answer or of the connection. */
  subscribe(listener: () => void, call?: CallArg): Disposable;

  // Loads.
  /** Keeps the Project and its Threads current until disposed. */
  follow(input: { readonly projectId: string }, call?: CallArg): Disposable;
  /** Starts the read again, whatever its state. It answers `loading` with its last `value` until it settles. */
  reload(
    input:
      | { readonly target: 'projects' }
      | { readonly target: 'threads'; readonly projectId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): void;
  /** Asks the host for one Thread, for a Project that is not described; `undefined` when the host has no such Thread. Changes no read. */
  findThread(
    input: { readonly threadId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Thread | undefined>;
  activity(call?: CallArg): Promise<readonly ProjectActivity[]>;

  // Writes: each calls the host and puts the answer into the mirror before it resolves.
  createProject(
    input: { readonly name: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Project>;
  renameProject(
    input: { readonly projectId: string; readonly name: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Project>;
  setProjectColor(
    input: { readonly projectId: string; readonly color?: ProjectColor },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Project>;
  terminateProject(
    input: { readonly projectId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Project>;
  deleteProject(
    input: { readonly projectId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<void>;
  createThread(
    input: {
      readonly projectId: string;
      readonly name: string;
      readonly parentThreadId?: string;
    },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Thread>;
  renameThread(
    input: { readonly threadId: string; readonly name: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Thread>;
  terminateThread(
    input: { readonly threadId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Thread>;
  deleteThread(
    input: { readonly threadId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<void>;
  /** Archives the Thread and its idle, unpinned descendants as one batch. `threads` holds every record the archive marked, the target first. */
  archiveThread(
    input: { readonly threadId: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<{ readonly batch: string; readonly threads: readonly Thread[] }>;
  /** Un-archives the Thread and its descendants of one batch: `batch`, else the Thread's own. Resolves with every restored record. */
  restoreThread(
    input: { readonly threadId: string; readonly batch?: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<readonly Thread[]>;
  setThreadPinned(
    input: { readonly threadId: string; readonly pinned: boolean },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<Thread>;

  // Presence.
  /** Tells the host that this window shows the Thread, until disposed. Changes no read and reports no failure. */
  view(input: { readonly threadId: string }, call?: CallArg): Disposable;
}

/** The ids of `rootId` and of every Thread of `threads` below it. Pure. */
export const threadSubtreeIds: (
  threads: readonly Thread[],
  rootId: string,
) => ReadonlySet<string>;

export const RuntimeRecordsV1 = ref<RuntimeRecords>('runtime.records/v1');
```

- `Project`, `Thread`, `ProjectColor` and `ProjectActivity` are the runtime's own types, already in `runtime/contracts`. The contract hands out the owner's records, which M7 asks for.
- **Described and followed.** A Project is in one of three states in a window, and how current its Threads are follows from the state:

  | State         | How a Project enters it                                                                                                                                                                                                    | What changes its list of Threads                                                                    |
  | ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | Not described | The start.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Nothing. `threads(projectId)` has no `value`, and `thread(id)` answers `undefined` for its Threads. |
  | Described     | The first `threads(projectId)` call starts one load of its Threads from the host. The Project is described when that load, or the first snapshot of a `follow`, has answered, and stays described until the window closes. | The mirror's own writes, and `reload`. Nothing arrives from the host.                               |
  | Followed      | At least one `follow` of the Project is held.                                                                                                                                                                              | The watch: each snapshot replaces the Project and its list, and each update is applied.             |
  - A described Project that is no longer followed keeps its last list and receives nothing from the host until it is followed again or reloaded. This is the layout's behavior today: it watches the selected Project only (`L/src/client/hooks/use-workspace.ts:335-342`), loads a Project's Threads when the Project is selected (`:145-171,268,397`), and keeps the other lists as they were; an absent list means "not described yet" (`L/src/client/domain/project-tree.ts:21`).
  - `projects()` loads the Project list once. Afterwards the list changes by the mirror's writes, by the Project record a snapshot carries, and by `reload`.
  - `project`, `thread` and `connection` answer from memory and start no load. `findThread` and `activity` each ask the host once and change no read.
  - A consumer that needs a Project's Threads current holds a `follow` of it. In the shipped composition `workspace` follows the selected Project and no other bundle follows, so a window holds one watch, as today.

- **`follow`.** While at least one `follow` of a Project is held, the part keeps one `runtime/v1.watchProject` stream for it (`runtime/v2.watchProject` from step 7(a)), with the rules the layout's watch has today (`L/src/client/api/project-watch.ts:1-14`): every subscription starts with a full snapshot; a dropped connection or a lost generation subscribes again without reporting; a stream that ends with a failure makes `threads(projectId)` answer `failed` with the code `project_stream_failed`.
- **Activation.** The part's `activate` provides the contract and returns. It calls no host method and awaits nothing: the first host call is made by the first `projects()` or `threads()` read or by the first `follow`. A host that cannot be reached is therefore a state of a read and never a failed activation (R3, T3.6).
- **The reads and their states** are the three failure cases of the concept ([Records mirror](../features/bundle-isolation.md#records-mirror-the-runtimes-client-part)):
  - _The part is absent or quarantined:_ a bundle that lists `runtime.records/v1` under `requires` is quarantined with it; a bundle that lists it under `optional` receives no provider and draws nothing.
  - _The host cannot be reached:_ `connection()` answers `disconnected`, and every read keeps its last `value`.
  - _One read fails:_ that read answers `failed` with the error and its last `value`, and `reload` starts it again.
- **The writes** keep the adapter's behavior: the payload guards, the retry of a delete every 500 ms while the host answers `active`, and the error messages of the REST routes (`L/src/client/api/workspace-api.ts:1-10,34-69,142-162`).
- **The three archive writes (D33).** `archiveThread`, `restoreThread` and `setThreadPinned` are the adapter's `threads.archive`, `threads.restore` and `threads.setPinned`, which the thread archive added (`L/src/client/api/workspace-api.ts:210-239,368-404`). They call `runtime.threads/v1` 1.8.0, which the runtime provides itself.
  - Each puts every record of its answer into the mirror before it resolves, as the layout's actions apply them today (`L/src/client/hooks/use-workspace.ts:559-599`): the Threads an archive marked, the Threads a restore brought back, the pinned or unpinned Thread.
  - An archived Thread stays in `threads(projectId)` and in `thread(id)`, with `archivedAt` set. The mirror hides no record: a consumer that draws only the Threads that are not archived tests the field, as the layout's `isArchived` does (`L/src/client/domain/thread-archive.ts:18-19`).
  - The toast "Thread archived" and its Undo are not the mirror's: the action that calls `archiveThread` shows them and keeps the `batch` for the Undo (`L/src/client/hooks/use-workspace.ts:572-587`), and that action goes to `workspace` ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)).
- **`view` (D33).** A window tells the host which Threads it shows, so that the runtime's archiver leaves them alone ([docs/designs/thread-archive.md](thread-archive.md), sections 6 and 10.8).
  - While at least one `view` of a Thread is held, the part keeps one `runtime.threads/v1.viewThread` stream for it. The code is the layout's `createThreadViews` (`L/src/client/api/thread-views.ts:36-75`), moved whole: one stream per Thread, followed with `followStream` of the Project watch (`L/src/client/api/project-watch.ts:113-198`), so a dropped connection, a host that drains and a lost generation open the view again without a report.
  - The part counts the views held for each Thread. Every `view` call and every disposal of one hands the set of Threads with a view held to `set` of `createThreadViews`. So a second `view` of a Thread opens no second stream, the last disposal ends the stream, and a stream that ended with a failure (`not_found` for a Thread the host does not have, or `internal`) is opened again by the next call or disposal while a view of that Thread is still held. This is the rule `set` has today (`L/src/client/api/thread-views.ts:17-26`).
  - The holder learns nothing: the one item of the stream carries nothing, and a failure is neither reported nor logged, as today (`L/src/client/api/thread-views.ts:28-35`). `view` starts no load, changes no read and calls no subscriber.
  - The `viewThread` stream is opened on the first `view`, so the part's activation still calls no host method.
- **`threadSubtreeIds`** is the layout's function of that name (`L/src/client/domain/workspace.ts:107-127`), declared in `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/records.ts` and exported by `runtime/contracts`. The mirror's store uses it to remove a deleted Thread with its descendants, and `workspace` uses it to count the descendants a delete removes (`L/src/client/domain/thread-archive.ts:166-174`). `workspace` requires `runtime.records/v1`, so it imports the function as a value (D32). It is the one record helper a file that goes to `workspace` calls; the others move into the part and are not exported.
- **The error of a rejected call (CB-4 of the fifth review).** A write, `findThread` or `activity` that fails rejects with the `ClientError` of `desktop/reads` that the adapter throws today (`packages/desktop/src/reads/errors.ts:64-91`), unchanged. It is not a `ContractError`.
  - _The adapter's own codes,_ each with the adapter's message and no `cause`: `invalid_input`, from a payload guard or a route's id check, before any host call (`L/src/client/api/workspace-api.ts:85-126,329`); `not_found`, when the host has no such record or answers `missing` (`:130-133,150-151,339-341,374-376,393-395,400-403`); `conflict`, when a delete answers `forbidden`, when the host refuses to create a Thread, when an archive answers `busy` or `already_archived`, or when a restore answers `not_archived` (`:154-158,342,377-382,396`); `invalid_output`, for a list whose cursor repeats (`:175-177`).
  - _The host's codes._ A `ContractError` of the host whose code is not `internal` keeps its code, takes the adapter's message for that code and becomes the `cause` (`L/src/client/api/workspace-api.ts:53-72`).
  - _`internal`,_ for any other failure, with the message "The request could not be completed." and no `cause` (`L/src/client/api/workspace-api.ts:73`).
  - _Why the class stays._ The provider and its consumers are client parts of one window: a call through `services.use` reaches the provider in the same process, and a rejection arrives as the object the provider threw (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/dispatch.ts:214-231`). The surfaces read `code`, `message`, `retryable` and `cause` by field, through `errorInfo`, and never by `instanceof` (`packages/desktop/src/reads/errors.ts:56-63`, `L/src/client/stores/workspace.ts:188`). Keeping the object the adapter throws changes nothing a reader sees, by construction. Rebuilding each error as a `ContractError` would have to carry the same four fields to give the same notice, and would gain nothing: no consumer tests the class.
  - The `error` of a read that answers `failed` holds the `code` and the `message` of the same rejection.
- **Who lists it how.** `requires`, as `runtime.records/v1@^1.0.0`: `workspace`, `chat`, `terminal`, `files`, `git`, `threads`. `optional`, as the id alone: `screen`, and `layout` from phase 3 until step 4(c), while it still holds the navigation.
- The runtime's manifest gains `runtime.records/v1@1.0.0` under `provides`. The contract's schemas are `runtimeRecordsMethods`, in `src/contracts/records.ts` beside the types, written as the existing client contracts write theirs (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/services/settings-config.ts:38-54`): `noInput` for a read without input, `z.string()` for an id, a strict `z.object` for each input object above, `functionSchema` for the listener of `subscribe`, `disposableSchema` for a `Disposable`, the existing `projectSchema` and `threadSchema` for a record (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/model.ts:662,683`), and for a `RecordsRead` a `z.discriminatedUnion` on `status` with the three members of the type. The answer of `archiveThread` is a strict `z.object` of `batch: z.string()` and `threads: z.array(threadSchema)`, and that of `restoreThread` is `z.array(threadSchema)`. The schemas check types and shapes only, with no length and no format, so the adapter's guards answer every value a caller's types allow, with their own messages. An input of the wrong type is refused by the platform with a `ContractError` `invalid_input` before it reaches the adapter (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/dispatch.ts:177-185`), as for every contract.
- The part's build files, its package and its test entry are in [section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup); the fake of this contract that other bundles' tests and stories provide is in [section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file).

### 5. The shell (`layout`)

**Function.** The workspace window's frame and its slots. The manifest's `description` becomes "The workspace window's frame and its slots.", and `requires` loses `runtime/v1` and `runtime.threads/v1` (`L/manifest.json:23-24`). The element stays `layout-workspace`, and the shell registers the route `/` only, where it registers three today (`L/src/client/index.ts:59-65`); the paragraph on the routes below says when.

**The two forms (D5).** The shell draws the page form while the slot `shell.page` holds at least one item, and the conversation form otherwise.

| Place                         | Page form                                                              | Conversation form                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Sidebar column, top to bottom | `shell.sidebar`; the quarantined-bundle notice; `shell.sidebar.footer` | `shell.sidebar`; the panes of `layout.stack/v2`; the quarantined-bundle notice; `shell.sidebar.footer`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Title bar                     | `shell.titlebar.tabs`, then `shell.window.actions` at its end          | `shell.titlebar.tabs`, then `shell.header.actions` at its end                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Body                          | `shell.page`                                                           | The conversation column: the viewer tabs of `layout.tabs/v2`; `<core-outlet>`; `shell.conversation.footer` floating over its bottom; `shell.conversation.bottom` while shown; `shell.conversation.overlay`. Beside it the side panel: its views `shell.panel`, `shell.window.actions` at the end of its header, `shell.panel.footer`. |

- The shell always draws `<core-outlet>` in the conversation column. The text it shows today when no Thread is selected (`L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:325-347`) names a Project and becomes the element of the route `/projects/:projectId`, which `workspace` registers ([section 6](#6-workspace-navigation-workspace)).
- **The notice (D8; concept, X19).** Today it is the last child of the Project sidebar, after the stack panes (`L/src/client/components/organisms/project-sidebar.tsx:490-493`), and in the dashboard it sits between the sidebar's content and its footer (`L/src/client/components/organisms/dashboard-sidebar.tsx:146-147`). The order in the table keeps both positions. It keeps its margins: `mx-1.5` in the conversation form and `mx-2` in the page form. The slot `shell.sidebar.footer` (U22) is empty in the conversation form.
- **Why the rule holds at the first paint.** The window activates every client part before it mounts its root element (`packages/desktop/src/client/bootstrap.ts:56,90-94`), so `workspace` has added its items when the frame first draws. Without `workspace` the shell draws the conversation form with empty slots.

**The routes and the empty state, change by change (RD-M7 of the fourth review).** The reader sees the same screens after each change.

|                                                                                                    | `/`                                                                                     | `/projects` and `/projects/:projectId`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | The text "Start a thread or select one from the sidebar."                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Today, and until the sixth change of step 4(a)                                                     | The layout registers it and creates `layout-workspace` (`L/src/client/index.ts:59-62`). | The layout registers both, each creating a `layout-workspace` of its own, with no `parent` (`:63-65`).                                                                                                                                                                                 | Drawn by the frame, in place of `<core-outlet>`, while no Thread is selected (`L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:323-347`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| From the seventh change of step 4(a), which adds the five slots and the two forms, until step 4(c) | Unchanged.                                                                              | The layout still registers both, now with `parent: '/'`, as the chat registers its Thread route (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/index.ts:87-89`). `/projects` creates `layout-projects-route`, an element that draws nothing; `/projects/:projectId` creates `layout-project-empty`. | Drawn by `layout-project-empty`, a new element the layout defines in `L/src/client/elements/project-empty-element.tsx` from the markup of `L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:326-346`: the Project's name or "No project selected", the text, and the "New thread" button. The frame always draws `<core-outlet>`, which holds that element on `/projects/:projectId`. |
| From step 4(c)                                                                                     | Unchanged: the one route the shell registers.                                           | `workspace` registers both, with `parent: '/'`. The two elements move to it with the navigation and take the tags `workspace-projects-route` and `workspace-project-empty` ([section 6](#6-workspace-navigation-workspace)).                                                           | Drawn by `workspace-project-empty`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

- The element keeps both states of the markup: the Project's name with the button, and the title "No project selected" without the button while the Project of the route is not one the mirror holds (`L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:329,335`). On `/` and on `/projects` the page form covers the conversation column, today and after, so neither state shows there.
- A frame that was created once per route (`:57-63`) is created once per window from the seventh change on, because the two routes become children of `/`. What it shows comes from the stores, which outlive an element, so no state is lost with the two extra frames.

**`layout.tabs/v2`, new, 2.0.0 (D6, U27).** It replaces `layout.tabs/v1` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:17-166`), which the layout provides unchanged until the sixth change of step 4(a) (D30). The interface is that of 1.2.0 with these differences:

```ts
export interface TabRequest {
  // id, title, tag, props, ariaLabel, tooltip, menu: as in `layout.tabs/v1` 1.2.0.
  // `icon` and the type `TabIcon` are not part of v2.
  /** The element drawn as the trigger's 16 px icon, created through `ui/v1` with the tab's `props`. */
  readonly iconTag?: string;
  /** The viewer card draws no bottom padding while this tab is selected. */
  readonly openBottom?: boolean;
}

export interface LayoutTabs {
  // open, focus, current, subscribe: as in 1.2.0.
  close(
    input: {
      readonly id: string;
      /** The owner key whose tab closes; absent, the active owner. As in 1.1.0. */
      readonly threadId?: string;
      /** Closes the tab with this id in every owner; `threadId` is then ignored. */
      readonly everyOwner?: boolean;
    },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<void>;
  retitle(
    input: {
      readonly id: string;
      readonly title: string;
      readonly threadId?: string;
      readonly everyOwner?: boolean;
    },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<void>;
  /** Names the set of tabs the frame shows; absent, no set. Local. */
  setOwner(input: { readonly owner?: string }, call?: CallArg): void;
}

export const LayoutTabsV2 = ref<LayoutTabs>('layout.tabs/v2');
```

- **Two meanings differ from v1, and they are why the id is new (U27).** The owner of the tabs is the last key `setOwner` gave the shell, where the layout derives it from the route today (`L/src/client/index.ts:67-80`). A tab's icon is the element `iconTag` names, where `icon` names one of four drawings the layout holds (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:57`). The request schema of v2 is strict, as that of v1 is (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:106-133`), so a v2 request that carries `icon` is refused with `invalid_input`. The schemas of v2 are those of v1 with these changes: the request loses `icon` and gains `iconTag: z.string().min(1).optional()` and `openBottom: z.boolean().optional()`; the inputs of `close` and `retitle` gain `everyOwner: z.boolean().optional()`; and `setOwner` takes `z.object({ owner: z.string().min(1).optional() }).strict()` and answers `z.void()`.
- **The owner.** In step 4(a) the navigation is still in the layout and calls `setOwner` with the keys it derives today; from step 4(c) `workspace` does ([section 6](#6-workspace-navigation-workspace)). A key that named a Thread still equals its id, and `threadId` keeps its meaning. `close` and `retitle` gain no second name for the key: the chat uses `everyOwner`, `terminal` passes `threadId` (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/store.ts:142-146`), and the shell's own strip closes with `threadId` (`L/src/client/components/organisms/workspace-tabs.tsx:41`).
- **The transition inside step 4(a) (D30).** The second change of the step adds `LayoutTabsV2` to the contracts module and provides it from the store that provides v1 (`L/src/client/services/layout-tabs.ts:66`). For that time the 1.2.0 interface and request type stay in the module under the names `LegacyLayoutTabs` and `LegacyTabRequest`, typing `LayoutTabsV1`. Both ids read one owner and one set of tabs, so a tab opened through one is closed, retitled and read through the other. A v1 request keeps its meaning: its `icon` is drawn by the switch of `L/src/client/components/organisms/workspace-tabs.tsx:179-195`, which stays whole until the sixth change. In the same second change the layout's own callers move to v2: the tab strip (`L/src/client/components/organisms/workspace-tabs.tsx:15,40`) and the navigation, which opens the Thread tab with `iconTag: 'chat-thread-tab-icon'` and `openBottom: true` (`L/src/client/hooks/thread-navigation.ts:4,18-30`); the chat defines that icon element in the same change. The third, fourth and fifth changes move `files`, `git` and `terminal`, one bundle each: its manifest requires `layout.tabs/v2@^2.0.0`, its requests pass `iconTag` for an icon element it defines, and its tests' and stories' fakes take the new id. The sixth change deletes `LayoutTabsV1`, the two `Legacy` types, `TabIcon` and the switch, and the manifest's line `layout.tabs/v1@1.2.0`. [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions) lists every consumer with its lines.
- What leaves the shell with the names it knew: the constant `THREAD_PANEL_TAG` and `isThreadPanelTab` (`L/src/client/domain/workspace-tabs.ts:44-53`), replaced by `openBottom`, in the second change, in which the code that tells which Threads the window shows starts to test the tab's `iconTag` ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)); the type `TabIcon` and the two icon switches (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:57`, `L/src/client/components/organisms/workspace-tabs.tsx:179-195`, `L/src/client/components/organisms/project-panel.tsx:26-40`), replaced by `iconTag`, in the sixth; the tab kinds `diff`, `file` and `terminal` (`L/src/client/domain/workspace-tabs.ts:5-23`), which no code creates; `readsSandbox` (`:59-64`), which no source file calls; and, in step 4(c), `reconcileThreadTabs` with `services/thread-tabs.ts`, whose work the chat takes over with `retitle` and `close` and `everyOwner` ([section 10](#10-chat-points)). Between steps 4(a) and 4(c) the navigation, still in the layout, keeps opening the Thread tab itself and keeps `reconcileThreadTabs`, which finds its tabs by the `iconTag` `chat-thread-tab-icon` where it reads `icon` today (`L/src/client/domain/workspace-tabs.ts:81`).
- `open` with no owner still fails with `unavailable` and the message "No thread or project is selected." (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:72-76`). The text is the reader's and does not change.

**`layout.panel/v3`, new, 3.0.0 (U27).** It replaces `layout.panel/v2` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:240-263`), which the layout provides unchanged until the same sixth change. `select` has the input and the result of 2.0.0, and `LayoutPanelV3 = ref<LayoutPanel>('layout.panel/v3')` types the same interface.

- **One meaning differs.** Under v3 the panel starts with no view selected, where it starts on `files` (`L/src/client/stores/panel.ts:15`), and `files` calls `select('files')` through v3 when its client part activates. A composition without `files` shows no view until the reader picks one, as it does today.
- **The transition.** The second change provides v3 from the store that provides v2 (`L/src/client/services/layout-panel.ts:13-19`). The store keeps its initial `files` until the sixth change, so a caller of v2 meets what it met. The third change moves `files`, the one consumer (`bundles/features/files/manifest.json:23`, `src/client/commands.ts:10,63`), which from then on selects its view when it activates. The sixth change deletes `LayoutPanelV2`, the manifest's line `layout.panel/v2@2.0.0` and the store's initial `files`. The reader sees the Files view first after every change.
- **The items of the panel slot.** `PanelItemProps` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:251-258`) is the type of the slot's items and belongs to no contract id. It gains `iconTag?: string` in the second change, an element drawn as the view's icon, and `icon` becomes optional. `files` and `git`, the two fillers ([section 8](#8-slots)), pass `iconTag` from the changes that move them. The shell draws the element `iconTag` names and, for an item without one, the drawing `icon` names, until the sixth change deletes `icon` from the type and the switch of `L/src/client/components/organisms/project-panel.tsx:26-40`. An item with neither draws no icon, as an unknown name does today (`:37-38`).

**`layout.conversation/v1`, new, 1.0.0 (D7).** One method, moved unchanged from `layout.workspace/v1` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:182-187`):

```ts
export interface LayoutConversation {
  /** Shows (`true`) or hides the `shell.conversation.bottom` split for one item. */
  setBottomVisible(itemId: string, visible: boolean, call?: CallArg): void;
}
export const LayoutConversationV1 = ref<LayoutConversation>(
  'layout.conversation/v1',
);
```

**`layout.stack/v2`** does not change.

**Other names that leave.** The CSS variable becomes `--shell-conversation-footer-height`, set by the shell and read by the chat (`L/src/client/components/templates/conversation-frame.tsx:16`, `bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/styles.css`, `C/views/chat-view.tsx`). The Monaco rule of `L/src/client/styles.css:28-32` moves to `files` and the xterm rule (`:37-46`) to `terminal`. `L/package.json` lost `monaco-editor`, `@xterm/xterm`, `@xterm/addon-fit` and `resources` in phase 0, where the `dependencies` check found them unused ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)); it loses `runtime` (`:31`) in step 4(c), with its last import of `runtime/contracts`.

**Replaceable (U3).** The shell's interface is what `layout/contracts` exports: the four contract ids above, the slot names of [section 8](#8-slots) and their props types. A second shell is another bundle that provides the same ids and draws the same slot names; no other bundle names the tag `layout-workspace`.

### 6. Workspace navigation (`workspace`)

A new bundle, `bundles/desktop/workspace/`, with a client part and a contracts module.

- **Manifest.** `id`: `workspace`. `description`: "Choosing what the window shows: Projects, Threads and the selection." `engines.doric`: `^1.8.0`. `requires`: `hooks/v1@^1.0.0`, `ui/v1@^1.0.0`, `navigation/v1@^1.1.0`, `regions/v1@^1.0.0` and `commands/v1@^1.0.0`, the ranges the layout has for the same code (`L/manifest.json:18-22`); `runtime.records/v1@^1.0.0`; `layout.tabs/v2@^2.0.0`; and, from step 4(c) until step 4(e) removes it, `layout.conversation/v1@^1.0.0`, for the `setBottomVisible` it forwards. It lists nothing under `optional`. `provides`: `workspace.selection/v1@1.0.0`, `workspace.hooks/v1@1.0.0`, `workspace.sidebar/v1@1.0.0` and `workspace.dashboard/v1@1.0.0`, and during the transition `layout.workspace/v1@1.1.0`, `layout.sidebar/v1@1.1.0` and `layout.dashboard/v1@1.0.0` (D27). It has no `desktopScope`: every contract is a client contract.
- **Routes.** `/projects` and `/projects/:projectId`, both with `parent: '/'`, so the shell's element frames them (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/navigation.ts:22-26`). The element of `/projects/:projectId` is `workspace-project-empty`: the Project's name, "Start a thread or select one from the sidebar." and the "New thread" button. The element of `/projects` is `workspace-projects-route` and draws nothing; the page form covers it. Both exist in the layout from the seventh change of step 4(a) under the tags `layout-project-empty` and `layout-projects-route`, and step 4(c) moves them ([section 5](#5-the-shell-layout)).
- **Slots it fills.** `shell.sidebar` with `workspace-sidebar`, which draws the dashboard's sidebar on `/` and `/projects` and the Project sidebar elsewhere; `shell.sidebar.footer` with `workspace-sidebar-footer` on `/` and `/projects`; `shell.titlebar.tabs` with `workspace-project-tabs`; `shell.page` with `workspace-dashboard` on `/` and `/projects`, added and removed as the route changes.
- **Persistence.** The keys `doric.selection` and `doric.project-tabs` keep their names (U12) and are read and written by `workspace` (`L/src/client/domain/selection.ts:1`, `L/src/client/hooks/use-project-tabs.ts:16-20`).
- **The tab owner.** On every change of the route or of the selection, `workspace` calls `layout.tabs/v2.setOwner` with the key `tabOwnerOf` computes today (`L/src/client/domain/route-selection.ts:126-133`).

```ts
// bundles/desktop/workspace/src/contracts/index.ts
export interface WorkspaceSelection {
  readonly projectId?: string;
  readonly threadId?: string;
}

/** `workspace.selection/v1` (client, local): the validated selection, as ids. */
export interface WorkspaceSelectionService {
  /** The same object until the selection changes. */
  current(call?: CallArg): WorkspaceSelection;
  subscribe(listener: () => void, call?: CallArg): Disposable;
}
export const WorkspaceSelectionV1 = ref<WorkspaceSelectionService>(
  'workspace.selection/v1',
);

/** `workspace.hooks/v1`. */
export const WorkspaceHooksV1 = defineHooks('workspace.hooks/v1', {
  /** The reader asked to open a Thread beside the conversation. */
  onOpenThreadHook: defineHook({
    kind: 'observe',
    input: z.object({ projectId: z.string(), threadId: z.string() }).strict(),
  }),
});

/** `workspace.sidebar/v1`: `layout.sidebar/v1` 1.1.0, moved with its types unchanged. */
export const WorkspaceSidebarV1 = ref<WorkspaceSidebar>('workspace.sidebar/v1');
/** `workspace.dashboard/v1`: `layout.dashboard/v1` 1.0.0, moved unchanged. */
export const WorkspaceDashboardV1 = ref<WorkspaceDashboard>(
  'workspace.dashboard/v1',
);
```

- `WorkspaceSidebar` and `WorkspaceDashboard` are the interfaces `LayoutSidebar`, with its `ThreadRows` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:292-340`), and `LayoutDashboard` (`:273-289`) under new names, with no member changed.
- `current()` is a Project and a Thread the mirror describes, as `selection()` is today (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:172-177`), reduced to ids. A consumer that needs a record reads it from `runtime.records/v1`.
- **`onOpenThreadHook` (D4).** Two places open a Thread in a viewer tab today, both through `openThreadTab`, which names the chat's tag (`L/src/client/hooks/thread-navigation.ts:18-30`): the sidebar's "open in tab" (`L/src/client/components/organisms/project-sidebar.tsx:359`) and the navigation to a child Thread (`thread-navigation.ts:67-76`). From step 4(c) both execute the hook instead, and in the same step the chat observes it and opens the tab ([section 10](#10-chat-points)). With no observer nothing opens.
- **The transition (D27, U12).** In step 4(c) the layout stops providing its three navigation contracts and `workspace` provides them under their old ids: `layout.workspace/v1` with every method of 1.1.0, computed from the mirror and its selection, with `setBottomVisible` forwarding to `layout.conversation/v1`; and `layout.sidebar/v1` and `layout.dashboard/v1` from the same services that provide `workspace.sidebar/v1` and `workspace.dashboard/v1`. Step 4(d) moves the ten consumers of the three contracts, one bundle per merged change: `usage`, `mcp`, `resources`, `screen`, `threads`, `git`, `files`, `terminal`, `chat` and `settings`. Step 4(e) deletes the three providers and the contracts. `workspace` requires `layout.conversation/v1` only for those two steps.
- **Where the three old contracts are declared from step 4(c) to step 4(e).** In `bundles/desktop/workspace/src/contracts/transition.ts`, which the module's `index.ts` re-exports. It holds, under the names they have in the layout's module today: `LayoutWorkspaceV1`, `layoutWorkspaceMethods` and the interface `LayoutWorkspace` with its schemas (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:169-231`); `LayoutDashboardV1` and `layoutDashboardMethods` (`:283-289`); and `LayoutSidebarV1` and `layoutSidebarMethods` (`:331-340`). The last two refs are typed by `WorkspaceDashboard` and `WorkspaceSidebar`, which are the same interfaces. One name changes: the selection of records that `LayoutWorkspace.selection()` returns (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:194-197`) is exported as `LegacyWorkspaceSelection`, because `WorkspaceSelection` is the name of the new type of ids. No file outside the layout imports that type. The file imports `Project` and `Thread` from `runtime/contracts` by type, as the layout's module does (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:13`). It is the one file of `bundles/desktop/workspace/src/contracts` that does, and step 4(e) deletes it.
  - Three bundles import names of the moved contracts from `layout/contracts`, and each names `workspace/contracts` for them in step 4(c): `settings`, for `LayoutDashboardV1`, `LayoutWorkspaceV1` and `layoutWorkspaceMethods` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:34`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/index.ts:13`, `bundles/desktop/settings/tests/client/execution-picker.test.ts:22`, `bundles/desktop/settings/tests/support/host.ts:30`); `files`, for `LayoutWorkspaceV1` (`bundles/features/files/src/client/commands.ts:9-14`, `bundles/features/files/src/client/index.ts:20`, `bundles/features/files/src/client/tree/files-tree.tsx:25`); and `git`, for `LayoutWorkspaceV1` (`bundles/features/git/src/client/hooks/use-selection.ts:2`). An import statement that also names something that stays in `layout/contracts`, such as the tabs contract, becomes two statements. `files` and `git` require `layout.workspace/v1` and keep importing the ref as a value. `settings` lists the two contracts under `optional`, so from this step its sources import their types and declare the two refs by id (D32, [section 8](#8-slots)); its two test files import the values.
  - `settings` gains `workspace` under `dependencies`, and `files` and `git` gain it under `devDependencies`, beside their line for `layout`. The seven other consumers hold structural copies and change nothing in step 4(c). In step 4(d) each of the three takes the new names from the module it already imports.

### 7. Account (`account`)

A new bundle, `bundles/desktop/account/`, with a client part and a contracts module, and no contract of its own.

- **Manifest.** `id`: `account`. `version`: `1.0.0`. `description`: "The reader's account status and menu, and About." `engines.doric`: `^1.8.0`. `requires`: `ui/v1@^1.0.0`, `regions/v1@^1.0.0`, `commands/v1@^1.0.0`. It provides nothing and lists nothing under `optional`. It uses `doric.about/v1`, which the platform provides and no manifest lists (`L/src/client/domain/about.ts`).
- **What it draws.** The element `account-status`: the avatar button, the menu and the badge, as `L/src/client/components/organisms/account-status.tsx:36-80` draws them. It adds that element to `shell.window.actions`.
- **Slots it declares.** `account.status`, the badge over the avatar, and `account.menu`, the entries of the menu, between its header (the reader's name and "Local account") and its About line (the product name, the license and the version), where "Settings" is today (`L/src/client/components/organisms/account-status.tsx:50-74`), as data items ([section 8](#8-slots)).
- It names neither the settings command nor a route: the "Settings" entry is an item `settings` adds.

### 8. Slots

A slot is a region name (`regions/v1`, unchanged) that its owner exports as a constant from its contracts module, with a type for the `props` of its items (U10).

Nine slots are renamed and eight are new: the six of OD23, `shell.sidebar.footer` (U22) and `settings.provider.field` (D14). One keeps its name: `settings.sections`, the tenth slot the repository has today, which the concept's count of nine existing slots left out. By U10 it gains its constant, in step 6(c), when `settings` gains its contracts module; no filler's literal changes.

**Data items (D9).** The owner draws the control and runs the command; the filler gives data:

```ts
/** The `props` of an item of `account.menu` or `workspace.dashboard.footer`. */
export interface CommandItemProps {
  readonly label: string;
  /** The command the owner executes through `commands/v1` when the reader picks the entry. */
  readonly command: string;
  readonly input?: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;
}
```

- The type is declared twice, as `AccountMenuItemProps` in `account/contracts` and as `DashboardFooterItemProps` in `workspace/contracts`; neither bundle imports the other.
- The item's `tag` is the filler's icon element, drawn at 16 px before the label. `settings` defines `settings-entry-icon` and adds two items with `{ label, command: 'core.open-settings/v1', input: { defaultPath: '/settings' } }`: "Settings" to `account.menu` and "All settings" to `workspace.dashboard.footer`. `settings` lists `core.open-settings/v1` and `commands/v1` in its manifest.
- An item whose `props` fail the type is not drawn, and the owner reports it once per item id with `console.error` and the prefix `Doric:`, the way a client part reports today (`L/src/client/components/molecules/element-host.tsx:42`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/services/settings-config.ts:75`). No client part of a bundle uses `log/v1` today, and none gains it: `account` and `workspace` do not list it.

**How a bundle names a slot (RD-4 of the fifth review).**

- **The owner** declares each constant with no type annotation, `export const SHELL_PANEL = 'shell.panel';`, so that its type is the literal, and uses it as a value, in the drawing and in its tests: `<core-region name={SHELL_PANEL} />`, `useRegionItems(regions, SHELL_PANEL)`. That is what the constant is for at run time.
- **A filler** writes the name as a string literal and checks it against the owner's constant through a type-only import:

  ```ts
  import type { SHELL_PANEL } from 'layout/contracts';

  regions.add('shell.panel' satisfies typeof SHELL_PANEL, item);
  ```

  A misspelled literal fails the filler's client typecheck, which is a command of its `build` target ([section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup)). That is what the constant is for in a filler.

- **A filler never imports a slot constant as a value.** A built client part leaves `<id>/contracts` to the window (`packages/bundle/src/build/externals.ts:23`), and the window's import map has that entry only for the bundles of its generation (`app/desktop/doric/src/platform/protocol/import-map.ts:46-58`, `app/desktop/doric/src/platform/app.ts:589-606`). A value import of `account/contracts` in `resources` or in `settings` would therefore stop the whole client part of that bundle from loading in a composition without `account`, where only its one item is missing today ([section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build)). `import type` is erased from the built part, so a filler loads with or without the owner. A filler that already imports a contract of the owner as a value, as `files` imports `LayoutTabsV2`, lists that contract under `requires` and is quarantined without the owner in any case.
- **The owner is a line of the filler's `package.json`,** which the type import needs for `tsc` and for Biome's rule. The table gives each line; "has" means the line exists at this commit. A bundle with a host part lists the owner under `devDependencies`, a bundle with a client part alone under `dependencies` ([section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup)). No line closes a cycle of packages: the graph of the `package.json` files was checked after each step, with every line this spec adds or removes.

  | Step | Filler         | Constants it checks                                                                 | Line in the filler's `package.json`                                                                                                               |
  | ---- | -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | 4(a) | `chat`         | `SHELL_CONVERSATION_FOOTER`                                                         | `layout`, new, under `dependencies`                                                                                                               |
  | 4(a) | `usage`        | `SHELL_CONVERSATION_FOOTER`                                                         | `layout`, new, under `devDependencies`                                                                                                            |
  | 4(a) | `settings`     | `SHELL_CONVERSATION_FOOTER`, until the picker leaves it in step 6(c)                | `layout`, has (`bundles/desktop/settings/package.json:15`); the line leaves in step 6(c), with the bundle's last import of `layout/contracts`     |
  | 4(a) | `files`        | `SHELL_PANEL`                                                                       | `layout`, has (`bundles/features/files/package.json:28`)                                                                                          |
  | 4(a) | `git`          | `SHELL_PANEL`, `SHELL_PANEL_FOOTER`                                                 | `layout`, has (`bundles/features/git/package.json:28`)                                                                                            |
  | 4(a) | `terminal`     | None until step 7(c); then `SHELL_HEADER_ACTIONS`                                   | None until step 7(c); then `layout`, under `dependencies` ([section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs))                                                  |
  | 4(b) | `account`      | `SHELL_WINDOW_ACTIONS`                                                              | `layout`, in the new file                                                                                                                         |
  | 4(b) | `resources`    | `ACCOUNT_STATUS`                                                                    | `account`, new, under `devDependencies`                                                                                                           |
  | 4(b) | `settings`     | `ACCOUNT_MENU`                                                                      | `account`, new, under `dependencies`                                                                                                              |
  | 4(c) | `workspace`    | `SHELL_SIDEBAR`, `SHELL_SIDEBAR_FOOTER`, `SHELL_TITLEBAR_TABS`, `SHELL_PAGE`        | `layout`, in the new file                                                                                                                         |
  | 4(c) | `settings`     | `WORKSPACE_DASHBOARD_NAV`, `WORKSPACE_DASHBOARD_PAGE`, `WORKSPACE_DASHBOARD_FOOTER` | `workspace`, new, under `dependencies`; the same line carries its imports of the moved contracts ([section 6](#6-workspace-navigation-workspace)) |
  | 6(c) | `git`          | `SETTINGS_SECTIONS`                                                                 | `settings`, under `devDependencies`, the line [section 11](#11-settings-and-providers) adds in that step                                          |
  | 6(a) | `claude-login` | `SETTINGS_PROVIDER_FIELD`                                                           | `settings`, under `dependencies` ([section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup))                                                                      |

- **The one exception, until step 7(c).** From step 4(a) `terminal` writes `'shell.header.actions'` with no check. `layout` lists `runtime` until step 4(c) and `runtime` lists `terminal` until step 7(c), so a line for `layout` in the terminal's `package.json` would close the cycle `runtime` → `terminal` → `layout` → `runtime` in step 4(a). Step 7(c), which gives the terminal that line for its copy of `layout.tabs/v2`, adds the check. Until then review alone holds that one literal.
- **Tests and stories** may name a slot either way. A test or story file may import `<id>/contracts` as a value, because Node and the story build resolve the package; none has to.
- **The search for an unchecked literal.** `git grep -nE -A1 "(\"|')(shell\.[a-z.]+|workspace\.dashboard\.[a-z]+|account\.(status|menu)|settings\.(sections|provider\.field))(\"|')" -- 'bundles/*/*/src/client/*'` lists every slot name written as a string in a client part, with the line after it. A match is checked when `satisfies typeof` and the slot's constant follow the literal, on its line or the next. At this commit the search finds the four lines that name `settings.sections` and no other, because the other names do not exist yet. Unchecked matches are allowed at three points only: after step 4(c), the terminal's line and those four; after step 6(c), the terminal's line; after step 7(c), none (AC4.9, AC6.4, AC7.3).

**The same rule for a contract a client part lists under `optional` (D32).** A client part imports a value from `<id>/contracts` only when its manifest lists a contract of that bundle under `requires`; without the provider such a part is quarantined in any case. For a contract under `optional` it imports the contract's types with `import type` and declares the ref itself by the id, `ref<Api>('<id>')`. The repository records this for a module the import map lacks (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:776`), and the structural copies of the terminal and of the chat declare their refs that way today (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/peers.ts:24`, `C/api/workspace-contract.ts:19`). The `declared` test finds the literal id, so the manifest line stays necessary. Where this spec adds or moves such an import:

| Step | Client part        | Contract under `optional`                                                                                                            | What it imports                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---- | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 4(c) | `settings`         | `layout.workspace/v1` and `layout.dashboard/v1`; from its change of step 4(d), `workspace.selection/v1` and `workspace.dashboard/v1` | The types from `workspace/contracts`, and the refs by id, where it imports `LayoutWorkspaceV1` and `LayoutDashboardV1` as values from `layout/contracts` today. Its tests keep importing the refs and `layoutWorkspaceMethods` as values, now from `workspace/contracts`. |
| 5(b) | `files`, `default` | `chat.views/v1`                                                                                                                      | The types `ChatViews`, `ToolRendererSpec` and `ChatToolView` from `chat/contracts`, and the ref by id.                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 6(c) | `chat`             | `settings.models/v1`                                                                                                                 | The types of [section 11](#11-settings-and-providers) from `settings/contracts`, and the ref by id.                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 7(c) | `terminal`         | `chat.views/v1`                                                                                                                      | The same as `files`, when its copy becomes an import.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

- A bundle that requires the provider imports the ref as a value, as today: `files` and `git` for the navigation and the tabs contracts, `git` for `settings.config/v1`, and `terminal` for `runtime.records/v1`, the contracts of `workspace` and `layout.tabs/v2`.
- Two value imports with no `requires` line behind them exist today and stay: `git` imports helper functions of `files/contracts` (`bundles/features/git/src/client/changes/store.ts:20`), and `settings` imports the effort rules of `runtime/contracts` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/domain/config.ts:14,23`). Neither name is a ref, so neither can become a type import. Phase 9 therefore changes nothing for `git` in this respect; R19 names both.
- This spec read the code of the import map and did not run a window without a bundle. If a value import under `optional` does load there, the rule has cost nothing.

**The rename.** Each of the nine existing slots is renamed in one merged change that edits every file that names the slot: the owner's drawing, each filler, each test and story, and each README.

- **The search.** `git grep -nE "layout\.(header|footer|main\.bottom|overlay|panel(\.footer)?|dashboard\.(nav|page)|account\.status)([^/A-Za-z0-9_-]|$)" -- bundles app` finds a name however it is written: between single quotes, as a JSX attribute (`<Region name="layout.header" />`, `L/src/client/views/workspace-view.tsx:274,296,319,321,360`), in a comment, in a test's title and in a README. The last group keeps the contract ids out: `layout.panel/v2` and `layout.dashboard/v1` continue with `/`, and the toast id `layout-panel-read` has no dot. The step is accepted when the search finds nothing.
- **What it finds at this commit:** 136 lines in 56 files, and each change edits those that name its slot.
  - _Sources (27):_ `L/src/contracts/index.ts`; under `L/src/client/`, `views/workspace-view.tsx`, `components/organisms/{account-status,dashboard,dashboard-sidebar,project-panel,project-tabs}.tsx`, `components/templates/{conversation-frame,conversation-terminals,workspace-layout}.tsx`, `domain/dashboard.ts`, `services/layout-workspace.ts` and `stores/{bottom,dashboard,panel}.ts`; under `C/`, `index.ts`, `elements/chat-elements.tsx` and `views/chat-composer.tsx`; under `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/`, `index.ts`, `elements.ts` and `views/{dashboard-providers,settings-page}.tsx`; and the `src/client/index.ts` of `files`, `git`, `resources`, `terminal` and `usage`.
  - _Tests (13):_ `L/tests/client/{shell,dashboard-shell}.test.ts` and `L/tests/{dashboard,dashboard-service,panel-service}.test.ts`; `bundles/desktop/chat/tests/client/{composer,chat-view}.test.ts`; `bundles/desktop/settings/tests/client/{dashboard-providers,execution-picker}.test.ts`; `bundles/features/files/tests/manifest.test.ts`; `bundles/features/git/tests/{regions,manifest}.test.ts`; `bundles/features/resources/tests/client/connection-badge.test.ts`.
  - _Stories (9):_ `L/stories/components/organisms/{account-status,project-panel}.stories.tsx` and `L/stories/support/{shell.tsx,fake-host.ts,layout-host.tsx,story-elements.ts}`; `bundles/desktop/chat/stories/views/chat-composer.stories.tsx`; `bundles/features/git/stories/support/footer.tsx`; `bundles/features/usage/stories/support/footer.tsx`.
  - _READMEs (7):_ those of `chat`, `layout`, `settings`, `files`, `git`, `terminal` and `usage`.
- `packages/bundle/tests/ui-platform.test.ts` uses `layout.footer` as a sample name of the platform's own test and keeps it; the search does not read `packages`.

They stay in the layout until step 4(c), which deletes them there and writes them in the chat ([section 10](#10-chat-points)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

- **Files whose destination their name does not show.** `domain/connection.ts` and `stores/connection.ts` go to the runtime, which publishes `connection()`. `hooks/use-execution-time.ts` goes to `workspace`, where the tree draws the elapsed badge. `components/organisms/project-panel.tsx` stays: it is the side panel's frame. `api/host.ts` goes to the runtime, where its two references become the runtime's own. `api/thread-views.ts` goes to the runtime with the Project watch whose `followStream` it uses, and is reached through `view` of the mirror; the two other files named `thread-views.ts` do not go with it.
- **The history buttons go to `workspace`.** They are drawn inside the sidebar's header (`components/organisms/sidebar-header.tsx:91`), which is navigation and moves, and they call only `navigation/v1`. Moving them keeps their place without one more slot.
- **The archive goes to `workspace` with the Thread tree (D33, Q8).** The sidebar draws the Chat and Archived tabs between its search field and its Thread region, and the tree of the open tab inside that region (`components/organisms/project-sidebar.tsx:259,396,464-466`). All of it is drawn by the element `workspace-sidebar`. `components/molecules/sidebar-tabs.tsx`, `components/organisms/archived-threads.tsx` and `domain/thread-archive.ts` move, and so do the files the archive changed, each of which already had `workspace` as its destination. The open tab stays state of the sidebar's component. The shell gains no slot and no contract for it, and `layout.sidebar/v1`, which becomes `workspace.sidebar/v1`, does not change: the archive added nothing to `L/src/contracts`.
- **Which Threads the window shows, until step 4(c) (D33, Q7).** The layout's activation starts `watchThreadViews` (`index.ts:55`, `services/thread-views.ts:25-56`). On every change of `navigation/v1`, of the workspace store and of the tabs store (`services/thread-views.ts:43-45`) it computes `threadsOnScreen` (`domain/thread-views.ts:26-42`): the Thread of the Thread route, and the Thread of the tab owner's selected tab when that tab is a Thread tab.
  - _Phase 3._ `api/thread-views.ts` leaves with the Project watch. `watchThreadViews` loses its `connection` argument and its reference to `runtime.threads/v1`, and reads `runtime.records/v1` from the activation's services; while no bundle provides the mirror it holds no view. On every change it takes one `view` for each Thread of the new set and then disposes the views it took for the set before, in that order, so a Thread in both sets keeps its stream. Its cleanup disposes the views it holds. Its three sources and `threadsOnScreen` do not change.
  - _Step 4(a), second change._ `isThreadPanelTab` leaves the shell ([section 5](#5-the-shell-layout)). From that change `threadsOnScreen` tells a Thread tab by its `iconTag`, `chat-thread-tab-icon`, as `reconcileThreadTabs` does from the same change, and reads `props.threadId` as today (`domain/thread-views.ts:37-40`).
  - _Step 4(c)._ The two files, and the call in `index.ts`, are deleted from the layout, and the chat's own take their place ([section 10](#10-chat-points)). `workspace` gets none: which tab shows a Thread is the chat's knowledge (D4).
- **No copies (D29).** Three bundles draw other bundles' slot items after the split: `layout`, `workspace` and `account`. The two files they need become one platform export in step 4(a), and no bundle keeps a copy:

  ```tsx
  // packages/desktop/src/element-host.tsx, exported as `desktop/ui/element-host`
  /** Mounts the element `ui` creates for `tag` while mounted and disposes it after; a failed creation draws a compact notice. */
  export function ElementHost(props: {
    /** The caller's `ui/v1`. */
    readonly ui: Ui;
    readonly tag: string;
    readonly props?: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;
    readonly className?: string;
  }): ReactElement;

  /** A region's items as `regions` sorts them; the same array until the region changes. */
  export const useRegionItems: (
    regions: Regions,
    region: string,
  ) => readonly RegionEntry[];
  ```

  - The bodies are those of `L/src/client/components/molecules/element-host.tsx:16-76` and `L/src/client/hooks/use-region-items.ts:13-28`, with two changes: the services they read through `layoutServices()` (`L/src/client/components/molecules/element-host.tsx:37`, `L/src/client/hooks/use-region-items.ts:16-25`) become the argument `ui` and the argument `regions`, of the types `Ui` and `Regions` of `bundle/contracts`, which `desktop` already depends on (`packages/desktop/package.json:141`); and the notice's catalog entry `element_failed` ("View failed to open", `L/src/client/domain/error-catalog.ts:26`) becomes a constant of the component, so no caller passes a catalog.
  - `packages/desktop/package.json` gains the export `./ui/element-host`, written like `./ui/error-notice` (`:62-67`). The story `L/stories/components/molecules/element-host.stories.tsx` moves to `packages/desktop/stories/`. T4.1 is the platform's test.
  - Callers pass their own services: `layout` in `components/organisms/project-panel.tsx:16` and in the frame; `workspace` in the files that import the two today (`components/molecules/thread-branches.tsx:39`, `components/organisms/dashboard-sidebar.tsx:32`, `components/organisms/dashboard.tsx:29,32` and the header half of `project-sidebar.tsx:52`); `account` for the icon elements of `account.menu` and the items of `account.status`.
  - `domain/error-catalog.ts` goes to `workspace` without its `element_failed` entry.

- **`utility/move-item.ts` is not copied.** Its two importers, `domain/workspace-tabs.ts:2` and `domain/panel-order.ts:1`, stay in `layout`, and nothing that moves to `workspace` uses it. The file is byte for byte the platform's `moveItem` (`packages/desktop/src/panels/move-item.ts`, exported by `desktop/ui/panels`); that existing duplicate is outside this spec.

**The 45 test files** follow their subjects:
