| Deleted        | `thread-tabs.test.ts`: its cases are rewritten in the chat (T4.10). `thread-views.test.ts`, in step 4(c): its cases of `threadsOnScreen` (`:82-141`), the ones left after phase 3, are rewritten in the chat (T4.13).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

A moved test changes only its imports, contract ids, slot names and element tags (G2). Where a test of the layout pinned records and selection together, it becomes two tests, one in each owner. The 31 story files under `L/stories/` follow the component they show.

**Test and story support (D31).** Ten files under `L/stories/support/` (1,911 lines) and `L/tests/client/support/render-workspace.ts` (214) bind the whole layout to a fake host. Eleven of the layout's thirteen client tests render through them (`L/tests/client/*.test.ts`; `about.test.ts` and `footer-styles.test.ts` do not), and 12 of its 21 stories bind a host through them. After the split three bundles draw what one drew, and a test or story file may import another bundle only through `<id>/contracts` or `<id>/testing` (rule (2) of `imports`).

- **Each bundle is tested alone.** A test of `layout` renders the frame with stand-ins in the slots another bundle fills; a test of `workspace` or of `account` mounts that bundle's own elements with no frame around them. An element created through `ui/v1` is its own root and reads its services, so it mounts in a bare container. The composed window is what the desktop end-to-end suite runs ([Validation](#validation)).
- **`runtime/testing`, new in phase 3.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/testing.ts`, exported as `./testing` the way `postgres` exports its own (`bundles/foundation/postgres/package.json:20-25`), and emitted by the bundle's `tsconfig.json`: it imports only its own contracts module and `bundle/contracts`, and no file of `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/client`.

  ```ts
  /** The reads a fake can hold back or fail. */
  export type RecordsOperation =
    | 'projects'
    | 'threads'
    | 'activity'
    | 'findThread';

  export interface RecordsFakeOptions {
    readonly projects?: readonly Project[];
    /** The Threads of every Project. A Project is described from the first `threads(projectId)` call on. */
    readonly threads?: readonly Thread[];
    readonly activity?: readonly ProjectActivity[];
    /** `connected` when absent. */
    readonly connection?: 'connected' | 'disconnected';
    /** Reads that answer `loading` for good; `activity` and `findThread` then never settle. */
    readonly pending?: readonly RecordsOperation[];
    /** Reads that answer `failed`, and promises that reject with an `Error` that has the property `code`. Both carry the code `unavailable` and the message "The host is unreachable." */
    readonly failing?: readonly RecordsOperation[];
  }

  /** `runtime.records/v1` in memory, as a plain object: no host, no watch and no timer. */
  export const createRecordsApi: (
    options?: RecordsFakeOptions,
  ) => RuntimeRecords;

  /** The same object behind a service definition, for `createTestServices({ provide })`. */
  export const createRecordsFake: (
    options?: RecordsFakeOptions,
  ) => ServiceDefinition<'runtime.records/v1', typeof runtimeRecordsMethods>;
  ```

  - A read that is neither pending nor failing answers `ready` at once, from the options. A write changes the fake's records as the host would (a deleted Thread takes its children with it), calls the subscribers and resolves with the record. `archiveThread` marks the target and its descendants that are not archived, busy or pinned, and answers them with a new batch; `restoreThread` clears the mark of the Threads of the batch; `setThreadPinned` sets the field. `follow` and `view` each return a `Disposable` and do nothing, and `reload` calls the subscribers.
  - The options are those of the layout's story host for the same things (`L/stories/support/fake-host.ts:44-57`), and its write rules are that file's (`:299-558`), so a story or a test that seeds Projects and Threads today seeds the fake with the same values.
  - A test or a story passes the definition to whatever provides its services, `createTestServices({ provide: [...] })` (`packages/bundle/src/testing/index.ts:20-27`), or puts the object of `createRecordsApi` into its own story host under the contract's id.
  - A test that needs one call to wait or to fail replaces that member of the object with a function of its own, as `L/tests/client/workspace-errors.test.ts:104-110` replaces a member of the fake host's `runtime/v1` today. A failed write is then a rejection the test builds itself, with the code and the message of its case: the mirror rejects with a `ClientError`, which a surface reads by its fields ([section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime)). The fake's own rejections are plain `Error` objects with a `code`, so `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/testing.ts` imports nothing of `desktop`.
  - Who uses it: the layout's support from phase 3; `workspace` from step 4(c); and each consumer of `runtime.records/v1` from the change in which it first reads the contract (`chat` in step 4(a), the others in step 4(d)), except `terminal`. A line for `runtime` in the terminal's `package.json` would close a cycle of packages until step 7(c), because `runtime` lists `terminal` until then (`bundles/runtime/runtime/package.json:20`). From its change of step 4(d) the terminal's tests and its story fake `runtime.records/v1` as they fake `layout.workspace/v1` today, with an object literal that holds the members its structural copy declares (`bundles/features/terminal/tests/client/rows-selection.test.ts:128`, `bundles/features/terminal/stories/elements.stories.tsx:37`). Step 7(c), which gives the terminal its line for `runtime`, replaces those literals with `createRecordsApi`. The mirror itself is tested in the runtime against a fake of `runtime/v1` and `runtime.threads/v1` (T3.1 to T3.7 and T3.9), and T3.8 runs the fake.

- **No `layout/testing`.** The consumers of the shell's contracts fake them in their own support files today, in 34 test and story files outside the layout (for example `bundles/features/files/tests/open-file.test.ts:19-34`, `bundles/features/git/stories/support/git-harness.tsx`), and each bundle with stories has a story host of its own (`bundles/features/files/stories/support/services.ts`, `bundles/features/terminal/stories/support/services.ts`). Those fakes are a few methods each and stay where they are. `workspace` and `account` get story hosts of their own in the same way, written from the layout's; D29 is about source files and does not reach test support.

- The three render helpers return what `renderWorkspace` returns today (`L/tests/client/support/render-workspace.ts:13-37`): `services`, `regionItems`, `executed`, `root`, `errors`, `remount` and `cleanup`. A case that moves keeps its body and its assertions and changes the helper it calls.
- From the seventh change of step 4(a) the frame draws the navigation through the five new slots while the navigation is still in the layout. For that time the layout's fake `ui/v1` creates the element a tag was defined with, and a placeholder for a tag nobody defined, where it answers a placeholder for every tag but the terminal rows today (`fake-host.ts:607-619`); the layout's own activation defines the navigation's elements, so the whole-window tests keep rendering them. Step 4(c) takes the rule into the three story hosts unchanged.
- `L/tests/client/fake-host.test.ts` stays and tests the layout's host; its cases about Projects and Threads go to the runtime as T3.8.
- **Stories and their harness (CB-3 of the fifth review).** A story is previewed inside the nearest `.storybook/preview.tsx` above it (`bundles/features/papercraft/src/host/harness.ts:10-16`), which loads the sheet and sets the background; without one a story is drawn with no theme. `account` and `workspace` each get the file in the step that creates the bundle. It is the file of `bundles/features/mcp/.storybook/preview.tsx`, which imports `desktop/ui/theme.css` alone and wraps a story in `bg-background p-6 text-foreground`, as fits a bundle that ships no sheet. A bundle that later ships a `styles.css` adds the import, as the layout's file has it (`L/.storybook/preview.tsx:9-10`). The story files keep the paths they have under `L/stories/`: `components/organisms/account-status.stories.tsx` goes to `bundles/desktop/account/stories/` in step 4(b), and the stories of the files and halves that move to `workspace` go to `bundles/desktop/workspace/stories/` in step 4(c). The `tsconfig.client.json` of both bundles is the layout's and reads `stories/**` and `.storybook/**` already (`L/tsconfig.client.json:14-20`).

**Where the classes of the two new bundles come from.** The layout emits no utility classes: its sheet holds its own rules, and the classes its files use are emitted by the renderer's sheet, which scans every bundle's client sources (`app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css:19`), and by the sheets of seven bundles, which name the client directories of `layout`, `chat` and `settings` so that each emits the whole sorted set and no later sheet moves a shared class behind a responsive variant (`bundles/features/files/src/client/styles.css:13-20`; WP-10N, `docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:991`; `bundles/features/git/tests/styles-cascade.test.ts`). That is a recorded decision, and U8 keeps it.

- `workspace` and `account` emit no utilities either, and ship no `styles.css` unless a rule of their own needs one. Files that move out of `L/src/client` take their classes with them, so each of the seven sheets (`files`, `git`, `openui`, `resources`, `terminal`, `threads` and `usage`) gains the line `@source '../../../../desktop/account/src/client';` in step 4(b) and `@source '../../../../desktop/workspace/src/client';` in step 4(c), beside the three it has. The renderer's glob already covers both directories.
- The client parts of `runtime`, `default` and `claude-login` get no line and no sheet. The first draws nothing. The other two take markup the chat's and the settings' own sheets emit today, the `load_skill` block and the sign-in row (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/components/organisms/settings-provider.tsx:291-330`), and neither uses a responsive variant; their classes come from the renderer's sheet.
- A desktop app built before one of these steps holds the moved classes already, because it scanned them where they were. A class that is new in a moved file is missing in such an app until it is updated, which is the rule for every bundle today (`docs/designs/sidebar-alignment.md:948`).
- Phase 13 adds this to the list "Kept decisions" ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)), with the command `git grep -n "@source '../../../../desktop/" -- bundles`.

### 10. Chat points

**`chat.views/v1`, 1.1.0 → 1.2.0.** Two methods are added to `ChatViews` (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/contracts/index.ts:136-174`), beside `registerRenderer` and `registerFenceRenderer` and with their behavior (D10, D11):

```ts
/** 1.2.0: how the calls of one tool are drawn. */
export interface ToolRendererSpec {
  /** The tool's exact name. A second registration of a name is `registration_conflict`. */
  readonly toolName: string;
  /** The element that draws a call, created through `ui/v1`. Absent, the chat's generic row draws it. */
  readonly elementTag?: string;
  /** `true`: the call is a block of its own, outside the activity group. */
  readonly standalone?: boolean;
  /** `true`: the call's block is open until the reader closes it. */
  readonly openByDefault?: boolean;
}

/** The `view` property the chat sets on a tool element, again on every change. */
export interface ChatToolView {
  readonly projectId: string;
  readonly threadId: string;
  readonly promptId: string;
  /** Stable for the call's life and unique in the Thread. */
  readonly key: string;
  readonly name: string;
  readonly args: string;
  readonly result?: string;
  readonly error?: {
    readonly code: string;
    readonly message: string;
    readonly provider?: string;
    readonly status?: number;
    readonly retryable?: boolean;
  };
  readonly status: 'running' | 'finished' | 'failed';
  /** The reader's choice of open or closed; `null` while the reader has not chosen. */
  readonly chosen: boolean | null;
  /** Whether the caret rests on the block. */
  readonly focused: boolean;
}

/** 1.2.0: which element draws the prompts of one input source. */
export interface InputRendererSpec {
  /** An input source kind other than `user`; for the kind `bundle`, `bundle:<bundle id>`. */
  readonly source: string;
  readonly elementTag: string;
}

/** The `view` property the chat sets on an input element. */
export interface ChatInputView {
  readonly projectId: string;
  readonly threadId: string;
  readonly promptId: string;
  readonly source: {
    readonly kind: string;
    readonly threadId?: string;
    readonly terminalId?: string;
    readonly bundleId?: string;
    readonly reason?: string;
  };
  readonly text: string;
  readonly chosen: boolean | null;
  readonly focused: boolean;
}

export interface ChatViews {
  // registerProjector, registerRenderer, registerFenceRenderer, watchFences: unchanged.
  registerToolRenderer(spec: ToolRendererSpec, call?: CallArg): Disposable;
  registerInputRenderer(spec: InputRendererSpec, call?: CallArg): Disposable;
}
```

- **The element answers with one DOM event,** `chat-block-chosen`, a bubbling and composed `CustomEvent<{ chosen: boolean }>`, when the reader opens or closes its block. The chat keeps the choice and sets `view` again. This replaces the function `onChosenChange` the built-in drawings receive (`C/components/molecules/tool-item.tsx:24`). The error has the fields of `ErrorInfo` (`packages/desktop/src/reads/errors.ts:32-42`).
- **Failures of a registration.** `registration_conflict` for a key that is taken; `invalid_input` for a spec that fails its schema, for an `elementTag` that `ui/v1` has not defined, and for the source `user`. Nothing is registered then. These are the rules of `registerFenceRenderer` (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/contracts/index.ts:140-149`).
- **Which registration wins.** The first. Client parts activate in a fixed order, by `requires` and then by bundle id (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/catalog.ts:720-745`), so in one composition the same bundle wins on every start. The later registrant's call throws `registration_conflict`. A registrant makes each registration in a `whenAvailable` callback of its own and does not catch, so one refused registration leaves its others in place, and the platform records the failure as the diagnostic `when_available_failed` of that bundle (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/services.ts:245-251,279-281`). No registrant needs `log/v1` for it. The chat does not check that a registrant owns the tool it names, and cannot: a tool need not be in `tools/v1` (`load_skill` is the executor's own, `bundles/runtime/default/src/host/executor.ts:203-218`). Which bundles a composition enables is the operator's choice, as for the provider field ([section 11](#11-settings-and-providers)).
- **Arrival and departure.** A registration that arrives or is disposed while a transcript is shown rebuilds that transcript at once.
- **Without a registration,** a tool call is drawn by the chat's generic row inside the activity group and closed by default, and a prompt of a source other than `user` by the drawing the chat has for it today, or as its plain text when it has none.
- **The four lists go.** The drawing switch (`C/components/molecules/tool-item.tsx:44-80`, `C/components/organisms/conversation/plugins/insert-thread-turn-nodes.tsx:221`), the open-by-default list (`C/domain/collapsible.ts:69`), the list of tools outside the activity group (`C/domain/projector.ts:451-456`) and the list of tools that write the sandbox (`:1241`) are replaced by lookups in the registry, the last by D12 below.

**Who registers what** (phase 5):

| Tool or source    | Registrant                      | Spec                                                                                      | What moves from the chat                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `terminal`        | `terminal`                      | `{ elementTag: 'terminal-tool-call' }`                                                    | `C/components/molecules/terminal-tool-item.tsx`, `C/domain/terminal-tool.ts`; the live output it reads comes from the terminal's own store (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/store.ts`), and `C/stores/live-terminals.ts` (308 lines), `C/hooks/use-live-terminal.ts` and `C/api/terminals-contract.ts` are deleted. |
| source `terminal` | `terminal`                      | `{ elementTag: 'terminal-result' }`                                                       | `C/components/organisms/conversation/nodes/terminal-result-node.tsx` (213 lines) and `C/domain/terminal-result.ts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `edit`, `write`   | `files`                         | `{ elementTag: 'files-edit-call', standalone: true, openByDefault: true }`, once per name | `C/components/molecules/edit-tool-item.tsx`, `C/domain/edit-tool.ts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `view-image`      | `files`                         | `{ openByDefault: true }`                                                                 | Nothing: it has no drawing of its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `thread-spawn`    | `threads`                       | `{ elementTag: 'threads-spawn-call', standalone: true }`                                  | `C/components/organisms/conversation/nodes/thread-spawn-node.tsx` (334 lines), `C/domain/thread-spawn.ts`. It reads the child Thread from `runtime.records/v1`.                                                                                                                                                           |
| `load_skill`      | `default`, in a new client part | `{ elementTag: 'default-skill-call', standalone: true }`                                  | `C/components/molecules/skill-tool-item.tsx`, `C/domain/skill-load.ts`. The label of a skill whose name has the form `<source>/<group>/<name>` reads "`<source>` · `<group>`", which gives "mcp · server" (U15).                                                                                                          |

- Each registrant lists `chat.views/v1` under `optional` and registers through `whenAvailable`, as `threads` does today (`bundles/features/threads/src/client/index.ts:28-43`). The line carries no range, as no `optional` line can (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/manifest.ts:160`); against a chat older than 1.2.0 the method is absent, the call throws, and the platform records it as above.
- **How each registrant reaches the types.** `files` and `default` import the types `ChatViews`, `ToolRendererSpec` and `ChatToolView` from `chat/contracts` with `import type`, declare the ref by its id, `ref<ChatViews>('chat.views/v1')`, because they list the contract under `optional` (D32, [section 8](#8-slots)), and list `chat` under `dependencies`: no package the chat depends on depends on either. `threads` extends the copy it has (`bundles/features/threads/src/client/chat-contract.ts:59`). `terminal` adds the two methods and the two view types to its copies in `bundles/features/terminal/src/client/peers.ts`, because the chat depends on `runtime`, which depends on `terminal` until step 7(c); that step turns the copy into an import with the terminal's others ([section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs)).
- **The shared block.** The four drawings are built on `CollapsibleBlock` and the open/closed rule of `C/domain/collapsible.ts`. Both move to the platform package as `desktop/ui/collapsible-block` (`packages/desktop/src/collapsible-block.tsx`, with its entry in `packages/desktop/package.json` and in `app/desktop/doric-platform/tests/import-map.test.mjs`), the way `desktop/ui/error-notice` was added (commit `530f2717`). The chat keeps `toolOpenness` and the other rules that name its own blocks.
- **The stories of what moves (CB-3 of the fifth review).** The chat's client config typechecks its stories (`bundles/desktop/chat/tsconfig.client.json:14-20`), so a story file that imports something that leaves the chat moves, or is edited, in the same change.

It follows the conversation's node list (`:344-353`): it loses `ThreadSpawnNode`, its import and its branch in the change of `threads`, and `TerminalResultNode`, its branch and the live terminal's Thread context in the change of `terminal`. |

  - A moved story imports its component by a relative path in its new bundle. The helpers the four moved stories share with the chat's other stories, `WithChosen` (`bundles/desktop/chat/stories/support/chosen.tsx`, 22 lines) and the fixtures they read from `bundles/desktop/chat/stories/support/fixtures.ts`, are copied into the `stories/support/` of the bundle that needs them. Story support is not source, and D29 does not reach it ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)).
  - The chat's other stories stay. `bundles/desktop/chat/stories/components/organisms/conversation/transcript.stories.tsx:180,295,1074` logs calls of the moved tools; the chat's story host registers no drawing, so each such call is drawn by the chat's generic row from the change that moves its drawing.

- **The chat's Thread tabs** (step 4(c); the icon element in step 4(a)). The chat observes `workspace.hooks/v1.onOpenThreadHook` and opens `{ id: 'thread:<id>', tag: 'chat-thread-panel', iconTag: 'chat-thread-tab-icon', openBottom: true, props: { projectId, threadId } }` through `layout.tabs/v2`. It subscribes to `runtime.records/v1` and, for every Thread tab it opened, calls `retitle` with `everyOwner` when the Thread's name changes and `close` with `everyOwner` when the Thread is gone. The rule is the one `reconcileThreadTabs` has today (`L/src/client/domain/workspace-tabs.ts:74-103`): a tab whose Project is not described, or whose `threads(projectId)` answers `loading`, is left as it is; a tab whose Thread is missing from the described list is closed; any other tab takes the Thread's name. The chat follows no Project, so a tab of a Project that is no longer followed keeps its title and stays open until `workspace` follows that Project again, as today ([section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime)). `chat-thread-tab-icon` draws the mark the tab wears today (`L/src/client/components/organisms/workspace-tabs.tsx:187-197`). It needs three facts of the Thread, and the chat has all three without `domain/thread-kind.ts`, which goes to `workspace` and is not copied: an empty name draws the dashed bubble; a Thread without `parentThreadId` the bubble; a Thread for which the chat's own `isAgentThread` is true (`C/domain/workspace.ts:51-54`) the bot; any other Thread the bubble with a plus. The Thread is read from `runtime.records/v1` by the `threadId` of the tab's props; while the mirror does not describe it, the element draws the bubble, as the tab does today for a Thread the tree does not hold. T4.10 covers the four marks.
- **Which Threads the window shows** (step 4(c); D33, Q7). The chat takes over what the layout's `watchThreadViews` does until then ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)), with two new files.

  ```ts
  // bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/domain/thread-views.ts
  /** The Threads this window shows: the Thread of the Thread route, and the Thread of the selected viewer tab when the chat opened that tab. At most two ids. Pure. */
  export const threadsOnScreen: (
    match: RouteMatch | undefined,
    tab: CurrentTab | undefined,
  ) => ReadonlySet<string>;

  // bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/services/thread-views.ts
  /** Holds a view of every Thread the window shows; returns the cleanup. */
  export const watchThreadViews: (services: Services) => () => void;
  ```

  - _The route's Thread_ is `match.params.threadId` when `match.pattern` is the chat's own `THREAD_ROUTE` (`C/domain/thread-route.ts:2`).
  - _The tab's Thread._ `tab` is what `layout.tabs/v2.current()` answers, the selected tab of the active owner, of the type `CurrentTab` of `layout/contracts` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:59-64`). It counts when its `props.threadId` is a string and its `id` is `thread:` followed by that string, which is the id the chat gives the tabs it opens (above). The chat tests its own id, where the layout tests the tab's element today (`L/src/client/domain/thread-views.ts:37`).
  - _The watch._ `watchThreadViews` subscribes to `navigation/v1`, to `layout.tabs/v2` and to `runtime.records/v1`. At once and on every change it takes one `view` of the mirror for each Thread of `threadsOnScreen(navigation.current(), tabs.current())`, and then disposes the views it took for the set before, in that order, so a Thread in both sets keeps its stream ([section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime)). The chat's `activate` starts it where it starts the reconciliation of its tabs, and its cleanup disposes every view it holds.
  - _Why the set is the one the layout computes today._ The active owner is the key `workspace` gives `setOwner`, which is the key `tabOwnerOf` computes (`L/src/client/domain/route-selection.ts:126-133`), so `current()` is the tab `threadsOnScreen` finds today (`L/src/client/domain/thread-views.ts:34-36`). On a route with no owner `current()` answers nothing, as today. The subscription to the mirror stands in for the layout's subscription to its workspace store: it fires when a record changes, so a view whose stream ended with a failure is taken again at the next change of a record, of the route or of the tabs.
  - _Without the chat_ no bundle holds a view, and nothing draws a Thread either. The archiver then follows its other rules alone ([docs/designs/thread-archive.md](thread-archive.md), section 2).
  - T4.13 covers the set and the watch.

**The refresh after a tool (D12, U27).** `chat.hooks/v2`, new, 2.0.0, replaces `chat.hooks/v1` (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/contracts/index.ts:212`). Its five hooks have the names, kinds and inputs of 1.1.0. One differs in when it fires, and that is why the id is new: `onSandboxActivityHook` fires when the chat's store starts to hold a Thread, when the working directory of a Thread it holds changes, or when that Thread's state changes to anything but `running` (`C/domain/project-refresh.ts:18-26`), and not after a finished tool call (`:27-33`).

- `files` observes `onThreadUpdateHook` and, for an update of kind `event` whose event has the type `tool.finished` and whose `call.name` is `edit` or `write`, calls `files.refresh/v1.request(projectId)`. `git` does the same for the name `git`. `terminal` already requests the refresh from its own store (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/store.ts:106-113`).
- The coordinator coalesces requests over 250 ms (`bundles/features/files/src/client/refresh/coordinator.ts:3-4`).
- **The transition inside step 5(c) (D30), six merged changes.**
  1. _The chat defines both._ Its contracts module exports `ChatHooksV2 = defineHooks('chat.hooks/v2', …)` beside `ChatHooksV1`, and its client part defines a handle for each where it defines one today (`C/index.ts:67`). Every `execute` of an `observe` hook goes to both handles. For `onBeforeRenderBlockHook`, the one `transform`, the block passes through the registrants of v1 and then through those of v2; the repository has no registrant of it. `changedSandboxProjects` (`C/domain/project-refresh.ts:11-35`) answers two sets, the Projects of the three lifecycle causes and the Projects of a finished tool call: v1 fires for both, as today, and v2 for the first. The chat's manifest gains `chat.hooks/v2@2.0.0` under `provides`.
  2. _`git` observes v2._ It is a new observer, with the copy of the hook in `bundles/features/git/src/client/api/peers.ts` and the line `chat.hooks/v2` under `optional`. Until the next change a finished `git` call asks for the refresh twice, through v1 and `files` and through `git` itself, and the coordinator makes one refresh of the two.
  3. _`files` moves._ Its structural copy takes the new id and gains `onThreadUpdateHook` (`bundles/features/files/src/client/api/peers.ts:17-18`), its client part observes both hooks of v2 (`src/client/index.ts:22,103-107`), and its manifest's `optional` line becomes `chat.hooks/v2`. `git` moves before `files` so that no change leaves a finished `git` call without a refresh.
  4. _`notifications` moves:_ the id of its structural copy (`bundles/desktop/notifications/src/client/observer.ts:15`) and its `optional` line.
  5. _`openui` moves:_ the same (`bundles/features/openui/src/client/chat-contract.ts:116`).
  6. _The chat removes v1:_ `ChatHooksV1`, its handle, the tool-call set of `changedSandboxProjects` (`C/domain/project-refresh.ts:27-33`), `C/domain/projector.ts:1240-1254` (`sandboxTools`, `sandboxWrites`) and the manifest's line `chat.hooks/v1@1.1.0`.

**The execution picker** arrives from `settings` in step 6(c) ([section 11](#11-settings-and-providers)).

**The manifest.** `description` becomes "The conversation: transcript, composer and the execution picker." `optional` loses `runtime.terminals/v1` and `terminal/v1` (phase 5) and gains `settings.models/v1` (step 6(c)); `requires` gains `runtime.records/v1@^1.0.0` (step 4(a)), `workspace.hooks/v1@^1.0.0` and `layout.tabs/v2@^2.0.0` (step 4(c)) and `workspace.selection/v1@^1.0.0` (step 6(c)). Its line `runtime.threads/v1@^1.8.0` does not change (`bundles/desktop/chat/manifest.json:30`): the thread archive raised it above the `^1.7.0` that `setThreadExecution` needs, the range `settings` asks for today (`bundles/desktop/settings/manifest.json:20`).

### 11. Settings and providers

**The contracts module (step 6(c)).** `settings` gains `bundles/desktop/settings/src/contracts/` and the export `./contracts` in its `package.json` (`:6-8` exports only `package.json` today). No file of it imports `src/client`. Its build files are in [section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup).

| File under `bundles/desktop/settings/src/contracts/` | Holds                                                                                                                                                                           | Comes from                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `configuration.ts`                                   | The types `ProviderKindId`, `ProviderModel`, `ProviderConfiguration`, `ContextCompactionConfig`, `Profile`, `AutoArchive`, `Configuration` and `ModelExecution`.                | `src/client/domain/config.ts:43,156-170,177-184,247-253,260-263,272-275,277-305,314-322`, with their comments. They are the eight types a draft is made of; `AutoArchive` is the type of `Configuration.threads.autoArchive`, which the thread archive added (`:303-304`). `ModelExecution.effort` takes the runtime's `ReasoningEffort` by a type-only import of `runtime/contracts`, where it takes the settings' own today (`:40`); both are the same seven literals (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/model.ts:141-150`), so no value changes its type. |
| `config.ts`                                          | `settings.config/v1`: `ConfigurationDraft`, `SettingsConfig`, `SettingsConfigV1` and `settingsConfigMethods`.                                                                   | `src/client/services/settings-config.ts:7-54`, with `Configuration` imported from `./configuration.js`. The id, the version 1.1.0, the three methods and their schemas do not change. The bridge and `createSettingsConfig` stay in the client file (`:56-130`) and import the four names from the contracts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `models.ts`                                          | `settings.models/v1`, below.                                                                                                                                                    | New.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `slots.ts`                                           | `SETTINGS_SECTIONS`, the name of the slot `settings.sections`, from step 6(c); `SETTINGS_PROVIDER_FIELD`, `ProviderFieldItemProps` and `ProviderFieldView`, added in step 6(a). | New. The three places where `settings` writes `'settings.sections'` take the constant in step 6(c) (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/index.ts:69`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/appearance-section.ts:45`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/views/settings-view.tsx:336`), and `git` checks its literal against it ([section 8](#8-slots)).                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `index.ts`                                           | `export * from` each of the four.                                                                                                                                               | New.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

- `src/client/domain/config.ts` re-exports the eight types (`export type { … } from '../../contracts/index.js'`), so the other files of `settings` keep their imports; a client part reaches its own contracts by that relative path, as the layout's does (`L/src/client/hooks/thread-navigation.ts:4`).
- `git`'s structural copy of `settings.config/v1` (`bundles/features/git/src/client/api/peers.ts:54-64`) becomes an import of `settings/contracts`, and `bundles/features/git/package.json` gains `settings` under `devDependencies`, beside the other client-only packages it lists there. `git`'s narrower `VersioningDraft` (`bundles/features/git/src/client/api/peers.ts:48-52`) stays its own type for what it reads of a draft.

**`settings.models/v1`, new, 1.0.0 (D13, U16).** A client contract. `settings` supplies everything the picker shows and every execution a pick yields; the chat draws it and writes the Thread (concept, M7: the view is allowed because `settings` adds the labels, the groups and the icons).

```ts
import type { ReasoningEffort } from 'runtime/contracts';

/** A provider, a model and an optional effort: the input of `runtime.threads/v1.setThreadExecution` without its `id`. */
export interface ModelExecution {
  readonly providerId: string;
  readonly model: string;
  readonly effort?: ReasoningEffort;
}

/** What a Thread record stores of its execution; a Thread created before `runtime.threads/v1` 1.7.0 stores none of it. */
export interface StoredExecution {
  readonly providerId?: string;
  readonly model?: string;
  readonly effort?: string;
}

export interface ModelChoice {
  readonly label: string;
  /** The choice's key, unique in the view. */
  readonly value: string;
  /** SVG markup of the model's icon, when it has one. */
  readonly icon?: string;
  /** What the Thread runs on when the reader picks this model. */
  readonly execution: ModelExecution;
}

export interface ModelGroup {
  /** The provider's label; absent when one provider holds every model. */
  readonly label?: string;
  readonly choices: readonly ModelChoice[];
}

export interface EffortChoice {
  readonly label: string;
  readonly value: ReasoningEffort;
  /** "(default)" on the effort the model's catalog names as its default. */
  readonly note?: string;
  /** What the Thread runs on when the reader picks this effort. */
  readonly execution: ModelExecution;
}

export type ExecutionView =
  | {
      /** No configuration was read yet. */
      readonly status: 'loading';
      /** The last read's failure, kept while a read that follows it runs. */
      readonly error?: ErrorInfo;
      readonly reloading: boolean;
    }
  | {
      readonly status: 'ready';
      /** A failed re-read; the view keeps the last configuration beside it. */
      readonly error?: ErrorInfo;
      readonly reloading: boolean;
      /** The execution shown: `stored` resolved against the configuration, or the default. */
      readonly execution: ModelExecution;
      /** The shown model's icon. */
      readonly icon?: string;
      /** The `value` of the shown model's choice. */
      readonly value: string;
      /** `false`: no provider lists a model, and the model is typed by hand. */
      readonly listsModels: boolean;
      readonly groups: readonly ModelGroup[];
      /** The efforts the shown model accepts, without `none`. */
      readonly efforts: readonly EffortChoice[];
      /** The shown effort's label; "Unavailable" when `efforts` is empty. */
      readonly effortLabel: string;
      /** The provider a model typed by hand is written on. */
      readonly typedProviderId: string;
    };

export interface SettingsModels {
  /**
   * The picker's data for one stored execution, or for the default execution
   * when `stored` is absent. Local. The same object for equal arguments until
   * the configuration, an icon or the read's state changes.
   */
  view(stored?: StoredExecution, call?: CallArg): ExecutionView;
  /** Called after every change of what `view` answers. Local. */
  subscribe(listener: () => void, call?: CallArg): Disposable;
  /** Reads the configuration again. Returns at once and never throws; a call while a read runs starts no second one. */
  reload(call?: CallArg): void;
}
export const SettingsModelsV1 = ref<SettingsModels>('settings.models/v1');
```

- `ErrorInfo` is the platform's (`packages/desktop/src/reads/errors.ts:32`). The module also exports `settingsModelsMethods`, the zod schemas of the three methods, written as `settingsConfigMethods` is (`src/client/services/settings-config.ts:44-54`); the schema of `ExecutionView` is strict.
- The view carries no provider configuration value, no endpoint and no credential, and no field the picker does not draw or write (concept, M7 (2)). It is read only: the chat writes a Thread's execution through `runtime.threads/v1`, and nothing writes the default through this contract (M7 (3)).

**How `settings` computes a view.** A new file, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/services/settings-models.ts`, provides the contract from the functions the picker calls today. No rule is written again, and none moves:

| Field of the view                                | Computed by, today                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `status`, `error`, `reloading`, and `reload()`   | The read of `hooks/use-config-snapshot.ts:24-59` with `hooks/use-read-failure.ts:13-28`: the query `queryKeys.configSnapshot` on the window's `settingsQueryClient()` (`src/client/queries/client.ts:26-29`), read again on the window's `focus`. The service observes that query with a `QueryObserver`. |
| `execution`                                      | With `stored` absent, `configuration.models.execution`. Else `resolveThreadExecution(stored, configuration)` of `runtime/contracts`, keeping `providerId`, `model` and an `effort` that is a `ReasoningEffort`: `threadExecution` of `components/organisms/execution-picker.tsx:55-66`.                   |
| The configuration every other field is read from | `withExecution(configuration, execution)` (`domain/config.ts:1641-1647`), "the view configuration" of `execution-picker.tsx:136-148`.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `groups`, `value`, `icon`                        | `modelGroups` (`domain/config.ts:1482-1526`), `modelChoiceKey` (`:1633-1634`) and the icon lookup of `modelIconRequests` (`domain/model-icons.ts:51-72`), read with the query of `hooks/use-model-icons.ts:20-34`.                                                                                        |
| `groups[].choices[].execution`                   | `selectModelChoice(view, choice.value).models.execution` (`domain/config.ts:1650-1661`), which applies `updateModel` (`:1587-1607`): another model starts at its start effort.                                                                                                                            |
| `efforts`, `effortLabel`                         | `modelEfforts` without `none`, `effortLabel` (`domain/config.ts:443-444`), the note from `modelDefaultEffort`, and "Unavailable" for an empty list: `execution-picker.tsx:222-240`. Each choice's `execution` is `setExecutionEffort(view, value).models.execution` (`domain/config.ts:1615-1626`).       |
| `listsModels`                                    | Whether any provider lists a model (`execution-picker.tsx:228-230`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `typedProviderId`                                | The shown provider when it is configured, else the default's (`execution-picker.tsx:212-216`).                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

- **A typed model.** The chat writes `{ providerId: typedProviderId, model }` with no effort. This equals what `updateModel` yields today (`execution-picker.tsx:217-219`) whenever the field is drawn: it is drawn only while `listsModels` is `false`, no model then has an entry, and `modelStartEffort` answers `undefined` for a model without one (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/execution.ts:174-185`).
- **Icons.** Until the first answer no choice has an icon; while a later answer is pending the previous icons stay; a failed read gives no icons and no error. These are the rules of `use-model-icons.ts:12-34`. The request of a view names its shown model when no provider lists it, as today (`domain/model-icons.ts:57-61`), so each distinct stored execution has its own icon query.
- **Identity and memory.** The service keeps one computed view per distinct `stored` (compared by its three fields; the default is one more), at most eight, the least recently read evicted with its icon observer. A view is computed again, and the listeners are called, when the configuration, the read's state or that view's icons change. The eight are a bound, not a need: one footer picker reads one or two at a time, the record's and a write in flight.
- **Lifetime.** The observers run while the service has a subscriber and stop with the last; `view` called without a subscriber computes from what the query cache holds. The client part disposes the service before `clearSettingsQueryClient()` (`src/client/index.ts:108-112`).
- `settings` keeps importing the execution rules from `runtime/contracts` (`domain/config.ts:14,23`) and so keeps `runtime` in its `package.json`. These are functions, not a contract id, and its manifest loses `runtime.threads/v1` all the same.
- **No third list of efforts.** The contract publishes no array of efforts. Its fields are typed by the runtime's `ReasoningEffort`, a type-only import of `runtime/contracts` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/model.ts:141-150`), which the chat already lists as a dependency (`bundles/desktop/chat/package.json:35`). `settings` keeps its own array where it is, unexported from the contracts module (`domain/config.ts:30-40`); it has the runtime's seven values, so a value the service checked against it is a `ReasoningEffort`.

**The provider field slot (D14).** `settings.provider.field`: an item replaces one field of the provider form for one kind.

```ts
/** The `props` of an item of `settings.provider.field`. */
export interface ProviderFieldItemProps {
  /** The provider kind whose form the item is drawn in. */
  readonly kind: string;
  /** The key of the kind's field the element is drawn in place of. */
  readonly field: string;
}

/** The `view` property `settings` sets on the item's element. */
export interface ProviderFieldView {
  readonly kind: string;
  readonly field: string;
  /** The field's value in the draft: for a secret field, a credential id. */
  readonly value?: string;
  /** `missing`: the field is required and empty. `unknown`: the value names a credential the store no longer has. */
  readonly problem?: 'missing' | 'unknown';
}
```

- The element answers with the DOM event `settings-provider-field`, a bubbling and composed `CustomEvent<{ value: string }>`. `settings` reads its credentials again, then writes `value` into the draft's field, only if the page still shows a provider of that `kind`. This is the rule of `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-provider-page.ts:91-110`.
- With two items for one kind and field, the first by `order` is drawn. With none, `settings` draws the field itself.
- `claude-login` gains a client part with the element `claude-login-field` and adds `{ id: 'claude-login', tag: 'claude-login-field', props: { kind: 'claude', field: 'token' } }`. It takes from `settings`: `src/client/hooks/use-claude-login.ts`, the sign-in row of `settings-provider.tsx:179-201,291-298`, the adapter lines of `api/settings-api.ts:652-655,1372-1394`, and the tests `tests/client/claude-login.test.ts` and `tests/claude-login-adapter.test.ts`. Its messages, an empty token asking to sign in and an unknown credential asking to sign in again, go with it.
- **What the moved code takes with it, and what it leaves (RD-3 of the fifth review).** The new files are under `bundles/features/claude-login/src/client/`.
  - _The adapter,_ `bundles/features/claude-login/src/client/api.ts`: `start` and `finish` over `ClaudeLoginV1`, which the part imports from its own contracts module by a relative path, with the bodies of `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:1372-1396`. `finish` answers the `CredentialDescription` of the contract, and the hook publishes its `id`; `credentialFrom` stays in `settings` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:1148`).
  - _Its failures._ The rule of `claudeLoginFailure` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:655-658`) with the two helpers it calls (`:125-128,143-152`), written out in the adapter over the `ClientError` of `desktop/reads`. A `ContractError` whose code is not `internal` rejects with that code, the error's own message and the error as `cause`. A `ContractError` whose code is `internal` rejects with `internal` and the error's message. Anything else rejects with `internal` and "The request could not be completed.". The class was `SettingsApiError`, a `ClientError` under another `name` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:95-97`); no surface shows the name (`packages/desktop/src/reads/errors.ts:26-31`).
  - _`openBrowser`._ The adapter calls `services.use(DoricBrowserV1).open(url)`, with `DoricBrowserV1` from `bundle/contracts` (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/platform.ts:949`), as the settings adapter does (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:1450-1452`). `doric.browser/v1` is a platform contract: a bundle uses one without listing it (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/services.ts:138`), and no manifest lists one (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/ids.ts:26-28`), so the manifest of `claude-login` gains no line for it. `settings` keeps its own `openBrowser` for the link to a provider's site (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/components/organisms/dashboard-provider-page.tsx:53-55`).
  - _The catalog entry._ `sign_in_failed` moves out of the settings catalog (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/domain/error-catalog.ts:28`) into a catalog of the part, `bundles/features/claude-login/src/client/error-catalog.ts`, which exports `claudeLoginErrorCatalog`, an `ErrorCatalog` of `desktop/reads` with the one entry `sign_in_failed: { title: 'Sign-in failed', retryable: false }`. The hook passes it to `toastError` in place of `settingsErrorCatalog` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-claude-login.ts:119-124`). No other surface of `settings` names the code.
  - _The credentials cache._ The hook loses `useQueryClient`, `queryKeys` and `settle` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-claude-login.ts:1,10,71,78-92,112`): it cannot write a cache of `settings`. When `finish` answers, the element dispatches `settings-provider-field` with the credential's `id` as `value` and shows the success toast. `settings` handles the event in `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-provider-page.ts`. It awaits `queryClient.refetchQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.credentials })`, the call its credentials hook makes (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-credentials.ts:147`), and then keeps the item's `kind` and `field` and the event's `value` in a state of the hook. An effect on that state clears it and, if the page still shows a provider of that `kind`, writes `value` into `configuration[field]` through `change`. This is today's effect (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-provider-page.ts:91-110`) with the event in place of `claudeLogin.signedIn`: the commit runs in a render that already has the refreshed list, so the configuration it sends is one the host accepts. If the refetch fails, the list is the old one, the field names a credential the page does not know, the element's view gets `problem: 'unknown'`, and its message asks the reader to sign in again.
  - _Availability._ `setClaudeLoginAvailable` and `useClaudeLoginAvailable` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-claude-login.ts:18-36`) are deleted with the `whenAvailable` that sets them (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/index.ts:12,29,90-93`). The item is in the slot exactly while the client part of `claude-login` is active.
  - _What else leaves `settings`._ The prop `claudeLogin` and its type, in the two views, the provider screen and the dashboard page (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/views/settings-view.tsx:31-32,190-191,204,311`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/views/dashboard-providers.tsx:14-15,34-35,43,101`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/components/organisms/settings-provider.tsx:45,179,186`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/components/organisms/dashboard-provider-page.tsx:17,30,38,98`). The member `claude` of its adapter with the import of `claude-login/contracts` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:32,650-658,1210,1372-1398`). The fake of `claude.login/v1` in its test and story support (`bundles/desktop/settings/tests/support/host.ts:28,127-130`, `bundles/desktop/settings/stories/support/host.tsx:49,168`). The line `claude.login/v1` of its manifest's `optional` (`bundles/desktop/settings/manifest.json:10`).
  - _The platform range._ The moved hook imports `desktop/ui/error-notice` (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/hooks/use-claude-login.ts:3`), which platform 1.7.0 added (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts:8-9`). The manifest's `engines.doric` therefore becomes `^1.7.0` ([section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric)).
- The page does not show which bundle drew a field (concept, [Security and performance](../features/bundle-isolation.md#security-and-performance)): the composition is the operator's choice, and a label on one field would not stop a bundle that draws elsewhere.

**The `site` field (D21, U11).** The kind descriptor gains one optional member, in both declarations:

```ts
// packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/models.ts:30-43, inside modelKindDescriptorSchema
site: z.object({ url: z.url(), label: z.string().min(1) }).strict().optional(),

// packages/llms/src/lib/providers/kinds.ts:47-61, inside ProviderKind
/** The site a provider page links to, with the control's label. */
readonly site?: { readonly url: string; readonly label: string };
```

- `openrouter` sets `site: { url: 'https://openrouter.ai', label: 'Open OpenRouter' }` in `bundles/providers/openrouter/src/host/kind.ts:31`, the values of the fixed map today (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/domain/provider-icon.ts:1-5`).
- `settings` draws the link from the descriptor (`src/client/components/organisms/dashboard-provider-page.tsx:45,80-86`), only when the URL's scheme is `https:`, and shows the label. `providerSite` and its map are deleted with their tests (`tests/provider-icon.test.ts:7-15`), and the cases of `tests/client/dashboard-providers.test.ts:407-428,682-701` read the descriptor.
- `settings`' own `ProviderKind` (`src/client/domain/config.ts:101`) gains the member. The second definition of the `openrouter` kind in `packages/llms/src/lib/providers/kinds.ts:134` gains the same `site`, so that the two do not differ.

### 12. Runtime hand-backs

In this section a path that starts with `src/` or `tests/` and follows no other bundle's path in its sentence or cell is under `bundles/runtime/runtime/`.

**`runtime.terminals/v1` is removed** (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/terminals.ts`, `src/host/services/terminal-sessions.ts`). Its three consumers call `terminal/v1`: `git` (`bundles/features/git/src/host/index.ts:2,20`, `service.ts:63`, the method `list`), the chat (deleted with its live-terminal store in phase 5) and the terminal's own tests. For `git` this is step 7(c): its host part imports `TerminalServiceV1` from `terminal/contracts` (`bundles/features/terminal/src/contracts/index.ts:314`) in place of `RuntimeTerminalsV1`, still through `whenAvailable`; its manifest's `optional` loses `runtime.terminals/v1`, which phase 0 added, and gains `terminal/v1`; and `bundles/features/git/package.json` gains `terminal` under `dependencies`. No package `terminal` depends on depends on `git`. The `terminals` watch goes with it (`src/contracts/watches.ts:81-95,241-244`).

**`runtime/v2`, new, 2.0.0 (D18, U27).** It replaces `runtime/v1` (`src/contracts/runtime.ts:251`). Every method has the input and the output of 1.3.0 but one: the `snapshot` item of `watchProject` has no field `terminals` (`src/contracts/watches.ts:63-71`). That is why the id is new. A caller that wants a Project's terminals reads `terminal/v1`.

- **Who is affected.** No source file in the repository reads the field. The layout's watch drops it (`L/src/client/api/project-watch.ts:60-71`), and phase 3 moves that watch into the runtime's client part. The transport never calls `watchProject`: it calls `project`, `projects`, `vmSsh` and `watch` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/projects.ts:118,170`, `bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/vms.ts:28`, `bundles/foundation/transport/src/kernel.ts:144,153-154`), and builds the `project:snapshot` of its socket from its own read of `terminal/v1` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/sockets/workspace.ts:135-143`). So no REST and no Socket.IO payload changes (U18), and the `site` change of step 6(b), which is in `models/v1`, is not touched.
- **Phase 7 has three steps (D30).**
  - _Step 7(a): the runtime provides both ids._ `src/contracts/runtime.ts` exports `RuntimeV2 = ref<Runtime>('runtime/v2')`, with `Runtime` and `ProjectWatchItem` as they are without the field; the 1.3.0 shapes stay for that time as `LegacyRuntime` and `LegacyProjectWatchItem`, typing `RuntimeV1`. `src/host/services/runtime.ts:24` defines the two services over the same operations, and only the `watchProject` of v1 still fills `terminals`. The manifest gains `runtime/v2@2.0.0` under `provides` and the key `runtime/v2` under `desktopScope`, with the three keys `runtime/v1` has (`bundles/runtime/runtime/manifest.json:10,38`). The runtime's client part calls v2. The same change adds the id to `app/backend/doric-host/tests/bridge-exposure.snapshot.json` (`:96`). On the desktop's side it replaces the id, because from this change no client part calls `runtime/v1`: the four answers of the host the end-to-end tests fake, and its header comment, name `runtime/v2` (`app/desktop/doric-e2e/tests/support/workspace-host.ts:4,354,355,360,371`).
  - _Step 7(b): the consumers move,_ one per merged change. `transport`: its manifest's line and the list its kernel declares become `runtime/v2@^2.0.0`, and its two uses take `RuntimeV2` (`bundles/foundation/transport/manifest.json:10`, `src/kernel.ts:21,78,116,153`); the comments and the README that name the id follow in the same change (`src/kernel.ts:93`, `src/host/sockets/workspace.ts:44,232`, `bundles/foundation/transport/README.md:44,56`). `mcp`: the same (`bundles/features/mcp/manifest.json:14`, `src/host/index.ts:23,344`). The host application: `required.contracts` of the shipped composition names `runtime/v2` (`app/backend/doric-host/composition.json:42`), with the host tests that name the id (`app/backend/doric-host/tests/bundles.test.ts:623-666`, `app/backend/doric-host/tests/bridge.test.ts:487-511`) and the comment of `app/desktop/doric/tests/support/core.ts:296`. Each change edits the test support of its bundle that names the id.
  - _Step 7(c): the runtime removes `runtime/v1`,_ with `RuntimeV1`, the two `Legacy` types, the manifest's `provides` line and `desktopScope` key, and the entry of the bridge snapshot. The comments and the README of the runtime that name the id name `runtime/v2` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/README.md:16`, `src/contracts/rest-errors.ts:118`, `src/contracts/runtime.ts:35`, `src/host/domain/notices.ts:10`, `src/host/domain/service.ts:87`, `src/host/lifecycle.ts:19`, `src/host/services/runtime.ts:10`), and so do its tests (`bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/activation.test.ts:20,163-206`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/support/harness.ts:44,453`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/workspace-restart.test.ts:22`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/runtime-contracts.test.ts`). Everything else this section describes merges in the same change.
- **The size of the move.** Outside the runtime, two source files, two manifests and the shipped composition name the id, and so do nine test and support files of `transport`, `mcp`, the host and the desktop. Two tests of the platform package use the literal as a sample name and keep it (`packages/bundle/tests/composition.test.ts:48`, `packages/bundle/tests/directory.test.ts:87`). The risk is not in the size but in a composition the repository does not hold: [section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build) says what a custom composition that requires `runtime/v1` meets (R15).

**The runtime stops reading `terminal/v1` (D18).** All of it is in step 7(c), so that `runtime/v1` fills its snapshot until it is removed.

- `src/host/index.ts:10,178-185` (the import and the `whenAvailable`) and the two `terminals` reads (`:110,124`) are deleted, with the option `terminals` of the workspace service (`src/host/domain/service.ts:106,135`) and the read `terminals` of the watches (`src/host/services/watches.ts:43,55,301,354`).
- The field `terminals` of the snapshot leaves `src/contracts/watches.ts:63-71` with `runtime/v1`; `runtime/v2` never had it.
- The member `terminals` of the tool host and its type `TerminalControl` are deleted (`src/contracts/tool-host/terminals.ts`, `src/contracts/tool-host/host.ts:16`, `src/contracts/tool-host/index.ts:5`). No source file sets or calls it; the `terminal` tool resolves its own service (`bundles/features/terminal/src/host/tool.ts:158`).
- The runtime's test harness stops importing `terminal/host` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/support/harness.ts`); `tests/domain/workspace-terminals.test.ts` is deleted, and its cases that are the terminal's are already in `bundles/features/terminal/tests/provider.test.ts`.
- The runtime's manifest loses `terminal/v1` and `resources/v1` from `optional`, and `runtime.terminals/v1` from `provides` and from its `desktopScope` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/manifest.json:15,42`).
- **The package edge turns round.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/package.json` loses `terminal` (`:20`), and `nx sync` removes the reference from `bundles/runtime/runtime/tsconfig.json:16`. The entry of `bundles/runtime/runtime/tsconfig.spec.json:24-26` is removed by hand, because no tool writes that file's list ([section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup)). This is in the same change in which `terminal` gains `runtime`, so the two packages never depend on each other: Nx builds a bundle after the packages its `package.json` lists (`dependsOn: ["^build"]`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/project.json:9`), and a cycle there fails the build.

**The nine lease reads** leave `runtime.lease/v1` (`src/contracts/lease.ts:110-133`) in step 7(c), and the contract goes from 1.2.0 to 1.3.0 (U23). No read moves: each is a second implementation of a read its owner already has, left in the runtime when stage 10 moved the reads out, and nothing outside the runtime calls any of the nine.

| Read of `runtime.lease/v1`    | Its owner's read, which serves the window and the REST routes today                                                                                                                                     | In phase 7 |
| ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- |
| `files`, `tree`, `file`       | `files/v1.directory`, `tree` and `content`, over `withLease` (`bundles/features/files/src/host/reads.ts:138-173`; its header, `:1-5`, says they were moved from the runtime).                           | Deleted    |
| `diff`, `changes`, `fileDiff` | `git.commands/v1.diff`, `changes` and `fileDiff` (`bundles/features/git/src/host/changes.ts:1-4`, moved from the runtime's `file-changes.ts` and `files.ts`).                                           | Deleted    |
| `git`, `branches`             | `git.commands/v1.status`, `branches` and `switchBranch` (`bundles/features/git/src/host/status.ts`, `branches.ts`; `service.ts:230,267`).                                                               | Deleted    |
| `resources`                   | `resources/v1.project`, over `withLease` (`bundles/features/resources/src/host/index.ts:32-33`). The runtime's read only forwards to it (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/services/lease.ts:163-175`). | Deleted    |

- **Evidence that nothing calls them.** Outside `bundles/runtime/runtime`, every use of `RuntimeLeaseV1` calls `exec`, `readFile` or `withLease` (`bundles/runtime/default/src/host/skills/sources.ts:105`, `skills/load-skill.ts:29`, `agents/definitions.ts:187,270`; `bundles/features/files/src/host/reads.ts:144-187`; `bundles/features/resources/src/host/index.ts:32`; `bundles/features/papercraft/src/host/sandbox.ts:309,350`). The transport's routes for files, diffs, changes and branches call `files/v1` and `git.commands/v1` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/projects.ts:316,401,444,482`, `threads.ts:509,543`). `files` runs its own reads on the leased sandbox, and three of the six file reads are `git`'s.
- **Deleted in the runtime:** the nine members of `RuntimeLease` and their schemas in `src/contracts/lease.ts`; their declarations in the service's type, with the nine type names it imports for them (`src/host/domain/types.ts:91-102,164-165`, `:6,13,15-19,21,33`); the nine delegations of the lease provider (`src/host/services/lease.ts:127-186`); the service methods they call (`src/host/domain/service.ts:616-751` for the Project reads, and the Thread reads that call `threadGit`, `switchBranch`, `readBranches` and `runner.git`, `:1200-1252`); `runner.git` (`src/host/domain/runner.ts:1559-1575`); `src/host/provisional/` (4 files, 852 lines), which only `service.ts:29-35` and `runner.ts:45-49` import. `ssh`, `exec`, `readFile`, `readFileBytes`, `writeFile` and `withLease` stay.
- **The repository hint stays (U10).** `cwdRepoHint` and `HintProbeIncompleteError` (`src/host/provisional/git-status.ts:123-156`) move to a new file, `src/host/domain/repository-hint.ts`, with the script and the two helpers they use (`HINT_SCRIPT`, `:69`; `isGithub` and `hostOf`, `:266-282`); `runner.ts:46-47` imports them from there. The hint is still refreshed on every Git read, because `git`'s status asks the runtime for it (`bundles/features/git/src/host/service.ts:153`).
- **The runtime's reading of `resources/v1`** goes with the `resources` read: `src/host/peers.ts:1-23` and `src/host/index.ts:29,71,90-93,188-204`.

**The record types of the nine reads.** They are declared in `src/contracts/model.ts:93-123,347-349,367-480,560-576`, with their schemas at `:805-838,881-1024,1118-1137`. Each has one of three ends. The lines of `model.ts` outside these ranges, among them `Project`, `Thread`, `ThreadEvent`, `InputSource` and `ReasoningEffort`, do not change.

One test helper of the host names `BranchResult` in a type (`app/backend/doric-host/tests/helpers/legacy/types.ts:487-491`); the bullet on that helper below removes the line. `git` has types named `ProjectChanges`, `ProjectFileDiff` and `Branches` of its own, with other shapes (`bundles/features/git/src/contracts/reads.ts:34-55`, `index.ts:57-61`). | Deleted. `restErrors.switchBranch` (`src/contracts/rest-errors.ts:257-264`) keeps its body and takes the structural union `{ readonly status: 'ready' \| 'missing' \| 'inactive' \| 'pending' } \| { readonly status: 'refused'; readonly message: string }`, declared beside it as `SwitchBranchOutcome`. For `ThreadGit` and `Branch` this takes the place of the import and the re-export. `GitOperation` is a new export of `git/contracts`: the module does not export it today, and `status.ts` imports it from there afterwards. `git` already writes their schemas itself (`:158-196`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
