- **Every name `git/contracts` and `resources/contracts` export today stays exported,** with the same shape, so no consumer of either module changes.
- **`ProjectLeaseState`, `ProjectSsh`, `LeaseOutcome` and `CwdRepo` stay in `runtime/contracts`.** The lease and the hint are the runtime's. `git`, `files` and `resources` keep importing `LeaseOutcome` from it (`bundles/features/git/src/contracts/index.ts:30`, `bundles/features/files/src/contracts/index.ts:24`, `bundles/features/resources/src/contracts/index.ts:16`), and the transport `ProjectLeaseState` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/projects.ts:6`).
- **The direction of every import, after phase 7.** `git/contracts` imports from `runtime/contracts` (`LeaseOutcome`) and from `files/contracts` (`ReadState`, `ProjectLeaseState`; `bundles/features/git/src/contracts/index.ts:26`, `reads.ts:6`), as today. `resources/contracts` imports from `runtime/contracts`. `files/contracts` imports from `runtime/contracts` and from no feature bundle. `runtime/contracts` imports from none of the three. No import runs from `files` to `git`, so G12 holds, and the `cycles` test sees no new edge.
- **The terminal's records are not among them.** `Terminal`, `TerminalOutput` and their schemas stay in `runtime/contracts`: three kinds of `RuntimeNotice` name them (`src/contracts/runtime.ts:63-70`). The `terminals` field of the snapshot, which names them too (`src/contracts/watches.ts:70`), leaves with `runtime/v1` (D18). `TerminalSnapshot` and its schema are deleted with their two users: `src/contracts/terminals.ts`, and the chat's `C/api/terminals-contract.ts`, which leaves with the chat's live-terminal store in phase 5.
- **One test helper of the host** imports five of the names from `runtime/contracts`: `ContainerResources`, `FileDiff`, `ProjectChangeSet`, `RepositoryChanges` and `TerminalSnapshot` (`app/backend/doric-host/tests/helpers/legacy/types.ts:3-12`), and names a sixth, `BranchResult`, in the member `branches` of its legacy Thread service (`:487-491`). Step 7(c) declares the five shapes in that file, beside the legacy shapes it already declares (`:270-286`), and deletes the member `branches`: no helper implements it and no test calls it (`git grep -n branches -- app/backend/doric-host/tests/helpers` finds that line alone). Phase 10 rewrites or deletes the helper.
- **Tests.** The four files of `tests/provisional/` are deleted with their subjects. Before each is deleted, its case titles are compared with the owner's test (`bundles/features/files/tests/reads.test.ts`; `bundles/features/git/tests/{changes,change-reads,branches,thread-git}.test.ts`), and a case the owner lacks is ported there, against the owner's contract. The hint cases of `git-status.test.ts` move to `tests/domain/repository-hint.test.ts`.
- **Secrets.** The concept asked this spec to confirm, before any file read moves, that every secret the runtime redacts is also registered through `secrets/v1`. No read moves, and none of the nine redacts its answer: no file under `src/host/provisional/` and no line of `src/host/services/lease.ts` names the redactor, which the runner alone calls (`src/host/domain/runner.ts:236,315,382,472`). The owners' reads, which serve every caller today, do not redact either. So this phase adds no registration step. The gap that does exist is the Git token in the terminal's stream, which [Non-goals](#non-goals) names.

**The terminal's structural copies become imports (step 7(c)).** `bundles/features/terminal/src/client/peers.ts` holds its copies because `layout` and `files` sit above the runtime, which imports `terminal/contracts` (`:1-5`). At this commit they are four: `layout.workspace/v1` (`:10-24`), `layout.tabs/v1` (`:26-78`), `layout.sidebar/v1` (`:80-98`) and `files.refresh/v1` (`:100-109`); steps 4(a), 4(d) and 5(b) rewrite and extend them first, so the lines differ by then. With the runtime no longer depending on `terminal`, step 7(c) turns each copy but one into an import and lists the package under `dependencies` in `bundles/features/terminal/package.json`:

| Copy at step 7(c)                                                                                                   | Becomes an import from                                                                                                                                              | New line in `package.json` |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
| `runtime.records/v1` and the Thread record (from `layout.workspace/v1`, step 4(d))                                  | `runtime/contracts`                                                                                                                                                 | `runtime`                  |
| `workspace.selection/v1` and `workspace.sidebar/v1` (from `layout.workspace/v1` and `layout.sidebar/v1`, step 4(d)) | `workspace/contracts`                                                                                                                                               | `workspace`                |
| `layout.tabs/v2` (step 4(a))                                                                                        | `layout/contracts`                                                                                                                                                  | `layout`                   |
| `chat.views/v1` (step 5(b))                                                                                         | `chat/contracts`, for its types alone: the terminal lists the contract under `optional`, so the ref stays declared by id (D32)                                      | `chat`                     |
| `files.refresh/v1`                                                                                                  | Stays a copy: `files` lists `terminal` in its `package.json` (`bundles/features/files/package.json:19`), so an import would close a cycle between the two packages. | —                          |

- None of `runtime`, `workspace`, `layout` and `chat` depends on `terminal` after this step: the runtime's line leaves in it, and the other three list `runtime`, `layout`, `comments`, `history`, `settings` and platform packages.

### 13. The REST and Socket.IO API

The API of `transport` is public (U18). It has no version: no path carries one (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/app.ts:23-36`), and `transport` provides no contract (`bundles/foundation/transport/manifest.json:7`).

**The one change.** `GET /providers/kinds` answers `{ kinds: await models.kinds() }` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/providers.ts:69-70`), so from step 6(b) an object of `kinds` may carry `site: { url, label }`.

| Aspect                                                | Answer                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| What changes                                          | One optional key on the objects of `kinds`. A kind without a site has no such key. In the shipped composition one kind carries it, `openrouter`. |
| What does not                                         | No key is removed or renamed; no value changes type or meaning; the route, its status codes and its errors stay.                                 |
| A consumer that ignores unknown keys                  | Is not affected.                                                                                                                                 |
| A consumer that validates a kind with a closed schema | Fails on the `openrouter` kind from the first host that carries step 6(b).                                                                       |
| Version                                               | None exists to raise. `transport` keeps `0.0.1`; no file of it changes.                                                                          |

**Who reads the kinds.** In the repository two readers exist, and neither rejects a key it does not know:

- _The desktop._ A window calls `models/v1.kinds` over the bridge. The desktop's main process validates the answer against the schema document that the provider's bundle ships (`app/desktop/doric/src/platform/bridge/client-routing.ts:113-172`, `app/desktop/doric/src/platform/bridge/dispatcher.ts:754-791`). The host builds that document with the core, so a desktop app older than the host validates against the newer document, which has `site`. `settings` then takes the fields it knows from each object (`bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/api/settings-api.ts:953-974`).
- _The host._ The platform's schema of a kind is strict (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/models.ts:30-43`). It is checked where the provider answers (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/dispatch.ts:196-204`), in the host process, where the platform and the bundles come from one build.
- No file of the repository calls `GET /providers/kinds` but the transport's own test, which compares the answer with its own list of kinds (`bundles/foundation/transport/tests/host-routes.test.ts:335-346`).

Consumers outside the repository exist (OD25), and this spec cannot read how they validate. One that rejects unknown keys fails on the `openrouter` kind from the first host that carries step 6(b), and reverting the step later does not repair a consumer that already met that host. What this spec does about it is give notice: the rule below and the announcement of the field go into the host README in phase 0, before any other phase merges (R8). The owner accepted that as enough, knowing no consumer outside the repository that validates a kind with a closed schema (U26).

**The compatibility rule,** written into the REST reference of the host README in phase 0, under its conventions (`app/backend/doric-host/README.md:365-372`): "An answer may gain optional fields in any release. A client ignores the fields it does not know. A field is not removed or renamed, and does not change its type or meaning, without a notice in the release notes of an earlier release." Phase 0 writes one more sentence below it: "A later release adds the optional field `site`, `{ url, label }`, to the objects of `GET /providers/kinds`." Step 6(b) replaces that sentence with the description of the fields of a kind descriptor, which the reference does not list today (`:299`), and the release notes of the step name the route and the field.

**Everything else is unchanged, and why.**

| Change of Spec 1                                                                                       | Why no REST or Socket.IO payload changes                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The nine lease reads leave (`runtime.lease/v1`)                                                        | The routes for files, trees, diffs, changes and Git state call `files/v1` and `git.commands/v1` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/projects.ts:482`, `threads.ts:509`). The one lease member the transport calls, `ssh`, stays (`projects.ts:227`). |
| `runtime.terminals/v1` is removed; the runtime's snapshot carries no terminals                         | The terminal routes and the `/projects` socket read `terminal/v1` (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/sockets/workspace.ts:135-143`).                                                                                                                       |
| Record types are declared in `git/contracts` and `resources/contracts` in place of `runtime/contracts` | The declarations move with their shapes. `git` and `resources` already write the schemas their methods answer with, and those do not change.                                                                                                                    |
| The kernel's buffer constant                                                                           | Its value is today's (D20).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Skill sources, file marks, the mirror, the shell                                                       | Window and host contracts; none is a route.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

T13.1 pins this: the tests of `bundles/foundation/transport/tests/` pass at the end of Spec 1 with no change to an expected payload.

### 14. Skill sources

**`default.skill-sources/v1`, new, 1.0.0 (D15, U11).** A host many-contract that `default` declares in `bundles/runtime/default/src/contracts/` and does not provide. A bundle that offers skills to the agent provides it.

```ts
export interface SkillSourceEntry {
  /** A second part of the skill's id, such as an MCP server's slug. */
  readonly group?: string;
  /** The skill's name; with the source and the group it is unique. */
  readonly name: string;
  readonly description: string;
  /** What `read` takes; only the source reads it. */
  readonly reference: string;
}

export interface SkillSources {
  /** The skills the source offers to the prompt of `call.scope`, whose working directory is `cwd`. */
  list(
    input: { readonly cwd: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<{ readonly skills: readonly SkillSourceEntry[] }>;
  /** One skill's text, and the sentence that says where it comes from. The source bounds the time the read may take. */
  read(
    input: { readonly reference: string },
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Promise<{ readonly origin?: string; readonly text: string }>;
}
export const SkillSourcesV1 = ref<SkillSources>('default.skill-sources/v1');
const skillSourceEntrySchema = z
  .object({
    group: z.string().min(1).optional(),
    name: z.string().min(1),
    description: z.string(),
    reference: z.string().min(1),
  })
  .strict();

export const skillSourcesMethods = {
  list: {
    input: z.object({ cwd: z.string() }).strict(),
    output: z.object({ skills: z.array(skillSourceEntrySchema) }).strict(),
  },
  read: {
    input: z.object({ reference: z.string().min(1) }).strict(),
    output: z
      .object({ origin: z.string().optional(), text: z.string() })
      .strict(),
  },
} as const;
```

- **The source's id** is the `bundleId` that `whenAvailable` gives with each provider (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/context.ts:13-30`). `default` names a skill `<bundleId>/<group>/<name>`, or `<bundleId>/<name>` without a group, so an MCP skill keeps the id `mcp/<server slug>/<name>` (`bundles/runtime/default/src/host/skills/catalog.ts:126-138`). `default` keeps its own two sources, the bundle skills of `skills/v1` and the working directory (`cwd/<name>`), and ignores a provider whose `bundleId` is `cwd`.
- **Listing.** `default` calls `list` of every provider in parallel with its own two reads (`bundles/runtime/default/src/host/skills/sources.ts:170-174`) and waits for each at most 5,000 ms, the limit of its working-directory scan (`:21-22`). A provider that is late, rejects, or answers something that fails the schema yields no skills for that prompt, and `default` reports it once per prompt through `log/v1` with the provider's `bundleId`; its manifest lists `log/v1` already (`bundles/runtime/default/manifest.json:18`). The deadline is for a source that calls out while it lists. `mcp` does not: it answers from the servers it has stored and from the skills it listed when each server connected (`bundles/features/mcp/src/host/manager.ts:179-201,577`).
- **Reading.** `load_skill` of a provider's skill calls that provider's `read` with the entry's `reference` and with the tool call's `call`, whose signal ends the read when the prompt is cancelled. `default` sets no deadline of its own on a read: the source bounds it. `mcp` keeps the bound it has today, 30,000 ms for one read (`bundles/features/mcp/src/host/skills.ts:14-15,66-74`), so a read that succeeds today succeeds afterwards. `default` puts `origin`, when present, as the first line and the body after it, with the frontmatter removed by its own `skillBody`, as `bundles/runtime/default/src/host/skills/load-skill.ts:22-41` does today. A read that rejects answers the tool call with today's error, `The skill <name> could not be loaded: <message>` (`:104-116`).
- **`mcp` provides it** from its host part (`{ cardinality: 'many' }`), with what leaves `default`: the listing of the connected servers' skills (`sources.ts:131-151`), with `group: serverSlug(name)` and a `reference` that carries the server id and the URI; the read (`load-skill.ts:32-40`), with `origin` "From the MCP server "<name>" (`<uri>`). Its other files are not available."
- **Manifests and packages.** `default`: `optional` loses `mcp/v1` and gains `default.skill-sources/v1`, the line the `declared` test asks of a bundle that names an id another bundle provides, as the runtime lists `sandbox/v1` and `executor/v1` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/manifest.json:34-35`). Its `package.json` loses `mcp` (`bundles/runtime/default/package.json:20`), and `nx sync` removes the reference of `bundles/runtime/default/tsconfig.json:25`. `mcp`: `provides` gains `default.skill-sources/v1@1.0.0`, and its `package.json` gains `default` under `dependencies`. Both edits are in the one change of phase 8, so the two packages never depend on each other. `mcp/v1` does not change.
- **The tests of `default` that fake `mcp/v1`.** `bundles/runtime/default/tests/support/harness.ts` imports `mcp/contracts` and holds a fake `mcp/v1` (`:23,165-170,288-330`), which the harness provides on request (`:448-449,524,629-631`) and four test files use: `bundles/runtime/default/tests/{skills-live,skills-sources,skills-catalog,load-skill}.test.ts`. Phase 8 replaces it with `FakeSkillSource` and `skillSourceService`, a fake provider of `default.skill-sources/v1` that records its `list` and `read` calls. The harness option `mcp` becomes `sources`, a record from a bundle id to a fake source, and `calls.mcp` becomes `calls.sources`.
  - A source's id is its provider's bundle id, and `createTestServices` provides every fake under the one id `test-provider` (`packages/bundle/src/testing/index.ts:42-51`). Phase 8 adds an option to it, `providers?: readonly { readonly bundleId: string; readonly provide: readonly AnyServiceDefinition[] }[]`, which provides each list from a principal of its own with that id; `provide` keeps its meaning. `bundle/testing` is a test entry of the platform package, no part of a bundle imports it, and the change raises no platform version.
  - The cases of `bundles/runtime/default/tests/skills-live.test.ts` that drive the live catalog through MCP servers (`:196-256,349-415,497-511`) are `default`'s behavior and stay. They provide their fake source under the bundle id `mcp`, so their expected ids and texts (`mcp/docs/guide`) do not change. The cases that test what `mcp` does with its servers move to `mcp` (T8.2).
- The edge of the cycle test runs `mcp` → `default`.

### 15. File marks

**`files.decorations/v1`, new, 1.0.0 (D16, U11).** A client many-contract that `files` declares in `bundles/features/files/src/contracts/` and does not provide.

```ts
/** One mark on one path of the tree. */
export interface FileMark {
  /** What the row's label reads for the mark. */
  readonly description: string;
  /** The badge count of a directory. */
  readonly count: number;
  readonly tone?: string;
  readonly letter?: string;
  readonly struck?: boolean;
}

export interface DecorationsObservation {
  readonly marks: readonly { readonly path: string; readonly mark: FileMark }[];
  readonly error?: string;
  readonly fetching: boolean;
}

export interface FilesDecorations {
  /** The marks of the tree `scope` names, now and after every change. */
  observe(
    scope: TreeScope,
    listener: (state: DecorationsObservation) => void,
    call?: CallArg,
  ): Disposable;
}
export const FilesDecorationsV1 = ref<FilesDecorations>('files.decorations/v1');
```

- The types are those of `git.changes/v1` as the tree copies them today (`bundles/features/files/src/client/api/peers.ts:27-49`), with one change: the marks are a list, because a `Map` is not plain data (U6).
- **`files` asks.** The tree observes every provider for the scope it shows (`bundles/features/files/src/client/tree/files-tree.tsx:91`) and merges the answers; for a path two providers mark, the provider with the smaller bundle id, compared as strings, wins. An `error` of a provider is shown as the tree shows the error of `git.changes/v1` today.
- **`git` provides it** from the store that provides `git.changes/v1` (`bundles/features/git/src/client/changes/store.ts:233`). `git.changes/v1` stays, for git's own Changes view (D17).
- **Manifests.** `files`: `optional` loses `git.changes/v1` and gains `files.decorations/v1`. `git`: `provides` gains `files.decorations/v1@1.0.0`; its three `files.*` lines stay under `optional`, and its copies of `files.sandbox/v1`, `files.refresh/v1` and `files.open-file/v1` become imports from `files/contracts` (`bundles/features/git/src/client/api/peers.ts:10-46`).
- The edge of the cycle test runs `git` → `files`, and no edge runs back.

### 16. Postgres

**The contract and the export go (U5, D22).**

- `postgres.database/v1` is removed: `bundles/foundation/postgres/src/contracts/index.ts` loses its interface, reference and methods, `src/host/database-service.ts` is deleted, and `src/host/postgres.ts:52,58-62` stops providing it. No source file outside `postgres` uses the contract.
- The package export `./prisma` is removed (`bundles/foundation/postgres/package.json:14-19`). `postgres/testing` (`src/testing.ts`) also exports `PrismaClient`, `Prisma` and the type `Database`, for the eleven test files that import them from `postgres/prisma` today: ten under `app/backend/doric-host/tests/` and `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/store/support.ts:16`.
- The bundle goes to 2.0.0, as its own comment announces (`src/contracts/index.ts:4-6`).

**The migration.** `bundles/foundation/postgres/prisma/migrations/20261117000000_drop_legacy_runtime_tables/migration.sql`. It is written as the repository's data migrations are: a header comment that says what it does, that it cannot be reversed and that the database is backed up first, and one explicit transaction (`bundles/foundation/postgres/prisma/migrations/20261010000000_runtime_documents/migration.sql:1-23,270`; `docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:1171`).

```sql
BEGIN;

DO $$
DECLARE
  legacy text;
  held bigint;
BEGIN
  -- Lock before counting. A writer that already holds a table makes this
  -- wait; one that comes later waits for the commit and finds no table.
  FOREACH legacy IN ARRAY ARRAY['thread_event', 'thread', 'project'] LOOP
    IF to_regclass(legacy) IS NOT NULL THEN
      EXECUTE format('LOCK TABLE %I IN ACCESS EXCLUSIVE MODE', legacy);
    END IF;
  END LOOP;

  FOREACH legacy IN ARRAY ARRAY['thread_event', 'thread', 'project'] LOOP
    IF to_regclass(legacy) IS NOT NULL THEN
      EXECUTE format('SELECT count(*) FROM %I', legacy) INTO held;
      IF held > 0 THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Legacy table "%" holds % rows; they were written after the stage-7 migration. See the host README, "Legacy rows at the drop migration".', legacy, held;
      END IF;
    END IF;
  END LOOP;
END $$;

DROP TABLE IF EXISTS "thread_event";
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS "thread";
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS "project";

COMMIT;
```

- **One transaction.** The `BEGIN` and the `COMMIT` are the migration's own, and its safety does not rest on what Prisma wraps around a migration. A raised exception aborts the transaction, so no table is dropped. The repository tests the same pattern for the coalescing migration, which raises inside its transaction and leaves the data as it was (`bundles/foundation/postgres/tests/coalesce-deltas.test.ts:460-494`).
- **The lock.** `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` conflicts with every other lock, so from the lock to the commit no other session reads or writes the three tables, and the rows the count sees are the rows the drop removes. No writer is expected: the operator's steps stop the host before the migration, and since stage 7 nothing but the startup check reads the tables (below). The lock costs nothing then, and no test covers a concurrent writer.
- **Waiting and deadlock.** The lock waits, without a limit, for a session that holds one of the tables. The operator's steps stop the host first, as they do for the coalescing migration (`app/backend/doric-host/README.md:885-889`), so none is expected. The tables are locked in the order of the drops, which follows the foreign keys from the referencing table to the referenced one (`bundles/foundation/postgres/prisma/migrations/20260825000000_initial/migration.sql:117-123`); an insert takes its locks in the same order. If a writer and the migration still wait for each other, PostgreSQL ends one of the two transactions with a deadlock error. When it ends the migration, nothing has changed, and the operator's path below applies.
- The models `Project`, `Thread` and `ThreadEvent` leave `prisma/schema.prisma` (`:45,63,106`), with any enum only they use.
- The date in the folder's name is the phase's merge date if that is later than 2026-11-17; the name must sort after `20261021000000_coalesce_thread_deltas`.

**A build from before phase 10 against the migrated database.** The migration cannot be undone, so a rollback of the code meets a database that has it. Three facts say that such a build still runs when it is from stage 7 or later, and AC10.5 checks it before the phase merges:

- Its `prisma migrate deploy` applies nothing and reports no error. The command "does not issue a warning if an already applied migration is missing from migration history" (Prisma ORM 7, [Development and production](https://www.prisma.io/docs/orm/v7/prisma-migrate/workflows/development-and-production), read on 2026-11-17; the repository pins Prisma 7.9.1, `bundles/foundation/postgres/package.json:28-38`).
- Its startup check passes at its first query when the tables are absent (`bundles/foundation/postgres/src/host/database.ts:38-49`).
- No production code reads the three tables but that check: a search of `bundles/*/*/src`, `app/*/*/src` and `packages/*/src` for the three models and the three table names finds only `database.ts:53-55`.
- A build from before stage 7 is the other case: it still reads and writes the tables, and fails at its first query of one. It could not serve this database before the drop either: stage 7 moved the rows away and deleted them (`bundles/foundation/postgres/prisma/migrations/20261010000000_runtime_documents/migration.sql:16-18`).
- These facts come from Prisma's documentation and from reading the code; the rollback was not run for this spec. AC10.5 runs it. If it fails, the phase does not change the migration: the README section and the release notes of the phase say that a rollback of the code past phase 10 needs the backup restored, and R16 states the limit.

**The startup check loses the marker.** `refuseUnmigrated` (`src/host/database.ts:19-35`) refuses when the legacy tables exist and hold a row; the third query, which names `runtime.migration/v1`, is deleted (`:60-71`), with the `optional` line phase 0 added. Where the tables are absent it passes at its first query, as today (`:47-49`).

**The proof of stage 7 (U12).** `app/backend/doric-host/tests/stage7-migration.test.ts` is rewritten: it applies the migration files up to `20261010000000_runtime_documents` exclusive, inserts legacy rows with SQL, applies `20261010000000_runtime_documents`, and asserts the documents and the marker. The helpers under `app/backend/doric-host/tests/helpers/legacy/` that build rows through Prisma models are rewritten as SQL inserts or deleted with the tests that used only them.

**The operator's path** is written into the host README in this phase, under "Legacy rows at the drop migration". Before the deploy: quit the desktop app, stop the host and back up the database, the first three steps of the coalescing migration (`app/backend/doric-host/README.md:885-894`). When the migration raises, as the concept's R5 states it: read the table and the count in the error; restore the backup taken before the deploy, or export and delete the rows; run `prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back` for the migration; deploy again.

### 17. Bundle lists and the build

Four hand-written lists name the bundles in the host's build files (U7; concept, X21). Each gets a source, and none is generated into a committed file (D25).

| List today                                                                                                                                                                  | After phase 11                                                                                   |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| The `stage` command of `app/backend/doric-host/project.json:23`, 31 paths                                                                                                   | `node packages/bundle/dist/build/cli.js stage app/backend/doric-host/dist/bundles --all bundles` |
| `build-bundles.dependsOn[0].projects` of the same file, 31 project names                                                                                                    | `"projects": ["tag:type:bundle"]`                                                                |
| The `tsc --build` list, the `tsc -p` of `settings`, the per-manifest `build` loop, the papercraft runner and the `stage` list of `app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:50-97` | One line: `NX_DAEMON=false npx nx run doric-host:build-bundles --skip-nx-cache`                  |
| `enabled` in `app/backend/doric-host/composition.json:3-34`                                                                                                                 | Stays hand-written (U12). It gains `account` in step 4(b) and `workspace` in step 4(c).          |

- **`stage --all <root>`.** A new form of the `stage` command (`packages/bundle/src/build/cli.ts:305-328,349-350`): it stages every directory `<root>/*/*` that holds a `manifest.json`, in the order of the directory paths. The order has no effect on the result: `stageBundles` replaces `<dest>/<id>` with each bundle's own `dist` (`packages/bundle/src/build/cli.ts:306-327`), so no bundle's output touches another's; a fixed order only makes the command's failure name the same directory on every run. The form with explicit directories stays. A directory with a manifest and no `dist/manifest.json` fails the command with the directory's name.
- **The Nx pattern.** Nx 22.6.5 passes `dependsOn[].projects` through `findMatchingProjects` (the function `normalizeDependencyConfigProjects` in [the task runner's utilities of the Nx repository](https://github.com/nrwl/nx/blob/22.6.5/packages/nx/src/tasks-runner/utils.ts)), which accepts `tag:` patterns ([its project matcher](https://github.com/nrwl/nx/blob/22.6.5/packages/nx/src/utils/find-matching-projects.ts); both read on 2026-11-17, at the tag of the version `package.json:54` pins), and every bundle project has the tag `type:bundle`. This settles X21: no list is written by a tool.
- **The image.** The `.Dockerfile` builds the bundles with direct `tsc` calls today and records why: "dependencies are untrusted build inputs", so `npm ci` runs with `--ignore-scripts`, and "direct tsc builds avoid coupling the container build to the Nx task graph" (`app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:27-30,47`). D25 replaces those calls with the Nx target, which changes that record, and the owner reopened the second reason for it (U25). The two reasons, weighed:
  - _Untrusted inputs._ `npm ci --ignore-scripts` stays, and no package is added. Nx already runs in the builder: its `post-install` is called there (`:48`). What is new is that the builder computes the project graph, which loads the three plugins `nx.json` names (`nx.json:15-33`: `@nx/js/typescript`, `@nxlv/python` and `@nx/webpack/plugin`), and runs Nx's task runner. All of it comes from the lockfile, as `tsc`, `esbuild` and `prisma` do, which the builder runs today. The set of lockfile packages that execute at build time grows by those plugins; no lifecycle script runs.
  - _Coupling to the task graph._ It is what D25 asks for. Each bundle's Nx `build` target is the one definition of how it builds: the two `tsc` steps and the build CLI (`L/project.json:13-15`), and for `papercraft` its sandbox runner. CI runs that definition on every pull request, `doric-host:build-bundles` included (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:70`, `app/backend/doric-host/project.json:9`). No CI job runs the image's own commands, and they have diverged once: the image omitted `openui` (`app/backend/doric-host/tests/stage-lists.test.ts:1-5`). The cost: the image does what the targets do, so it also typechecks each client part and builds what the bundles' `^build` asks for, which makes the build slower; and a change that breaks an Nx target or a plugin breaks the image.
  - The builder runs Nx with `NX_DAEMON=false` and `--skip-nx-cache`, so no daemon outlives the `RUN` step and nothing is read from a cache.
  - The lines of the `.Dockerfile` that name no bundle stay: `prisma generate`, the `tsc --build` of `packages/storage` and of the host, `platform-schemas.js`, and the moves of the host's own output. The comment of `:27-46` is rewritten to describe the new steps and to keep the first reason.
  - The image is its own merged change, step 11(b), so that it can be reverted alone (R10). The change of the recorded reason is the owner's (U25).
- **`stage-lists.test.ts`** loses its three cases about lists: the case about the Nx stage command in step 11(a) (`app/backend/doric-host/tests/stage-lists.test.ts:159-167`) and the two about the image in step 11(b) (`:169-188`). The `lists` test of [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file) replaces them, and its two entries leave the violations file with them. The first case, about the order of `openui` and `papercraft` in the shipped composition (`:152-157`), is about no list and stays.
- **A custom composition.** A composition named by `DORIC_COMPOSITION` is booted as written (`app/backend/doric-host/README.md:108`), so its operator adds the two new bundles by hand. Without `account`, from step 4(b), the account menu, its badge and About are absent, and nothing reports it: no bundle requires `account`. Without `workspace`, from step 4(c), the sidebar, the Project tabs and the dashboard are absent, and each bundle that requires a contract `workspace` provides is quarantined with `missing_dependency` (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/catalog.ts:648`) and named on the desktop's splash and in the shell's notice: in step 4(c) the seven that require `layout.workspace/v1` (`terminal`, `files`, `git`, `screen`, `usage`, `mcp` and `resources`). `settings` lists the navigation contracts under `optional` and imports no value of `workspace/contracts` or of `account/contracts` (D32), so its window opens without either bundle. The release notes of steps 4(b) and 4(c) name the line to add to `enabled`, and the section "Bundles and composition" of the host README (`:132-146`) gains, in step 4(b), one sentence after its link to `composition.json`: "A custom composition adds `account` to `enabled`"; step 4(c) adds `workspace` to that sentence. The README gets no list of bundles of its own: `composition.json` is the list (R15).
  - **`runtime/v1` under `required.contracts`.** A composition names the contracts without which the host does not serve (`app/backend/doric-host/composition.json:40-47`). From step 7(c) nothing provides `runtime/v1`, and a host whose composition still requires it logs `structural providers unavailable: runtime/v1`, stops and exits (`app/backend/doric-host/src/lib/bundles/boot.ts:119-162`). The operator of a custom composition writes `runtime/v2` there, which the runtime provides from step 7(a). The release notes of steps 7(a) and 7(c) say so, and step 7(a) adds the sentence to the same section of the README.
- **`nx sync`.** The references of the root `tsconfig.json`, of the host's `tsconfig.json` and `tsconfig.lib.json` and of each bundle's `tsconfig.json` are written by `nx sync` from the project graph, which follows the `package.json` files and the imports. A bundle's three other configs are written by hand ([section 19](#19-build-and-package-setup)). CI gains the step `npx nx sync:check` before the build, so that a new bundle whose references were not written fails there.
- **Adding a bundle afterwards** takes its folder with a `project.json` tagged `type:bundle`, a run of `npm install` and of `nx sync`, and, if the deployment runs it, one line in `enabled` (G7).

### 18. Platform version and `engines.doric`

`PLATFORM_VERSION` rises by a minor in each phase that adds to the platform (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts:1-11`), and a bundle that imports a new platform module statically raises `engines.doric` to that version, as commit `530f2717` did for 1.7.0 (`chat`, `settings` and `layout`). The rule is the repository's (`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md:33`): the desktop app and the bundles are not released together, a window's platform is the app's, and a bundle whose range the app does not satisfy is quarantined there (`app/desktop/doric/src/platform/catalog/resolve.ts:89`, `packages/bundle/src/runtime/catalog.ts:279-286`).

| Phase | Platform        | Addition                                                                  | `engines.doric` rises to that version in                                                                                      |
| ----- | --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 4(a)  | 1.7.0 → 1.8.0   | `desktop/ui/element-host` ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)) | `layout`; `account` and `workspace` start at it                                                                               |
| 6(c)  | 1.8.0 → 1.9.0   | `desktop/ui/model-icon` ([section 11](#11-settings-and-providers))        | `settings`, `chat`                                                                                                            |
| 5(a)  | 1.9.0 → 1.10.0  | `desktop/ui/collapsible-block` ([section 10](#10-chat-points))            | `chat` in step 5(a); `files`, `default`, `threads` and `terminal` in step 5(b), each in the change that registers its drawing |
| 6(b)  | 1.10.0 → 1.11.0 | `site` in the kind descriptor of `models/v1` and `models.provider/v1`     | None                                                                                                                          |

- The rows are in merge order (D1). The three exports of `desktop` are the whole of D29; each is added in the step that first needs it, by the rule of `packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts:1-10`, a minor per phase that adds to the platform.
- Each of the four changes adds its clause to the comment of `version.ts:1-10`, its export to `packages/desktop/package.json` and its entry to `app/desktop/doric-platform/tests/import-map.test.mjs`, and its commit message names the versions and the validation command, as `530f2717` does.
- **Step 6(b) raises no range.** `site` is a field a bundle reads at run time, not a module it imports. The precedent is the core: it provides `navigation/v1`, which reached 1.1.0 with platform 1.4.0 (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts:5`), and its range is still `^1.0.0` (`bundles/foundation/core/manifest.json:5-7`). With the core's range unchanged, an older desktop app does not lose the core.
- **An older desktop app (R14).** From each change that raises a range, a desktop app older than that change's platform quarantines the bundles of the row with `engines_unsatisfied`, and with `dependency_quarantined` every bundle that requires a contract of theirs. Its splash lists them with the reason, such as `requires doric ^1.8.0; platform is 1.7.0`, and offers "Continue without them" (`app/desktop/doric/src/splash-page/view.ts:72-93`). From step 4(a) the list holds `layout`, and a window opened without it draws neither a frame nor the notice (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:1428`). A newer app runs older bundles, since 1.11.0 satisfies `^1.7.0`, so updating the app before the host avoids the quarantine; the release notes of each change that raises a range say so. The two earlier raises accepted this risk (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:1426`), and the owner accepted it for these three (U24).
- **Step 6(a) raises one range at no new platform version (RD-3 of the fifth review).** `claude-login` goes from `^1.0.0` to `^1.7.0` (`bundles/features/claude-login/manifest.json:5`), because its new client part imports `desktop/ui/error-notice`, which platform 1.7.0 added ([section 11](#11-settings-and-providers)). No desktop app is newly affected. `layout`, `chat` and `settings` have required `^1.7.0` since commit `530f2717` (`L/manifest.json:6`, `bundles/desktop/chat/manifest.json:6`, `bundles/desktop/settings/manifest.json:6`), and step 4(a), which merges earlier, raises the layout to `^1.8.0`. An app that cannot load `claude-login` could therefore not draw a window before this step either. U24 and R14 stand as they are: both are about the three new platform minors, and this step adds none. The release notes of step 6(a) name the range.
- **The runtime's client part raises nothing.** It imports `desktop/reads`, which the build compiles into each client part; the module is not one the window's import map serves, as `desktop/ui` is (`packages/bundle/src/build/externals.ts:10-25`). `ClientError` and `errorInfo` are therefore the part's own copy, whatever the app's platform.
- **The four new ids raise nothing (U27).** `layout.tabs/v2`, `layout.panel/v3`, `chat.hooks/v2` and `runtime/v2` are contracts of bundles. No platform package and no file of the desktop app names one of the old ids (a search of `packages/*/src` and `app/desktop/doric/src` finds none), so the platform version does not rise for them and no bundle raises `engines.doric`. U24 and R14 stand as they are: the same three platform minors, for the same three exports. A window gets the provider and its consumers from one host, so it never holds a consumer of a new id beside a provider of the old one.
- `PLATFORM_ASSETS_VERSION` stays `1.0.0`.
- The `--all` form of `stage`, the boundary tests and the new CI steps change no version: a bundle cannot depend on them.
- No other bundle's `engines.doric` changes.
- If D1's order changes, a phase takes the next free minor; the table's numbers follow the order, not the phase.

### 19. Build and package setup

What each new part and each new package needs in order to build, typecheck and test. The pattern is the one the bundles with a client part have today; nothing here is a new mechanism.

**How a part is built today.**

- A bundle's `build` target runs three commands in order (`L/project.json:11-17`). `tsc --build <bundle>/tsconfig.json` emits `src` without `src/client` into `dist`, the contracts module with it (`L/tsconfig.json:5-15`). `tsc -p <bundle>/tsconfig.client.json` typechecks the client part, the contracts and the stories and emits nothing (`L/tsconfig.client.json:8,14-20`). The build CLI then bundles `src/client/index.ts` when the file exists, leaves `bundle/contracts` and every `<id>/contracts` external, and loads the built part once (`packages/bundle/src/build/parts.ts:1-7`, `packages/bundle/src/build/externals.ts:10-25`, `packages/bundle/src/build/cli.ts:198-244`).
- **A contracts module that another bundle imports is emitted and exported.** The importer's built part resolves `<id>/contracts` through the provider's `package.json`, so the provider's `tsconfig.json` emits `dist/contracts/index.js` and its `package.json` has the export `./contracts` (`L/package.json:8-13`).
- **A bundle with a `.tsx` file in its client part sets `"jsx": "react-jsx"` in `tsconfig.json`,** although that file excludes the client: esbuild reads the `tsconfig.json` nearest the entry (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:989`, `bundles/features/terminal/tsconfig.json`).
- **A bundle lists every workspace package it imports,** and no two bundles list each other. The line is what Biome's rule checks, and it is the Nx edge that builds the other package first (`"dependsOn": ["^build"]`, `L/project.json:9`), so a cycle of such lines fails the build.
- **The lockfile (RD-1 of the fifth review).** CI installs with `npm ci` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:45`), and so does the image (`app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:47`). `npm ci` fails when `package-lock.json` disagrees with a `package.json` of the workspace, and the lockfile holds the `dependencies` and `devDependencies` of every workspace package and one link for each (`package-lock.json:955-973,33339-33342`). A change that edits either key in any `package.json`, or adds a workspace package, runs `npm install` with npm 11.13.0, the version CI and the image pin (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:42-43`, `app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:21-25`), and commits `package-lock.json`. A change that edits only `exports` leaves the lockfile as it is. The table of package lines at the end of this section names the steps.
- **`nx sync` writes some `references`, not all (RD-6 and CB-2 of the fifth review).** Its generator `@nx/js:typescript-sync` writes the references of the root `tsconfig.json`, of each project's `tsconfig.json`, and of a project's `tsconfig.app.json`, `tsconfig.lib.json`, `tsconfig.build.json`, `tsconfig.cjs.json`, `tsconfig.esm.json` or `tsconfig.runtime.json` where the project has one ([the generator at the tag of the installed version, 22.6.5](https://github.com/nrwl/nx/blob/22.6.5/packages/js/src/generators/typescript-sync/typescript-sync.ts), read on 2026-11-17). No bundle has a file of those six names, and the generator reads no `tsconfig.client.json`, `tsconfig.client-spec.json` or `tsconfig.spec.json`. A change that edits a `package.json` runs `npx nx sync` and commits what it wrote: the `tsconfig.json` of each project whose lines changed, and the root `tsconfig.json` when a project with a composite `tsconfig.json` appears, as `account` and `workspace` do, and `settings` in step 6(c). From step 11(a) CI runs `npx nx sync:check` ([section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build)), which checks those files and no other.
- **The references written by hand.** The three other configs of a bundle hold lists a person wrote: a platform package as `../../../packages/<name>` in the client config and as `../../../packages/<name>/tsconfig.lib.json` in the two spec configs, and a bundle as its directory (`bundles/features/terminal/tsconfig.client.json:22-25`, `bundles/features/terminal/tsconfig.client-spec.json:16-23`, `L/tsconfig.client-spec.json:16-26`). With a reference, `tsc -p` reads the declarations the referenced project emitted, which `^build` has built by then (`L/project.json:9`). Without it, `tsc` reads that package's sources through the `@org/source` condition (`tsconfig.base.json:19`). Both compile: five client configs have no list today (`chat`, `layout`, `notifications`, `threads` and the core), and neither does the emitting spec config of `docker`, whose tests import four workspace packages (`bundles/sandboxes/docker/tsconfig.spec.json`). No tool checks these lists, so this spec fixes them by three rules. A new config is written with the list its table below gives. A step that removes a package line removes the hand-written references to that package, which the table of package lines names. No existing list gains an entry.
- **`rootDir`, and the TS6059 the fifth review expected.** A new client-spec config emits into `dist-tests` with `rootDir` `"."`, and TypeScript reports TS6059 for a file it would emit from outside `rootDir`. A file of another workspace package is never such a file, with or without a reference: it is reached through `node_modules`, and TypeScript neither emits a file it found there nor holds it to `rootDir`. This spec ran `tsc -p` of TypeScript 5.9.3 on two existing configs with their `references` removed and their output sent to a scratch directory: `bundles/features/terminal/tsconfig.client-spec.json` and `L/tsconfig.client-spec.json`, in a checkout with the dependencies installed, where both files and `tsconfig.base.json` are byte for byte those of this commit. Each run exited with 0 and emitted files of its own bundle only, while its program held the sources of `bundle` and `desktop` and, for the layout, of `runtime`. What raises TS6059 is a relative import that leaves the bundle's directory. The new tests import their own bundle's sources by a relative path and every other package by its name.
- **Where a line goes.** A workspace package whose contracts a source file imports goes under `dependencies`. In a bundle with a host part, a package only its client part, its stories or its tests use goes under `devDependencies`, as `terminal` lists `desktop` and `react` (`bundles/features/terminal/package.json:28-36`). A bundle with a client part alone lists under `dependencies` what its sources import, as the layout does (`L/package.json:15-35`), and under `devDependencies` what only its tests and stories import, as phase 0 lists `happy-dom` in the layout ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)). The versions are those the other bundles write: a workspace bundle `0.0.1`, `desktop` `1.0.0`, `react` and `react-dom` `^19.0.0`, `lucide-react` `^0.544.0`, `zustand` `^5.0.15`, `@tanstack/react-query` `^5.104.1`, `happy-dom` `20.8.9`, `tslib` `^2.3.0`, `zod` `^4.4.3`.
- The lists below are what the moved files import at this commit. The two dependency checks of [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file) hold each list exact from then on: a package that is imported and not listed fails `npm run lint`, and one that is listed and not used fails `dependencies`.

**`runtime`, phase 3: a client part and the export `./testing`.** Paths are under `bundles/runtime/runtime/`.

## Flows

1. **The window starts on a Thread.** Every client part activates; the runtime's part calls no host method while it does. `workspace` reads `projects()`, which starts the load of the Projects, reads the route `/projects/<p>/threads/<t>`, sets the tab owner to `<t>`, follows `<p>` and publishes the selection `{ projectId: <p>, threadId: <t> }` once the mirror describes the Thread. The window mounts its root: the shell draws the conversation form, with `workspace-sidebar` in `shell.sidebar` and the chat's element in `<core-outlet>`. The chat reads the Thread from `runtime.records/v1` by the id of its route and takes a `view` of it, and the runtime's part opens the one `viewThread` stream for `<t>`.
2. **The reader opens a child Thread in a tab.** The sidebar executes `workspace.hooks/v1.onOpenThreadHook` with the ids. The chat's observer calls `layout.tabs/v2.open` with its own element and icon. The shell draws the tab in the active owner's set. When the child is renamed, the mirror notifies, and the chat calls `retitle` with `everyOwner`.
3. **The agent runs `edit`.** The chat receives the event `tool.finished`, updates its projection and looks the name up in its registry: `files` registered `files-edit-call`, standalone and open by default, so the chat creates that element and sets its `view`. The chat executes `onThreadUpdateHook`; the observer of `files` sees `tool.finished` with the name `edit` and requests `files.refresh/v1` for the Project. The reader closes the block: the element dispatches `chat-block-chosen`, and the chat sets `view` again with `chosen: false`.
4. **A prompt starts.** `default` reads its bundle skills, scans the working directory and calls `list` of every provider of `default.skill-sources/v1`, all in parallel. `mcp` answers within the deadline with its servers' skills, and the catalog names them `mcp/<slug>/<name>`. The agent calls `load_skill` with such a name; `default` finds the entry, calls `read` of `mcp` with its `reference`, and answers the tool with the origin line and the body.
5. **The files tree marks changes.** The tree observes every provider of `files.decorations/v1` for its Project and working directory. `git` reads its changes for that scope and answers with the marks; the tree draws them. `git` notifies again after its next read.
6. **The reader opens the settings.** The reader picks "Settings" in the account menu. `account` executes the item's command, `core.open-settings/v1`, with the item's input `{ defaultPath: '/settings' }`. With a settings window open, the core focuses it; with none, it opens one at `/settings`.
7. **The runtime's client part fails to activate.** The failure is in one window: each window activates its client parts in its own environment (`packages/desktop/src/client/activate.ts:1-2,150`). That window's activation quarantines the runtime there with `activate_failed` (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/activation.ts:366-376`) and, with `dependency_quarantined`, every bundle that requires `runtime.records/v1` (`:285-296`): `workspace`, `chat`, `terminal`, `files`, `git` and `threads`. The runtime's host part is in another process and keeps running; its Projects, Threads and prompts are not affected, and the REST API keeps answering. Another window activates on its own and fails only if the same error occurs there. In the window that failed, the shell draws the conversation form with empty slots and the notice, which names the quarantined bundles; `screen`, which lists the contract under `optional`, activates without it.
8. **The reader archives a Thread.** The row's action in `workspace-sidebar` calls `runtime.records/v1.archiveThread`. The runtime's part calls the host, puts the marked Threads into the mirror and resolves with them and the batch. The sidebar's Chat tab stops listing them, the Archived tab lists them, and `workspace` shows the toast "Thread archived". Undo calls `restoreThread` with the batch. While the window shows a Thread, the chat holds a `view` of it, and the host's archiver leaves that Thread where it is.

## Contracts and versions

**The rules.** Additions follow the repository's table (`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md:44-49`): a new method, a new optional input field or a new output field is a minor. A removed member or a changed meaning is a major (`:49`), and a major is a new id, because the `/vN` of an id is its major (`:37`). A contract that disappears is removed, with no parallel provider afterwards (concept, A1).

**How a manifest line is written.** A `requires` line is `<id>@^<M>.<m>.0`, where `<M>.<m>` is the version of the contract at which the last member the bundle uses was added: `^1.0.0` for a contract this spec creates, `^2.0.0` or `^3.0.0` for one of the four new majors, and the existing range for a line that only moves with its code. An `optional` line is the id alone, because the manifest's schema gives it no range (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/manifest.ts:158-160`). A bundle lists a contract under `requires` when it calls `services.use` on it, which the runtime refuses for an unlisted id (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/services.ts:133-151`), and under `optional` when it reaches it through `whenAvailable`, a hook or an event; the build's dry activation and the `declared` test fail on a missing line.

**The four new majors (U27, D2, D30).** Four contracts change the meaning of a call and take a major. This is the one statement of the four; the rest of this spec links here. The scheme is the repository's: the suffix of the id rises by one, the provider lists `<id>@N.0.0`, a consumer requires `<id>@^N.0.0`, an `optional` line carries the id alone, and the exported reference takes the suffix (`LayoutTabsV2`). `layout.panel/v2@2.0.0` and `layout.stack/v2@2.0.0` were made this way (`L/manifest.json:12,15`, `L/src/contracts/index.ts:263,442`).

| New id                  | Replaces                | What differs from the old id                                                                                                                                                                                                | Added in            | Consumers in the repository, and the change that moves each                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Old id removed in   |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| `layout.tabs/v2@2.0.0`  | `layout.tabs/v1@1.2.0`  | The owner of the tabs is what `setOwner` names, where the layout derived it from the route. A tab's icon is the element `iconTag` names; `icon` and its four names are gone. Added: `setOwner`, `everyOwner`, `openBottom`. | Step 4(a), change 2 | The layout's own strip and navigation (`L/src/client/components/organisms/workspace-tabs.tsx:15,40`, `L/src/client/hooks/thread-navigation.ts:4,21`): change 2. `files` (`bundles/features/files/manifest.json:24`, `src/client/commands.ts:11,69,117`, `src/client/tree/files-tree.tsx:25,89`): change 3. `git` (`bundles/features/git/manifest.json:23`, `src/client/commands.ts:13,118`, `src/client/changes/git-changes.tsx:16,57`): change 4. `terminal` (`bundles/features/terminal/manifest.json:20`, `src/client/peers.ts:78`, `src/client/index.ts:33,134,156,163`, `src/client/elements.tsx:17,94`): change 5. `workspace` and the chat first use the contract in step 4(c), as v2. | Step 4(a), change 6 |
| `layout.panel/v3@3.0.0` | `layout.panel/v2@2.0.0` | The panel starts with no view selected, where it started on `files`. `select` is unchanged.                                                                                                                                 | Step 4(a), change 2 | `files` (`bundles/features/files/manifest.json:23`, `src/client/commands.ts:10,63`): change 3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Step 4(a), change 6 |
| `chat.hooks/v2@2.0.0`   | `chat.hooks/v1@1.1.0`   | `onSandboxActivityHook` does not fire after a finished tool call. The other four hooks are unchanged.                                                                                                                       | Step 5(c), change 1 | `git`, a new observer: change 2. `files` (`bundles/features/files/src/client/api/peers.ts:18`, `src/client/index.ts:22,103`): change 3. `notifications` (`bundles/desktop/notifications/src/client/observer.ts:15`, `src/client/index.ts:12,22`): change 4. `openui` (`bundles/features/openui/src/client/chat-contract.ts:116`, `src/client/index.ts:18,60`): change 5. `files`, `notifications` and `openui` list `chat.hooks/v1` under `optional` from phase 0, and each line becomes `chat.hooks/v2` in the bundle's change; the line of `git` is new in change 2.                                                                                                                        | Step 5(c), change 6 |
| `runtime/v2@2.0.0`      | `runtime/v1@1.3.0`      | The `snapshot` item of `watchProject` has no field `terminals`. Every other method is unchanged.                                                                                                                            | Step 7(a)           | The runtime's client part, which holds from phase 3 the calls the layout makes today (`L/src/client/api/host.ts:10`, `L/src/client/api/workspace-api.ts:27,414`): step 7(a). `transport` (`bundles/foundation/transport/manifest.json:10`, `src/kernel.ts:21,78,116,153`), `mcp` (`bundles/features/mcp/manifest.json:14`, `src/host/index.ts:23,344`) and the host application (`app/backend/doric-host/composition.json:42`): step 7(b), one merged change each.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Step 7(c)           |

- **The transition (D30).** Between the change that adds a new id and the change that removes the old one, the provider provides both, and the old id keeps its version and its meaning: `layout.tabs/v1` draws `icon`, `layout.panel/v2` starts on `files`, `chat.hooks/v1` fires after a tool call, and `runtime/v1` fills `terminals`. Each consumer moves in its own merged change, with its manifest line, its structural copy where it has one, and the fakes of its tests and stories. The sections of the three providers give the order and what each change holds: [section 5](#5-the-shell-layout), [section 10](#10-chat-points) and [section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs).
- **Which rule sets the transition, and which ends it.** The repository provides a discontinued contract beside the new major until it is removed (`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md:60`); that is the transition. The concept proposes no deprecation window (A1), so the old id is removed by the last change of the step that added the new one, or of phase 7 for the runtime, as the three moved contracts are removed inside phase 4 (D27, U12). After that change nothing provides the old id.
- **The tests and stories that name an old id** move with their bundle: a search for each literal finds `layout.tabs/v1` in 20 test and story files, `layout.panel/v2` in 8, `chat.hooks/v1` in one and `runtime/v1` in 13, two of which are the platform's own and keep the literal ([section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs)). `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/activation.test.ts:118-119` lists the two ids of the layout and is edited in the change that removes them.
- **What a bundle outside the repository sees (R7).** One that lists an old id under `requires` is quarantined with `missing_dependency` from the change that removes it, and the notice names it. One that reaches the id through `whenAvailable`, or observes `chat.hooks/v1` through its own copy, gets nothing and no report (concept, A1). In both cases it stops running against a meaning that changed, which is what the new id is for.
- **The release notes.** The change that adds a new id and the change that removes the old one each carry the row's text ([Phases](#phases)).

**The versioning deviations (U21, U23, D2).** Two contracts keep their id and take a minor although the table asks for a major. The owner decided each: `core.open-settings/v1` in OD26 (U21), with the recorded reasons that the command stays in the core as a kept decision (OD17) and that no caller in the repository makes the changed call after phase 2; and `runtime.lease/v1` in decision 27 (U23), which decision 30 narrowed to this contract. A deviation covers the change of its row and no other. This is the one statement of the two; the rest of this spec links here.

| Contract                | Version       | Release note                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Merged in |
| ----------------------- | ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- |
| `core.open-settings/v1` | 1.0.0 → 1.1.0 | The input gains `defaultPath`. A call with neither `path` nor `defaultPath` fails with `invalid_input` when no settings window is open; before, it opened the window at `/settings`. Pass `{ defaultPath: '/settings' }` for the old behavior. | Step 2(a) |
| `runtime.lease/v1`      | 1.2.0 → 1.3.0 | The nine reads leave: `files`, `tree`, `file`, `diff`, `changes`, `fileDiff`, `resources`, `git` and `branches`. Use `files/v1`, `git.commands/v1` and `resources/v1`.                                                                         | Step 7(c) |

- **What a bundle outside the repository sees.** The id does not change, so the id says nothing of the change: such a bundle learns of it from the release note, or from its own behavior (R7).
- **The release note.** The merged change that makes a row's change carries the row's text, after the line "`<contract>@<new version>` keeps its id by the owner's decision, although the versioning table asks for a major:" ([Phases](#phases)).
- **`versioning.md`.** Phase 12 adds, under the table of rules by contract type of the rewritten `docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md` (`:44-49` today), the sentence "By the owner's decisions of 2026-11-17 (`docs/features/bundle-isolation.md`), two contracts kept their id and took a minor although a member left or a meaning changed:" and, below it, the two rows of this table with their contract, version and release note. The documentation plan's inventory names this addition in its row of that document. The four new majors follow the table and need no sentence there.

**Every contract this spec touches.** A row marked "U21" or "U23" is one of the two deviations, and a row marked "U27" one of the four new majors.

The old id is provided unchanged until step 7(c).                      | 2.0.0; the old id 1.3.0, then — | 7(a), 7(c) |

`git.changes/v1`, `mcp/v1`, `terminal/v1`, `files/v1`, `files.sandbox/v1`, `files.refresh/v1`, `files.open-file/v1`, `claude.login/v1`, `resources/v1` and every other contract not in the table do not change.

**Bundle versions.** Each merged change raises the version of every bundle it touches once, by the largest of: a major when a contract the bundle provides leaves; a minor when a contract, a slot or a slot item of the bundle appears, or a contract it provides takes a minor; a patch otherwise (`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md:57`). The table gives the major and minor each bundle has at the end; the patch follows from the number of merged changes. Phase 0 is one such change for 14 bundles, and the owner's answer F1 confirms the rule for it: each takes a patch, and the column "After phase 0" gives the version ([Files to change](#phase-0)).
