The platform version is in [section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric). A consumer of a contract that takes a minor raises its range only when it uses the addition. A consumer of a contract that takes a major changes the id in its manifest, which is a patch of the consumer.

## Persisted data

- **localStorage.** `doric.selection` and `doric.project-tabs` keep their names and formats; `workspace` reads and writes them (U12). The core's one-time migration that names `doric.selection` stays (`bundles/foundation/core/src/desktop/migrations.ts:10-13`).
- **Database.** One migration, `20261117000000_drop_legacy_runtime_tables`, drops `thread_event`, `thread` and `project` after checking that they are empty ([section 16](#16-postgres)). It cannot be undone. A build from before phase 10 still runs against the migrated database, and the tables do not come back (R16). No other table changes.
- **Documents and events.** No storage document, stream or event type changes. The document type `runtime.migration/v1` stays defined by the runtime; `postgres` stops reading it.
- **Manifests.** Twenty-two `optional` lines are added in phase 0 and eight of them leave again with their use.

## Alternatives considered

The concept's [Alternatives considered](../features/bundle-isolation.md#alternatives-considered) records the alternatives to the decisions it made. These are the alternatives to this spec's decisions.

- **`core.open-settings/v2`.** It follows the repository's table. Rejected by the owner (OD26): the command stays in the core under its id as a kept decision (OD17), and no caller in the repository makes the call whose meaning changes.
- **The four ids kept, each with a minor,** as version 3 of this spec had them by decision 27. Rejected by the owner (U27): each of the four changes the meaning of a call, and the repository's table gives a changed meaning a new id.
- **A new id for `runtime.lease/v1`** as well. It follows the repository's table. A new id changes the manifest and the reference of every consumer of the lease, although each uses only members that stay ([section 12](#12-runtime-hand-backs)). Rejected by the owner (U23): the contract loses nine reads nobody calls and keeps its id as a versioning deviation.
- **A new id with the old one provided beside it for one release.** The only course that does not stop a bundle outside the repository at once. It is the deprecation window the concept proposes against (A1), and it would keep for a release the code each change removes: the layout's set of icons, the chat's list of tools and the runtime's read of `terminal/v1`. Rejected for D30, which ends each transition inside its step.
- **Each new id in one merged change, with no transition:** the provider, every consumer and the removal together, as `layout.stack/v2` replaced `layout.stack/v1` (`L/src/contracts/index.ts:442`). No two ids at once, and no code that serves the old one. Rejected for D30: the change would cross four bundles for the layout's ids, five for the chat's and three with the host for the runtime's, and could only be reverted whole; the repository provides the old id until it is removed (`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md:60`), and D27 already moves consumers one bundle per change.
- **`layout.tabs/v2` renames `threadId` to `owner`,** since a major is taken anyway. Rejected for D6: decision 30 takes the major for two changes of meaning, and a rename is a third change, which alters the input of `terminal` and of the shell's strip for no behavior. Reshaping a contract is Spec 2's ([Non-goals](#non-goals)).
- **`layout.tabs/v2` keeps `icon`, accepted and not drawn,** as version 3 had it under the old id. Rejected: a field without effect has no place in a new contract.
- **A method of the shell that sets its form** (`showPage`). One more contract for one boolean. Rejected for D5: the slot's occupancy says the same, and a third bundle can show a page by filling the slot.
- **A mirror with reads only, and an `apply` method** that takes a record from whoever wrote it. Rejected for D3: any bundle could put any record into the mirror, and the layout's adapter, which already holds reads and writes together, would be split.
- **Menu entries as elements** in `account.menu`. Rejected for D9: an element of another bundle is a React root of its own inside the menu and takes no part in its focus and keyboard handling.
- **Tab and panel icons as names of a shared icon set.** Plain data and no element. Rejected for D6: it needs the whole icon set in the shell, and the Thread tab's icon depends on the Thread's kind, which no name carries.
- **The drawings' owners build on a chat element** in place of `desktop/ui/collapsible-block`. No platform change. Rejected for D10: the block takes React children and a React label, which an element of another root cannot receive.
- **A slot for the history buttons** so that they stay in the shell. Rejected: they are navigation, and one more slot would exist for two buttons.
- **`runtime/v2` keeps `terminals` as a list that is always empty,** as version 3 had it under the old id, where a removed field would have broken a strict reader. Rejected for D18: under a new id no reader depends on the field, and an empty list says something false about a Project that has terminals.
- **A build command that prints the bundle directories,** used by the image's `tsc` and `stage` lines. It keeps the image's steps as they are. Rejected for D25: `settings` and `papercraft` build differently from the others, so the image would still name them; the Nx target already knows how each bundle builds.
- **Keeping `postgres/prisma` for tests.** No test changes its import. Rejected for D22: OD14 asks for its removal where possible, and `postgres/testing` exists for tests.
- **A second list in the violations file for the kept decisions** (`recorded`). Rejected for D23: no test finds those decisions, so a test of that list can only check that its entries are well formed, and the entries repeat what the concept's Decisions already state and could drift from them.
- **A separate test file with its own script and CI step, and its own file helpers.** Rejected for D23: its `imports` test would walk the files `tests/lint.test.mjs:224-245` already walks with a stricter rule, and its helpers could only be copies, because that file exports nothing.
- **A snapshot of the configured providers and models in `settings.models/v1`,** with the grouping and the choice rules written again in the chat. It is the concept's wording. Rejected for D13: the rule that groups models, the rule that names the effort a chosen model starts at and the icon lookup would then live in two bundles, and `settings` would publish a subset of the configuration without the work that makes the view its own (concept, M7).
- **Copies of the shared files** in each bundle that needs them (`element-host.tsx` with `use-region-items.ts`, `model-icon.tsx`, the collapsible block). A copy needs no platform change: no platform minor, no raised `engines.doric` and no desktop app release, and so none of R14. Rejected for D29: each of the three draws the same thing in every bundle that uses it, a copy changes apart from its original, and the repository already shares such a component through `desktop` and raised the platform for it (`desktop/ui/error-notice`, commit `530f2717`). The cost D29 accepts is that a later change to one of the three needs a platform minor and a desktop app release, and that an older app quarantines the bundles that import it. The owner chose D29 with that cost (U24).
- **The three platform exports in one platform minor,** added together before step 4(a). The platform would rise twice where D26 has four, so an older desktop app would lose bundles at two points. Rejected for D26: `desktop/ui/collapsible-block` would move before the prototype of phase 5 shows that a drawing can leave the chat (R1), and at any of the points the remedy is the same, the newest app. The owner accepted the three points (U24).
- **A field `owner` on `close` and `retitle` of `layout.tabs/v1`,** with `threadId` kept as a second name. Rejected for D6: no caller would pass it, and a field can leave a contract only with a major.
- **`stage --all` in the order of the manifests' `requires`.** Rejected: each bundle is staged into its own directory, so the order changes nothing.
- **The image keeps its direct `tsc` build, and reads its two lists from the disk:** `tsc --build` over `bundles/*/*/tsconfig.json`, and `stage --all`. It keeps the reason the `.Dockerfile` records and loads no Nx plugin in the builder. Rejected for D25: the line for `settings`, whose `tsconfig.json` emits nothing and is not a build project (`bundles/desktop/settings/tsconfig.json:4-8`, `app/backend/doric-host/.Dockerfile:84`), and the lines for the sandbox runner of `papercraft` (`:89-91`) would still name a bundle, so the `lists` test would keep an entry; and the image would still build by commands that no CI job runs. The owner chose the Nx target (U25).
- **The image keeps its two hand-written lists and the two test cases that guard them,** and only `project.json` loses its list. It keeps the reason the `.Dockerfile` records and changes nothing in the image. Rejected for D25: the `lists` entry of the `.Dockerfile` would stay in the violations file for good, and the image would still build by commands that no CI job runs. The owner chose the Nx target (U25).
- **`nx affected -t test` in CI,** in place of every bundle's tests on every pull request. Less work per pull request. Rejected for D24: Nx finds what a change affects through imports and `package.json` dependencies, and bundles also depend on each other through structural copies, which are neither. `terminal` uses three contracts of `layout` through copies and names `layout` in no `package.json` (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/peers.ts:24,78,98`), so a change to the layout would not run the terminal's tests. No remote cache shortens the full run (`nx.json:43`); [section 2](#2-ci) bounds its cost with the second job.
- **The `cycles` test reports every edge inside a cycle,** which is how version 4 worded it. Rejected: once phase 0 has added its manifest lines, `core` → `postgres` → `runtime` → `core` joins 14 bundles into one component with 41 edges inside, until phase 10 removes the edge from `postgres`. Each of the 41 would need an entry, and so would every manifest line a phase adds in between. The test takes a reviewed list of wrong-way edges out of the graph instead and fails on any cycle that is left ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)).
- **The tests of `workspace` load the built client parts of `runtime` and `layout`,** as the tests of `threads` load the chat's (`bundles/features/threads/tests/client/support/window.ts:34-42`). It would keep the whole window in one test. Rejected for D31: a story cannot resolve another package's `dist`, so the stories would need a fake of the mirror all the same; a test of `workspace` would fail on a defect of the shell; and the same loading from `terminal` to the chat would close a cycle of packages until step 7(c). The composed window stays with the desktop end-to-end suite.
- **`layout/testing` with one fake of the shell's contracts.** Rejected for D31: the consumers fake the few methods they call, each in its own support file, and nothing they share would be saved by a fourth copy in the layout.
- **The four calls of the thread archive stay outside the mirror,** and the navigation calls `runtime.threads/v1` for them (Q6). Not taken: the layout, and `workspace` after it, would keep a line for `runtime.threads/v1`, a second adapter with the adapter's guards and messages, and its own copy of `followStream`, and AC3.1 and AC4.7 would each need an exception. An archived Thread would also reach the tree by the watch's next update and not when the call resolves, as it does today.
- **One member that replaces the window's whole set of views,** as `createThreadViews.set` does (`L/src/client/api/thread-views.ts:17-26`). Not taken: every call of such a member undoes what another caller set, so it serves one caller. `view` with a count gives the same streams and lets the layout hand the watch to the chat inside one change.
- **`workspace` tells which Threads the window shows** (Q7). Not taken: it would tell a Thread tab by the chat's tab id or by the chat's tag, which is the knowledge D4 took out of the navigation with `onOpenThreadHook`.
- **Each element that draws a Thread holds a view while it is connected** (Q7). Not taken: it replaces the rule of the thread archive with another. A tab that is mounted and not selected would count as shown, and what is shown would follow how the shell mounts tabs.
- **A slot for the sidebar's tabs** (Q8), so that another bundle could add one. Not taken: the two tabs are two filters of one tree, no second filler exists, and the concept adds an extension point only for a filler that exists.
- **A copy of `threadSubtreeIds` in `workspace`.** Not taken: the mirror's store removes a deleted Thread's descendants with the same function, and a second copy can drift from it. The runtime's contracts module already holds shared code of this kind, the fence rules the chat and `openui` call (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/fences.ts`).
- **Every registrant keeps a structural copy of `chat.views/v1`,** as `threads` does. Rejected: the concept keeps a copy legal only where an import would close a cycle (X8), and for `files` and `default` it closes none. `terminal` keeps its copy until step 7(c) for exactly that reason ([section 10](#10-chat-points)). An importer that lists the contract under `optional` imports its types and declares the ref by id (D32), which copies no shape.
- **A deadline of `default` on a source's `read`,** 5,000 ms as for `list`. Rejected for D15: `mcp` bounds a read at 30,000 ms today, so a read that takes between 5 and 30 seconds and succeeds today would fail, a visible change the concept did not accept.
- **`site` left out of the REST answer,** removed by the transport from `GET /providers/kinds`. No public payload would change. Rejected for D21: the transport would name a field of the kind descriptor, which it only forwards today (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/host/routes/providers.ts:69-70`), and a REST client could not offer the link the desktop offers. The owner chose the optional field (U26).
- **Step 6(b) waits until the owner has told the known consumers** of the field. Not taken: the owner knows no consumer outside the repository that validates a kind with a closed schema (U26), and the notice in the host README is there from phase 0.

## Risks

| #   | Risk                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Mitigation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R1  | A drawing cannot live outside the chat (concept, A3): the terminal row, the terminal's result block or the `thread-spawn` block needs chat state an element cannot be given.                                                                                                                  | Phase 5 starts with a throwaway prototype of the terminal row and the result block behind elements. A drawing that cannot cross stays built in, the chat keeps its name for it, and the phase report and its Evolution entry in the feature doc list it ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| R2  | A transcript whose tool calls are drawn by other bundles' elements opens slower than one the chat draws itself.                                                                                                                                                                               | The measure and the budget of AC5.4: at most 10% slower for the transcript of 500 tool calls, taken before any built-in is removed. A drawing that misses it stays built in.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| R3  | The runtime's client part fails to activate in a window, and the six bundles that require `runtime.records/v1` are quarantined there ([Flows](#flows), 7).                                                                                                                                    | _Compared with today._ The records are in the layout's client part today. The same failure there leaves the window with no frame and no notice (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:1428`) and takes with it the seven bundles that require `layout.workspace/v1`. After phase 3 the frame and the notice remain, and the host part is untouched. _Likelihood._ The part calls no host method and awaits nothing while it activates ([section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime); T3.6), so no state of the host or of the network can fail it. What remains is an error in the part's own module, which the part's tests and the desktop end-to-end suite meet before a merge. _After activation,_ the three stated behaviors have one test each (T3.3–T3.5). |
| R4  | A consumer left on a removed contract id or slot name fails silently (`whenAvailable` gives nothing; an item in an unknown slot is not drawn).                                                                                                                                                | Steps 4(a), 4(c) and 4(e) are accepted by a search that finds no old name (AC4.4, AC4.9, AC4.12), and steps 4(a), 5(c) and 7(c) by a search that finds none of the four replaced ids (AC4.5, AC5.5, AC7.8); the `declared` test fails on a manifest line that names an id no bundle provides.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| R5  | The drop migration meets rows that a host from before stage 7 wrote.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | The migration counts the rows of the three tables before it drops them, in one transaction, and raises when one exists ([section 16](#16-postgres); T10.3). When it raises, nothing has changed, and the operator's path is in the host README.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| R6  | The bundle step makes CI more than twice as long, or a bundle test passes and fails on one commit.                                                                                                                                                                                            | Phase 1 merges alone. It records the job's time and moves the step to a second job above twice the time. It runs the job five times, and each test whose result differs between the runs is skipped with its own Linear issue, which the skip names ([section 2](#2-ci)). The fix of such a test, and of a test that fails because the product is wrong, is outside this spec (U28), so a skip can outlive phase 13; AC13.4 keeps each tied to an open issue.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| R7  | A bundle outside the repository uses one of the four replaced ids (U27), a read that leaves `runtime.lease/v1` (U23), a contract that leaves or an old slot name, or executes `core.open-settings/v1` with no input (U21).                                                                    | The release notes of each change name what left. A removed or replaced contract under `requires` quarantines the bundle with its reason, and the call of `core.open-settings/v1` rejects with `invalid_input`; the other cases are silent (concept, A1). A replaced id stops such a bundle where version 3 would have run it against a changed meaning ([Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| R8  | A consumer of the REST API outside the repository rejects the unknown key `site`.                                                                                                                                                                                                             | No reader in the repository rejects it ([section 13](#13-the-rest-and-socketio-api)). For the others the mitigation is notice: the compatibility rule and the announcement of the field are in the host README from phase 0, and the release notes of step 6(b) name the route and the field. A consumer that still rejects the key fails on the `openrouter` kind from the first host with step 6(b), and reverting the step does not repair it. The owner accepted this, knowing no consumer outside the repository that validates a kind with a closed schema (U26).                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| R9  | The `declared` test misses a contract id built at run time, or reached through a re-export or a namespace import.                                                                                                                                                                             | Review; the `imports` test covers the import half.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| R10 | The image does not build through the Nx target, in step 11(b) or after a later change to Nx, to a plugin or to a target.                                                                                                                                                                      | The lockfile pins Nx and its plugins, and CI runs the same targets from the same lockfile on every pull request (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:70`), so only the base image differs in the builder. Step 11(b) merges only with the image built from its commit and the host started from it (AC11.4). The step is one merged change that touches the `.Dockerfile`, its `lists` entry and two test cases, so it is reverted alone, and the image builds from its lists again. Not verified before the step: that the three plugins load in the builder's image, which has no Python. The owner chose the Nx target and reopened the `.Dockerfile`'s recorded reason for it (U25).                                                                                                   |
| R11 | The picker regresses in the move: a stored model no longer offered, a write in flight, a refused write.                                                                                                                                                                                       | Its 22 test cases move with it, keep their assertions and change only what they fake (T6.4). The rules that stay in `settings` are tested against the contract (T6.3), and `settings` computes the view with the functions the picker calls today, unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| R12 | A skill source that is slow to list costs a prompt that source's skills.                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Accepted (concept, G9); `default` logs the source that was left out. A slow MCP server does not cause it: `mcp` lists from its storage and its memory ([section 14](#14-skill-sources)). A read is bounded by its source, as today.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| R13 | The conversation column shows the chat's element where it shows the "select a Thread" text today, for the moment between a restored route and the mirror describing its Thread.                                                                                                               | T4.11 pins what the chat's element draws for a Thread the mirror does not describe yet; if it is not what the reader sees today, the chat draws that text itself until the Thread arrives.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| R14 | A desktop app older than the host loses bundles as the ranges rise: `layout` from step 4(a), then `account` and `workspace`; `settings` and `chat` from step 6(c); `files`, `default`, `threads` and `terminal` in step 5(b); and with each, every bundle that requires a contract of theirs. | [Section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric). The app names the bundles and the reason on its splash. The core's range does not rise, so the app always reaches that list. A newer app runs older bundles, so updating the app before the host avoids the loss, and the release notes of each change that raises a range say so. The two earlier raises of the platform accepted this risk (`docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:1426`), and the owner accepted it for these three platform minors (U24). Step 6(a) raises `claude-login` to `^1.7.0`, a range every app that draws a window already meets.                                                                                                                                             |
| R15 | A custom composition does not enable `account` or `workspace`, or requires `runtime/v1`.                                                                                                                                                                                                      | [Section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build). The release notes of steps 4(b) and 4(c) name the line to add, and the host README names the two lines beside its link to `composition.json`. A custom composition that requires `runtime/v1` does not start from step 7(c); the release notes of steps 7(a) and 7(c) and the README name `runtime/v2`. Without `workspace`, the bundles that depend on it are named as quarantined. Without `account`, nothing reports the missing menu; that part of the risk is accepted, as SR13 of the UI redesign accepts an item in a slot that nothing draws (concept, [Recorded decisions this work leaves alone](../features/bundle-isolation.md#recorded-decisions-this-work-leaves-alone)).                                            |
| R16 | After phase 10 the code is rolled back to a build from before it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | [Section 16](#16-postgres): that build's `migrate deploy` applies nothing, its startup check passes, and nothing since stage 7 reads the tables. AC10.5 runs the rollback before the phase merges. If it fails, the README and the release notes say that the rollback needs the backup restored.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| R17 | During a transition the old id does not keep its meaning, and a consumer that has not moved yet misbehaves between two merged changes.                                                                                                                                                        | Both ids are served from one store or one set of operations, and the tests that exist for the old id run unchanged until the change that removes it (AC4.5, AC5.5, AC7.1). Each transition lasts one step, of at most six merged changes (D30).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| R18 | A class of a file that moved to `workspace` or `account` reaches the window in another order than today, and a responsive variant loses to a plain utility of a later sheet.                                                                                                                  | Each of the seven sheets that scan the layout's client gains the line of the new bundle in the change that moves the files ([section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)), so each still emits the whole sorted set. The four cascade tests keep passing (`bundles/features/git/tests/styles-cascade.test.ts`, and those of `settings`, `mcp` and `screen`), and the desktop end-to-end suite runs on steps 4(b) and 4(c). No test compares two sheets class by class; that gap exists today (`docs/designs/sidebar-alignment.md:948`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| R19 | A client part imports a value from `<id>/contracts` of a bundle its manifest does not require. In a composition without that bundle the window's import map has no entry for the module, and the whole part does not load.                                                                    | D32 ([section 8](#8-slots)): a slot's name is checked through `import type`, which the build erases, and a contract under `optional` is reached by its id with types alone; the table there lists the four places this spec adds or moves. The search for an unchecked slot literal runs at steps 4(c), 6(c) and 7(c). No test finds a value import under `optional`; review holds the rule. Two such imports exist today and stay, in `git` and in `settings`, and the shipped composition enables every bundle they name. The failure itself was read from the import map's code and not run.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| R20 | The watch that tells the host which Threads a window shows changes its bundle twice, in phase 3 and in step 4(c). A window that holds no view of a Thread on screen lets the archiver move an agent thread to Archived 30 to 40 seconds after its turn ends, while the reader looks at it.    | Each move is inside one merged change: the layout's watch takes `view` in the change that moves the stream into the runtime, and the chat's watch appears in the change that deletes the layout's. T3.9 and T4.13 hold the rule on each side, and AC3.4 and AC4.13 find a leftover. Nothing is lost if it fails: an archived Thread is listed in the Archived tab and restored from there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

## Phases

Each merged change leaves the product building, its tests passing and the reader's behavior unchanged, except for the changes [Non-goals](#non-goals) lists. The phases merge to `main` in the order of D1 (U9), which is the order of this table. A phase with lettered steps merges step by step, and a step is one merged change unless the table gives another number. A code phase corrects the README and the `AGENTS.md` of the bundles it touches; no phase after 0 edits the body of `GROUNDING.md` (concept, [Phases](../features/bundle-isolation.md#phases)).

> Amended on 2026-11-18 by decision 34: the steps are built as commits on `feat/bundle-isolation`, and the branch merges to `main` once, at the end. See [Revisions](#revisions).

- **Release notes.** A merged change carries a section "Release notes" in its pull request when it removes or renames something another bundle or an API client could use, or when it asks something of an operator. The same text becomes that change's dated entry in the Evolution of [docs/features/bundle-isolation.md](../features/bundle-isolation.md). The repository has no changelog file, and this spec adds none. Besides the removed and renamed names, the notes hold what the table below lists.
- **Evolution entries.** Every merged change that completes a step, or a phase without steps, adds a dated entry to that Evolution, whether or not it carries release notes: phases 0 and 1 and step 2(c) have none and get an entry. The entry names the step or phase, links the pull request, and repeats the release notes when the change has them. A change inside a step that carries release notes adds its own entry as well. The phase report and AC2.5 rely on that entry.
- **What the release notes hold,** by change:

  | Change                                                                                                                          | Text the release notes hold                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
  | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | The two versioning deviations, in steps 2(a) and 7(c)                                                                           | The row of the table of deviations in [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions).                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
  | The four new majors: the change that adds the new id, and the change that removes the old one (steps 4(a), 5(c), 7(a) and 7(c)) | The row of the table of new majors in [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions): on the first, "`<new id>` is provided beside `<old id>`, which leaves in a later change of this step; move to the new id"; on the second, "`<old id>` is no longer provided", with what differs. |
  | Steps 7(a) and 7(c)                                                                                                             | "A custom composition (`DORIC_COMPOSITION`) that names `runtime/v1` under `required.contracts` names `runtime/v2` instead; from step 7(c) the host does not start with the old line." ([section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build))                                                      |
  | Each change that raises an `engines.doric`, in steps 4(a), 4(b), 4(c), 6(c), 5(a) and 5(b)                                      | "This change needs the desktop app of platform 1.8.0 (or 1.9.0, 1.10.0) or later. Update the desktop app before the host. An older app lists the bundles it cannot load on its start screen." ([section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric))                                       |
  | Step 6(a)                                                                                                                       | "`claude-login` needs the desktop app of platform 1.7.0 or later, as `layout`, `chat` and `settings` already do." ([section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric))                                                                                                                   |
  | Steps 4(b) and 4(c)                                                                                                             | "A custom composition (`DORIC_COMPOSITION`) adds `account` (or `workspace`) to `enabled`." With what is missing without the line ([section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build)).                                                                                                          |
  | Step 6(b)                                                                                                                       | The route `GET /providers/kinds` and the field `site` ([section 13](#13-the-rest-and-socketio-api)).                                                                                                                                                                                      |
  | Phase 10                                                                                                                        | "The migration `20261117000000_drop_legacy_runtime_tables` cannot be reversed. Stop the host and back up the database before `prisma migrate deploy`." With the operator's path ([section 16](#16-postgres)).                                                                             |

- **Phase 10 cannot be undone.** It needs only phase 1, so that the rewritten proof of stage 7 runs in CI before the migration merges; D1 places it last among the code phases so that an operator who follows `main` meets it once the rest is in.
- **A step that fails its acceptance** does not merge. Nothing later depends on a partly merged phase, except as the column "Needs" says.
- **Undoing a change that removes an id.** Four changes remove a contract id that a transition kept: the sixth of step 4(a), step 4(e), the sixth of step 5(c) and step 7(c). Each is one merged change, and reverting it is the rollback. The provider then serves the old id again beside the new one; the consumers moved in earlier changes and are not touched; and none of the four writes persisted data or changes a payload of the REST and Socket.IO API, so nothing outside the code is restored. A revert of step 7(c) also brings back the nine lease reads, `runtime.terminals/v1` and the package line from `runtime` to `terminal`, and so turns the terminal's imports back into copies in the same revert. No platform version moves in any of the four, so the desktop app is not involved.

## Files to change

By phase, in the order of the phase numbers, with the section that designs each change. "Tests" names only new test files; the tests that move, are rewritten or are deleted are in [Test plan](#test-plan).

### Phase 0

- New: `tests/boundaries.test.mjs`, `tests/support/workspace.mjs`, `tests/support/boundaries.mjs`, `tests/boundary-exceptions.json` ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)).
- `tests/lint.test.mjs`: lines `:15` and `:156-245` leave for the two new files, and the file imports `root`. `package.json:8`: the script `lint:test` names both test files and runs them one after the other. `biome.json`: the second entry of `overrides`, `noUndeclaredDependencies` for `bundles/**`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:47`: the step's name.
- The eleven manifests of the table of 22 lines.

## Acceptance criteria

**Who checks what, and when.**

- Every merged change passes the commands of [Validation](#validation) before it merges.
- A step, or a phase without steps, is accepted at its last merged change: its criteria below hold there, and no entry of the violations file names it in `until`. A phase is accepted when its last step is.
- **The phase report.** Some criteria ask for a measurement or a list. The author of the last change of the step writes it into a section "Phase report" of that change's pull request. The reviewer of the pull request checks the section against the criteria before the merge. The change's Evolution entry in `docs/features/bundle-isolation.md` links the pull request, so the report is found from the repository. The criteria that ask for a report are AC0.1, AC1.1, AC1.2, AC1.3, AC5.4, AC10.5, AC13.2, AC13.3, AC13.4 and AC13.5; R1 asks for one as well.
- The criteria are in the order of the phase numbers; D1 gives the merge order.

### Phase 0

- AC0.1: The six boundary tests pass under `npm run lint:test` (T0.1, T0.2). The phase report shows each of `imports`, `core`, `lists` and `cycles` failing once with one of its entries removed from the violations file, and each of `declared` and `dependencies`, which have no entry, failing once with one of the lines phase 0 added to a manifest or removed from a `package.json` put back as it was. It lists every such line, and every difference from the tables of [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file). `tests/lint.test.mjs` declares no helper that `tests/support/workspace.mjs` exports.
- AC0.2: The 22 lines exist, each under `optional`; `declared` and `dependencies` have no entry, and `cycles` has the five of [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file). Each of the 14 manifests of [Files to change](#phase-0) is one patch above its version at `ebbf8f94`, and no other manifest's version changes.
- AC0.3: `npm run lint` passes with `noUndeclaredDependencies` on, `npm ci` passes with the committed `package-lock.json`, and `npx nx sync:check` passes.
- AC0.4: No file under a bundle's `src/`, `tests/` or `stories/` changes, except the five test files of [Files to change](#phase-0) that assert a bundle's version, and in them only the lines listed there. A bundle's `README.md` is in none of the three directories; the five READMEs of [Files to change](#phase-0) change in the lines listed there and in no other, and no other README or `AGENTS.md` of a bundle changes. `GROUNDING.md` carries the notice of [Docs to update](#docs-to-update) and has no other change, and `git grep -n "GROUNDING.md" -- AGENTS.md docs/architecture/bundles/migration.md docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md docs/architecture/bundles.md` finds no line that asks for an update of the file. `git diff --name-only ebbf8f94 -- 'bundles/*/*/src/*' 'bundles/*/*/tests/*' 'bundles/*/*/stories/*'` lists only the five test files.
- AC0.5: The REST reference of the host README holds the compatibility rule and the announcement of `site`.

### Phase 1

- AC1.1: The bundle step runs on every pull request, in the job `main` or, if the time rule moved it, in a job `bundles` that the phase report shows among the required status checks of the branch `main` ([section 2](#2-ci)).
- AC1.2: The phase report gives the job's time before and after and the result of each of the five runs, and the step is in its own job if the time more than doubled.
- AC1.3: No bundle test is skipped except through one of the three unset gate variables, or with a `skip` whose reason starts with the id of its Linear issue. The phase report lists each such skip with its issue.

### Phase 2

- **Step 2(a).** AC2.1: The string `'/settings'` is in no file of `bundles/foundation/core/src`. AC2.2: The four cases of the table of [section 3](#3-core-and-kernel) hold (T2.1).
- **Step 2(b).** AC2.3: After each of the six changes T2.2 passes for the contracts changed so far; after the sixth the core's manifest has no `desktopScope`, and the `core` test has no scope-key entry.
- **Step 2(c).** AC2.4: No file under `app/backend/doric-host/src` imports a bundle's package, the constant is 8,039,084, and T2.3 passes. AC2.5: The Evolution entry of the step names what the core still names by OD17: the command id `core.open-settings/v1`, the window keys `'settings'` and `'workspace'`, the two sets of window sizes and the key `doric.selection`.

### Phase 3

- AC3.1: The layout's client part calls no method of `runtime/v1` or `runtime.threads/v1`.
- AC3.2: The tests of the ten consumers of `layout.workspace/v1` pass unchanged.
- AC3.3: T3.1 to T3.9 pass, and the desktop end-to-end suite passes with no change to an assertion about what a screen shows.
- AC3.4: `git grep -nwE "viewThread|archiveThread|restoreThread|setThreadPinned" -- bundles/desktop/layout/src bundles/desktop/layout/README.md` finds no call of `runtime.threads/v1`: every line it finds names a member of `runtime.records/v1` or an action of the layout's own.

### Phase 4

- **Every step.** AC4.1: Every test the step moves passes in its new bundle, changed only as [section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file) allows. AC4.2: The desktop end-to-end suite passes with no change to an assertion about what a screen shows; among its cases, a window restarted on a Thread shows that Thread, its Project tab and its viewer tabs (`app/desktop/doric-e2e/tests/shell.test.ts`).
- **Step 4(a).** AC4.3: `git grep -nwE "chat-footer-height|TabIcon" -- bundles/desktop/layout/src` finds nothing, and `git grep -niE "monaco|xterm" -- bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/styles.css bundles/desktop/layout/package.json` finds nothing. The option `-w` keeps `CloseTabIcon` and `NewTabIcon` out (`L/src/client/components/organisms/project-tabs.tsx:33,53`). The search for the two libraries reads the sheet and the package and not the sources, because the sidebar's search shortcut tests an event's target against the classes `.monaco-editor` and `.xterm` (`L/src/client/components/organisms/project-sidebar.tsx:240`); they are the class names of two libraries and no bundle's, and the line moves to `workspace` unchanged in step 4(c). AC4.4: `git grep -nE "layout\.(header|footer|main\.bottom|overlay|panel(\.footer)?)([^/A-Za-z0-9_-]|$)" -- bundles app` finds nothing. AC4.5: T4.1 to T4.6 pass. From the step's second change to its fifth, the layout's tests of `layout.tabs/v1` and `layout.panel/v2` pass with no change to an assertion; after its sixth, `git grep -nE "layout\.tabs/v1|layout\.panel/v2|LayoutTabsV1|LayoutPanelV2" -- bundles app` finds nothing. From its seventh, the layout registers `/projects` and `/projects/:projectId` with `parent: '/'`, and the empty state is drawn by an element in the outlet (T4.2).
- **Step 4(b).** AC4.6: `git grep -n "layout\.account\.status" -- bundles app` finds nothing, no file of `L/src` names `doric.about/v1`, and T4.7 passes. Each of the seven sheets of [section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file) has the `@source` line of `account`, and the four `styles-cascade` tests pass.
- **Step 4(c).** AC4.7: The layout's manifest requires no contract that starts with `runtime`, provides `layout.tabs/v2`, `layout.panel/v3`, `layout.stack/v2` and `layout.conversation/v1` and nothing else, and has the new `description`. AC4.8: No file of `L/src` imports `runtime/contracts`, and no file of `bundles/desktop/workspace/src/contracts` does but `transition.ts`, which holds the three old contracts until step 4(e); no file of `settings`, `files` or `git` imports `LayoutWorkspaceV1`, `layoutWorkspaceMethods` or `LayoutDashboardV1` from `layout/contracts`; `L/package.json` lists neither `resources` nor `runtime`; and `git grep -nE "chat-thread-panel|chat-thread-tab-icon|core\.open-settings" -- bundles/desktop/layout/src` finds nothing. AC4.9: The search of [section 8](#8-slots) for the nine old slot names finds nothing, its search for an unchecked slot literal leaves unchecked the terminal's line and the four lines that name `settings.sections` and no other, each of the seven sheets has the `@source` line of `workspace`, and the four `styles-cascade` tests pass. AC4.10: "Open in tab" opens the chat's tab; in a composition without `chat` it opens nothing and reports nothing. AC4.13: `git grep -n "thread-views" -- bundles/desktop/layout bundles/desktop/workspace` finds nothing. T4.8 to T4.13 pass.
- **Step 4(d).** AC4.11: After the tenth change `git grep -nE "layout\.(workspace|sidebar|dashboard)/v1" -- bundles app packages ':!bundles/desktop/workspace'` finds nothing.
- **Step 4(e).** AC4.12: `git grep -nE "layout\.(workspace|sidebar|dashboard)/v1" -- bundles app packages` finds nothing, the manifest of `workspace` is at 1.0.0, `bundles/desktop/workspace/src/contracts/transition.ts` does not exist, and no file of `bundles/desktop/workspace/src/contracts` imports `runtime/contracts`.

### Phase 5

- **Step 5(a).** AC5.1: T5.1 and T5.2 pass with made-up registrants and no change to a file of `chat`. T5.3 and T5.4 pass.
- **Step 5(b).** AC5.2: After the fourth change the files the table of [section 10](#10-chat-points) lists are gone from the chat. AC5.3: Each moved drawing's tests pass in its owner, in the change that moves it, and each mounts the owner's element alone ([Test plan](#test-plan)). AC5.4: `transcript-cost.test.ts` reports, for the committed transcript of 500 tool calls, the median over ten runs of the time from selecting the Thread to its last row drawn, with the built-in drawings and with the registered elements; the second is at most 1.10 times the first, or the drawing responsible stays built in and the phase report says so.
- **Step 5(c).** AC5.5: T5.5 passes; from the step's first change to its fifth, the chat's tests of `chat.hooks/v1` pass with no change to an assertion; after its sixth, `git grep -nE "chat\.hooks/v1|ChatHooksV1" -- bundles app` finds nothing; and `git grep -nE "'(terminal|edit|write|view-image|thread-spawn|load_skill)'" -- bundles/desktop/chat/src ':!bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/domain/queue.ts' ':!bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/domain/projector-input.ts' ':!bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/components/molecules/prompt-author.tsx'` finds nothing but the name of a drawing that stayed built in by AC5.4. The three files left out name `terminal` as the runtime's input source kind and not as a tool: the type and the list of the kinds a stored prompt has (`C/domain/queue.ts:14`, `C/domain/projector-input.ts:30`), and the name and the icon of a queued prompt's author (`C/domain/queue.ts:78`, `C/components/molecules/prompt-author.tsx:15`). They stay: the chat draws a prompt's author for every kind the runtime's record has, and the input point of [section 10](#10-chat-points) replaces the result block and not the author.

## Test plan

The tests follow `.agents/skills/behavioral-testing`: each exercises a contract or another documented boundary, asserts the smallest observation that proves the behavior, and uses a double only for another bundle, the host or the clock. A new extension point is tested with a made-up registrant, never with the real one. The column "Step" names the step whose merged change adds the test; a test passes from that change on. The repository's conventions hold: Node's test runner, files under each bundle's `tests/`, client tests under `tests/client/`.

| T0.1  | 0     | Each of the six finders, given a small in-memory set of files and manifests, reports a planted violation and nothing for a clean set. The `imports` finder reports an import of `<package>/src/…` as it does today, and a dynamic `import()` like a static one; `<id>/package.json` of another bundle passes in a file under `tests/` and is found in a file under `src/` or `stories/`. `sourceFiles`, called on a Git repository the test creates in a temporary directory, returns a tracked file and an untracked one and leaves out an ignored file, a file under `dist` and a tracked file that was deleted from the disk. `declared`: each form of symbol [section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file) lists maps to its id; a type-only import is no use; the core is skipped for a symbol of `bundle/contracts`. ### Validation

Each merged change runs, before it merges: `npm ci`, which fails when `package-lock.json` disagrees with a `package.json`; `npx nx sync:check`, which fails when a `tsconfig.json` lacks or keeps a reference `nx sync` would write or remove; `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint:test`, which runs the boundary tests from phase 0 on, `npx nx run-many -t build typecheck test`, and `npx nx test doric-host` with `DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL` set. The changes of phases 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 also run the desktop end-to-end suite (`app/desktop/doric-e2e`): each changes a client part or a contract a window calls. Step 11(b) also builds the image.

## Docs to update

- **In each code phase:** the README and the `AGENTS.md` of every bundle the phase touches, and the READMEs and `AGENTS.md` files of `workspace` and `account` when they are created. In phase 0 these are the five READMEs of [Files to change](#phase-0).
- **`app/backend/doric-host/README.md`:** the compatibility rule of the REST reference and the announcement of `site` (phase 0); the lines a custom composition adds, `account` and `workspace`, beside the link to `composition.json` (steps 4(b) and 4(c)), and `runtime/v2` under `required.contracts` (step 7(a)); the kind descriptor (step 6(b)); "Legacy rows at the drop migration" (phase 10); how the image builds the bundles (step 11(b)).
- **`docs/features/bundle-isolation.md`:** in each code phase, its status, its "Current behavior and contracts" and one Evolution entry.
- **`GROUNDING.md`, the root `AGENTS.md`, `docs/architecture/bundles/migration.md`, `docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md` and `docs/architecture/bundles.md`:** the notice and the removed rules and clauses (phase 0). The root `AGENTS.md` also gains, in phase 0, the one line on `npm run lint:test` of [Files to change](#phase-0), an exception to the developer guidance that phase 12 writes.
- **The notice of `GROUNDING.md`,** word for word. It names this spec and HC-002 and says that the file is frozen and where it is wrong, as the documentation plan asks ([Steps of phase 12](bundle-isolation-docs.md#steps-of-phase-12-and-when-it-runs)). It keeps the gates in force, because the hard constraints stay in this file until phase 12 moves them (the plan's G17 and R20).

  ```md
  > **Frozen.** This file is not updated any more. Phase 0 of the bundle
  > isolation work ([docs/designs/bundle-isolation.md](docs/designs/bundle-isolation.md))
  > froze it, and the last step of that work's documentation phase reduces it to
  > a pointer.
  >
  > It describes the system as it was when it was frozen, and it is wrong
  > wherever a later change altered the system. Where this file and the
  > repository disagree, the repository is right: HC-002, "Current Repository
  > State Is Authoritative", below.
  >
  > The Authority order, the Hard Constraints and the Convention Parameters of
  > this file stay in force until they move to the root `AGENTS.md`. Do not edit
  > this file to record a change. The documents that replace it are planned in
  > [docs/designs/bundle-isolation-docs.md](docs/designs/bundle-isolation-docs.md).
  ```

- **`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md`:** the addition of phase 12 that [Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions) states. Until phase 12 each versioning deviation is in the release notes of its change, and that of `core.open-settings/v1` also in the core's README and the two doc comments ([section 3](#3-core-and-kernel)).
- **`docs/features/bundle-isolation.md`:** one Evolution entry per merged change that completes a step or a phase, and per merged change that carries release notes ([Phases](#phases)); in phase 13 its status, "Current behavior and contracts" and the link to this spec's final version.
- **Everything else** is phase 12: the architecture documents, the 36 feature docs, the `AGENTS.md` of every bundle and the retirement of `GROUNDING.md`, as the [documentation plan](bundle-isolation-docs.md) designs them. Among them is the correction of `docs/architecture/bundles/contracts.md:80` (concept, X8) and the new section of `docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md`.
- Feature docs this spec touches, each brought in line in phase 12: [docs/features/bundle-isolation.md](../features/bundle-isolation.md); [docs/features/ui-redesign.md](../features/ui-redesign.md) and [docs/features/per-thread-model.md](../features/per-thread-model.md), whose slot names and whose picker's owner change; [docs/features/cwd-tool.md](../features/cwd-tool.md), whose repository hint moves to another file; [docs/features/thread-archive.md](../features/thread-archive.md), whose "Current behavior and contracts" places the sidebar's tabs and the reporting of open Threads in the layout (`:40-46`), where this spec has `workspace`, the chat and `runtime.records/v1`; and [docs/features/error-messages.md](../features/error-messages.md), whose "Current behavior and contracts" places five things this spec moves (`:37-41`): the `claude-login` toasts go to `claude-login`, `EditToolItem` to `files`, the execution picker's notice to the chat, `WorkspaceErrorNotice` to `workspace` and `ElementHost` to the platform, and the place of the quarantine notice is now given by the slots of [section 5](#5-the-shell-layout).

## Questions

None is open. Version 7 asked Q6, Q7 and Q8, each about code that pull request #59 added to `main` after version 6 was written, and none reopened a decision the owner made. The owner's decision 31 (2026-11-18) answered each as this spec proposed, and the answers are U29–U31. The spec was already written with the proposal of each, which is what D3, D4 and D28 give when they are applied to the new code (D33), so the answers change no line of the design. The owner's approval of version 6 stands for the rest of the design.

Version 1 asked Q1 and Q2, which the owner's decision 27 answered (U22, U23). Version 3 asked three, each about something already decided, and the owner's decision 29 answered each as this spec proposed. Version 4 asks none: the owner's decision 30 answered the two Major findings of its review directly (U27, U28). Version 5 asks none: each finding of the fourth review was settled from the code, and where a fix chose between designs the choice is D31 or is in [Alternatives considered](#alternatives-considered). Version 6 asks none: each finding of the fifth review was settled from the code, and the one range it raises, of `claude-login`, reaches no desktop app that U24 does not already cover ([section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric)).

| Question                                                                                                                                                                                       | Answer                                                                                                                                                                      | Where                                                                                                      |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Q3. An older desktop app loses bundles as three platform minors raise `engines.doric`.                                                                                                         | Accepted as designed, with the release note "update the desktop app before the host" on each change that raises a range.                                                    | U24; R14, [section 18](#18-platform-version-and-enginesdoric)                                              |
| Q4. The image builds through the Nx target, against the reason its `.Dockerfile` records.                                                                                                      | As designed. The owner reopened the recorded reason.                                                                                                                        | U25, U8; D25, R10, [section 17](#17-bundle-lists-and-the-build)                                            |
| Q5. A consumer of the REST API outside the repository may reject the unknown key `site`.                                                                                                       | As designed, with the compatibility rule and the announcement in the host README from phase 0. The owner knows no such consumer that validates a kind with a closed schema. | U26; D21, R8, [section 13](#13-the-rest-and-socketio-api)                                                  |
| Q6. The thread archive added four calls of `runtime.threads/v1` to the layout: `archiveThread`, `restoreThread`, `setThreadPinned` and `viewThread`. Are they members of `runtime.records/v1`? | As designed: yes, as three writes and `view`.                                                                                                                               | U29; D33, [section 4](#4-the-records-mirror-runtime), T3.2, T3.9, AC3.4                                    |
| Q7. Which bundle tells the host which Threads the window shows, once the navigation has left the layout?                                                                                       | As designed: the layout until step 4(c), the chat from then.                                                                                                                | U30; D33, [section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file), [section 10](#10-chat-points), T4.13, AC4.13, R20 |
| Q8. Where are the sidebar's Chat and Archived tabs after the split?                                                                                                                            | As designed: in `workspace`, inside `workspace-sidebar`, with the Thread tree. No slot and no shell contract.                                                               | U31; D33, [section 9](#9-the-layout-split-file-by-file)                                                    |

The options the owner did not choose are in [Alternatives considered](#alternatives-considered).

**The options of Q6 to Q8.** The owner chose (a) of each. The others are listed with what each would have changed.

- **Q6.**
  - _(a) In the mirror,_ as written. D3 moves the adapter whole, and AC3.1 has the layout call no method of `runtime.threads/v1` from phase 3. The contract is new in phase 3, so the four members are part of its first version, 1.0.0.
  - _(b) Outside the mirror._ The navigation keeps a line for `runtime.threads/v1@^1.8.0` and its own adapter for the four calls. The mirror keeps nine writes. AC3.1 and AC4.7 gain an exception, and `workspace` requires a host contract of the runtime beside the mirror.
  - _Chosen: (a)._ With (b), section 4 would lose the four members and `threadSubtreeIds` would stay, section 9 would send `api/thread-views.ts` to `workspace`, and T3.9 would move there.
- **Q7.**
  - _(a) The chat, from step 4(c),_ as written. It registers the Thread route and opens the Thread tabs, so it tells both places by what it owns.
  - _(b) `workspace`._ It has the route and the selection. It would tell a Thread tab by the chat's tab id, `thread:<id>`, which it does not own.
  - _(c) Both._ `workspace` holds a view of the route's Thread and the chat a view of its selected tab's Thread. No bundle names another's id, and the mirror's count makes the overlap harmless. There are two watches where there is one.
  - _(d) The elements._ Each element that draws a Thread holds a view while it is connected. It changes the rule of the thread archive.
  - _Chosen: (a)._ With (b) or (c), section 10 would lose or halve its watch, `workspace` would gain one, and T4.13 would move or split; section 4 would not change.
- **Q8.**
  - _(a) In `workspace`,_ as written: the tabs, the Archived list and the archive rules move with the Thread tree, and the open tab is state of the sidebar's component.
  - _(b) A slot of `workspace` for sidebar tabs._ It lets another bundle add a tab. Nothing would fill it but `workspace` itself.
  - _(c) In the shell._ The shell would read `archivedAt` and so the runtime's records, which step 4(c) takes out of it.
  - _Chosen: (a)._ With (b), section 8 would gain a slot and its props type, and section 6 a filler.

- Implementation findings of phase 3 (2026-11-18). Recorded during the implementation on the branch `feat/bundle-isolation`, commit `c01705c8`. The version stays 7. The design of the code does not change; each finding below names the body passage it supersedes, or says it fills a gap with no body passage; the body text is left as accepted. MR1–MR6 are the mirror rules, which the coordinator decided during the implementation under the owner's instruction (2026-11-18) to implement without stopping; they are not the risks R1–R20 nor the model rules M1–M11.
  - **OD36, `threadSubtreeIds`.** The layout keeps its own `threadSubtreeIds` until step 4(c), because of D32. This deviates from Files to change for phase 3 (`:1993`), which has the layout use the one in `runtime/contracts`.
  - **MR1, a failed read.** A failed load's `error` is the `ClientError` it rejected with; a rejection that is not a `ClientError` is first converted by `hostFailure`. A failed watch stores a `project_stream_failed` error. The schema is loose. Fills a gap; no body passage.
  - **MR2, a load in flight.** Thread writes, Thread notices, snapshots and Project deletion drop that Project's Threads load in flight. Project rename, color and terminate, and the `project-updated` notice, do not. Fills a gap; no body passage.

  - **MR4, `reload`.** It always loads. Fills a gap; no body passage.
  - **MR5, the requested lists.** The layout keeps a `requested` set and reads only the lists it requested. Fills a gap; no body passage.
  - **MR6, the selection.** It is repaired as before. A Thread opened by navigation that is missing from a described list reloads that list; there is no overlay. Fills a gap; no body passage.
  - **Thread writes and `thread-updated`.** They do not describe a Project that was never described. Fills a gap; no body passage.
  - **`createRecordsFake`'s return type.** The spec's `ServiceDefinition<'runtime.records/v1', typeof runtimeRecordsMethods>` does not compile. It is `ServiceDefinition<'runtime.records/v1', ImplementationOf<typeof RuntimeRecordsV1>>`. A shared helper, `runtimeRecordsService(records)`, lives in `src/contracts/records.ts`.

  - **OD35, stale tests.** A test made stale by an intended product change is fixed, not skipped (see the feature doc's OD35).

CB-7: twenty-two lines and eight in [Persisted data](#persisted-data). CB-8: `lint:test` runs its two files with `--test-concurrency=1`; of the three fixes this is the one that also covers a fixture that exists while it is read. RD-N1: the rule for a bundle with a client part alone now puts what only tests and stories import under `devDependencies`. RD-N2: `"parallel": false` on the `typecheck` of `default` and `claude-login`. RD-N3: which of the six moved files import `desktop/reads`, `zustand/vanilla` and `runtime/contracts`, and that the runtime's part imports its own contracts by a relative path. RD-N4: the `decision` of every entry phase 0 writes. RD-N5: T0.1 tests `sourceFiles` on a repository it creates, and the `biome.json` entry is written out. RD-N6: the new `tsconfig.client.json` of `settings`, line by line.
  - **Found while checking, in neither review.** (1) `settings.sections` is a tenth existing slot, filled by `git`; it gains its constant in step 6(c). (2) Version 5 did not say where the three old navigation contracts are declared between steps 4(c) and 4(e), and three bundles import them by name from `layout/contracts`: [section 6](#6-workspace-navigation-workspace) names the file and the three import changes, and AC4.8 and AC4.12 follow. (3) `settings` keeps a fake of `claude.login/v1` in its test and story support and a test that reads the sources for the two sign-in toast ids; step 6(a) removes them. (4) `settings` loses its line for `layout` in step 6(c), with its last import of `layout/contracts`. (5) A value import of another bundle's contracts makes that bundle necessary for the part to load. D32 states the rule for a contract under `optional`, and four imports of version 5 follow it: `settings` for the navigation contracts, `files` and `default` and later `terminal` for `chat.views/v1`, and the chat for `settings.models/v1` (R19).
- Version 5 (2026-11-17). After the fourth review of this spec, a Readiness review (10 Major, 6 Nit) and a Codebase review against commit `a868b5d0` (9 Major, 9 Nit), with no Blocker. It checks `devDependencies` too. Its first run finds 14 lines, which phase 0 removes; none is in use. The files it reads come from Git and not from the disk, so a built tree and a fresh checkout agree.
  - **RD-M4, CB7, CB8 and CB9, FIX.** Each acceptance search was run against the tree and corrected. The slot search matches a name however it is quoted and finds 56 files, which [section 8](#8-slots) lists; AC4.3 no longer trips on `CloseTabIcon` or on the sidebar's shortcut guard; AC5.5 leaves out the three files that name the runtime's source kind `terminal`; AC7.2 and AC7.8 can pass, because steps 7(a) to 7(c) now list the end-to-end host, the transport's comments and README and the runtime's; AC7.7 uses `-w` and names the two types CB5 found; AC10.1 reads the code and not the frozen records.
  - **RD-M5, FIX.** Phase 0 declares `claude` in the core's `devDependencies`; the core's test keeps its four provider imports until step 6(b), as the `imports` entries say.
  - **RD-M6, FIX.** No client part reports through `log/v1`: a slot owner uses `console.error`, as the layout does, and a refused registration is recorded by the platform. The manifests of `workspace` and `account` are written in full, with ranges, and `layout.conversation/v1` is under `requires` of `workspace` from step 4(c) to step 4(e). Contracts and versions states how a line is written; an `optional` line has no range.
  - **RD-M7, FIX.** From the seventh change of step 4(a) the layout registers the two Project routes as children of `/` and draws the empty state as an element in the outlet; step 4(c) moves both to `workspace` ([section 5](#5-the-shell-layout)).
  - **RD-M8, FIX.** The desktop end-to-end suite also runs on the changes of phases 3 and 7.
  - **RD-M9, FIX.** The `declared` rule says how a symbol maps to an id, and the `lists` rule gives its two patterns; both were run against the cases the review names ([section 1](#1-boundary-tests-and-the-violations-file)).
  - **RD-M10, FIX.** T3.7, T3.8 and T8.3 are new; T0.1, T4.2, T4.9, T5.1, T5.2, T6.3, T8.1 and T9.1 gain the missing cases; and AC3.3, AC8.1 and AC9.1 cite the four tests no criterion cited.
